## VI Conclusion / 第六章 终章

===Original===
Once we had left her side and turned our boat’s nose towards the land, the yacht lay behind us, a black mass nearly absorbed in the general shadow. Not a light showed itself, and everything was as still as the grave; the only noise to be heard was the steady dip, dip of the oars in the smooth water and now and then the chirp of the rowlocks. For nearly half an hour we pulled on, pausing at intervals to listen, but nothing of an alarming nature met our ears. The island was every moment growing larger, the beach more plain to the eye, and the hill more clearly defined. As soon as the boat grounded we sprang out and, leaving one hand to look after her, made our way ashore. It was a strange experience that landing on a strange beach on such an errand and at such an hour, but we were all too much taken up with the work which lay before us to think of that. Having left the water’s edge we came to a standstill beneath a group of palms and discussed the situation. As the command of the expedition had fallen upon me I decided upon the following course of action: To begin with, I would leave the party behind me and set out by myself to ascertain the whereabouts of the hut. Having discovered this I would return, and we would thereupon make our way inland and endeavour to capture it. I explained the idea in as few words as possible to my followers, and then, bidding them wait for me where they were, at the same time warning them against letting their presence be discovered, I set off up the hill in the direction I knew the plateau to lie. The undergrowth was very thick and the ground rocky; for this reason it was nearly twenty minutes before I reached the top of the hill. Then down the other side I crept, picking my way carefully, and taking infinite precautions that no noise should serve to warn our foes of my coming.
At last I reached the plateau and looked about me. A small perpendicular cliff, some sixty feet in height, was before me, so throwing myself down upon my stomach, I wriggled my way to its edge. When I got there I looked over and discovered three well-built huts on a little plateau at the cliff’s base. At the same moment a roar of laughter greeted my ears from the building on the left. It was followed by the voice of a man singing to the accompaniment of a banjo. Under cover of his music I rose to my feet and crept back through the bushes, by the track along which I had come. I knew enough to distribute my forces now.
Having reached my friends again I informed them of what I had seen, and we then arranged the mode of attack as follows: The mate of the yacht, with two of the hands, would pass round the hill to the left of the plateau, Wetherell and another couple of men would take the right side, while Beckenham and myself crept down from the back. Not a sound was to be made or a shot fired until I blew my whistle. Then, with one last word of caution, we started on our climb.
By this time the clouds had cleared off the sky and the stars shone brightly. Now and again a bird would give a drowsy “caw” as we disturbed him, or a wild pig would jump up with a grunt and go trotting off into the undergrowth, but beyond these things all was very still. Once more I arrived at the small precipice behind the huts, and, having done so, sat down for a few moments to give the other parties time to take up their positions. Then, signing to Beckenham to accompany me, I followed the trend of the precipice along till I discovered a place where we might descend in safety. In less than a minute we were on the plateau below, creeping towards the centre hut. Still our approach was undetected. Bidding Beckenham in a whisper wait for me, I crept cautiously round to the front, keeping as much as possible in the shadow. As soon as I had found the door, I tiptoed towards it and prepared to force my way inside, but I had an adventure in store for me which I had not anticipated.
Seated in the doorway, almost hidden in the shadow, was the figure of a man. He must have been asleep, for he did not become aware of my presence until I was within a foot of him. Then he sprang to his feet and was about to give the alarm. Before he could do so, however, I was upon him. A desperate hand-to-hand struggle followed, in which I fought solely for his throat. This once obtained I tightened my fingers upon it and squeezed until he fell back unconscious. It was like a horrible nightmare, that combat without noise in the dark entry of the hut, and I was more than thankful that it ended so satisfactorily for me. As soon as I had disentangled myself, I rose to my feet and proceeded across his body into the hut itself. A swing door led from the porch, and this I pushed open.
===Chinese===
我们一离开它的船舷，把小艇的艇头转向陆地，游艇便落在了身后，成了一团黑影，几乎融进四周的夜色里。船上不见一点灯火，四下静得如同坟墓；听得见的，只有船桨在平滑水面上一下、一下沉稳的入水声，间或还有桨架发出的吱呀轻响。我们划了将近半个钟头，途中时时停下来倾听，但没有任何令人惊心的动静传进耳中。那座岛每一刻都在变大，海滩在眼中愈见分明，山冈的轮廓也愈见清晰。小艇一搁浅，我们便跃身而下，留一名水手看船，其余人上岸。在这样一个时辰、为着这样一桩差事，登上一片陌生的海滩，实在是奇特的经历，可我们全都一心惦着面前的大事，无暇及此。离开水边之后，我们在一丛棕榈树下停住脚，商议眼下的局面。既然远征的指挥之责落在了我身上，我便定下如下的行动步骤：首先，我把队伍留在身后，独自先行，去查明那些小屋的所在；探明之后我再回来，众人随即深入内陆，设法将它拿下。我用尽可能简短的话向部下交代了这个主意，随后吩咐他们在原地等我，同时告诫他们千万不可让行藏败露，自己便朝着心中所知高地的方向，动身登山。灌木丛极密，地面多石，因此过了将近二十分钟我才登上山顶。接着我又从另一侧蹑足而下，一路小心择路，百般谨慎，唯恐弄出半点声响，向敌人通报我的到来。
终于到了高地，我环顾四周。面前是一座陡直的小崖，高约六十英尺，于是我伏下身子，匍匐着挪到崖边。到了那里我探头下望，只见崖脚一小片平地上立着三座结结实实的小屋。就在这时，左首那座屋里爆出一阵大笑，送进我的耳朵。紧接着传来一个男人伴着班卓琴的歌声。借着他的琴声掩护，我站起身，顺着来时的小径，从灌木丛中退了回去。此刻该怎样部署人手，我心里已经有数了。
回到同伴们身边后，我把所见告知了他们，随后我们这样定下进攻的方案：游艇大副带两名水手，绕过山冈迂回至高地左侧；韦瑟雷尔和另外两人取右侧；我与贝肯汉姆则从背后摸下去。在我吹响哨子之前，不许出一点声，不许开一枪。于是，又叮嘱了最后一句要小心的话，我们便动身攀登。
这时云已散尽，星光灿烂。我们时不时惊起一只鸟，它便睡意沉沉地“呱”一声；有时一头野猪咕噜一声跳起来，小跑着钻进灌木丛去，但除此之外万籁俱寂。我再一次来到小屋后面那处小崖，坐下歇了几分钟，好让另外几路人马有时间各就各位。然后我示意贝肯汉姆随我来，沿着崖壁的走向一路探看，直到找着一处可以安全下去的地方。不到一分钟，我们已落到下方的高地上，朝居中那座小屋潜行过去。我们的逼近仍未被察觉。我低声吩咐贝肯汉姆等我，自己小心翼翼地绕到屋前，尽量贴着暗影。一找到屋门，我便踮起脚尖凑过去，准备强行破门而入，不料一桩始料未及的变故正等着我。
门道里坐着一个人影，几乎完全隐没在暗影中。他想必是睡着了，直到我离他不足一英尺，才察觉到我的存在。他霍地跳起身，正要示警，却被我先下手为强扑了上去。接着是一场殊死的肉搏，我一心只图他的喉咙。一旦得手，我便收紧手指死死掐住，直掐得他仰面昏死过去。在小屋幽暗的门道里进行这样一场无声的搏斗，恍如一场可怕的噩梦；它能这样称心如意地收场，我真是谢天谢地。一挣脱出身，我便站起身来，跨过他的躯体走进屋去。门廊里有一扇弹簧门，我把它推开了。

===Original===
“Who is it, and what do you want?” said a voice which I should have recognised everywhere.
In answer I took Phyllis in my arms and, whispering my name, kissed her over and over again. She uttered a little cry of astonishment and delight. Then, bidding her step quietly, I passed out into the starlight, leading her after me. As we were about to make for the path by which I had descended, Beckenham stepped forward, and at the same instant the man with whom I had been wrestling came to his senses and gave a shout of alarm. In an instant there was a noise of scurrying feet and a great shouting of orders.
“Make for the boats!” I cried at the top of my voice, and, taking Phyllis by the hand, set off as quickly as I could go up the path, Beckenham assisting her on the other side.
If I live to be a hundred I shall never forget that rush up the hill. In and out of trees and bushes, scratching ourselves and tearing our clothes, we dashed; conscious only of the necessity for speed. Before we were halfway down the other side Phyllis’s strength was quite exhausted, so I took her in my arms and carried her the remainder of the distance. At last we reached the boats and jumped on board. The rest of the party were already there, and the word being given we prepared to row out to the yacht. But before we could push off a painful surprise was in store for us. The Marquis, who had been counting the party, cried:﻿—
“Where is Mr. Wetherell?”
We looked round upon each other, and surely enough the old gentleman was missing. Discovering this, Phyllis nearly gave way and implored us to go back at once to find him. But having rescued her with so much difficulty I did not wish to run any risk of letting her fall into her enemies’ hands again; so selecting four volunteers from the party, I bade the rest pull the boat out to the yacht and give Miss Wetherell into the captain’s charge, while the balance accompanied me ashore again in search of her father. Having done this the boat was to return and wait for us.
===Chinese===
“是谁？你想干什么？”一个声音问道。这声音我在任何地方都能听出来。
作为回答，我把菲莉斯搂进怀里，低声说出自己的名字，一遍又一遍地吻她。她发出一声又惊又喜的轻呼。随后我嘱咐她放轻脚步，领着她走出小屋，来到星光之下。我们正要朝我下来的那条小径走去，贝肯汉姆迎上前来；与此同时，方才同我搏斗的那个男人醒了过来，高声示警。顷刻间，四下响起杂沓奔跑的脚步声和大声传呼号令的喊叫。
“朝小艇跑！”我扯开嗓门大喊，随即牵起菲莉斯的手，沿着小径拼命向上奔去，贝肯汉姆在另一侧搀扶着她。
哪怕活到一百岁，我也忘不了那一趟冲上山冈的狂奔。我们在树木和灌木丛间钻进钻出，身上刮得生疼，衣服也扯破了，心里只意识到一个“快”字。还没下到另一侧的半坡，菲莉斯的气力便耗尽了，于是我把她抱在怀里，抱着她走完剩下的路。终于我们奔到小艇前，跳了上去。其余的人早已到齐，一声令下，我们准备划回游艇。可还没等撑艇离岸，一桩令人心痛的意外正等着我们。一直在清点人数的侯爵失声喊道：——
“韦瑟雷尔先生在哪儿？”
我们面面相觑，老先生果然不见了。菲莉斯一发觉这情形，险些支撑不住，恳求我们立刻回去找他。可我好不容易才把她救出来，不愿再冒半点让她重落敌手的风险；于是我从队伍里挑了四名志愿者，命其余的人把艇划回游艇，把韦瑟雷尔小姐交托给船长照管，余下的随我再登岸搜寻她的父亲。这趟差事办完之后，小艇再回来接应我们。

===Original===
Quickly we splashed our way back to the beach, and then, plunging into the undergrowth, began our search for the missing man. As we did not know where to search, it was like looking for a needle in a bundle of hay, but presently one of the hands remembered having seen him descending the hill, so we devoted our attentions to that side. For nearly two hours we toiled up and down, but without success. Not a sign of the old gentleman was to be seen. Could he have mistaken his way and be even now searching for us on another beach? To make sure of this we set off and thoroughly searched the two bays in the direction he would most likely have taken. But still without success. Perhaps he had been captured and carried back to the huts? In that case we had better proceed thither and try to rescue him. This, however, was a much more serious undertaking, and you may imagine it was with considerable care that we approached the plateau again.
When we reached it the huts were as quiet as when I had first made their acquaintance. Not a sound came up to the top of the little precipice save the rustling of the wind in the palms at its foot. It seemed difficult to believe that there had been such a tumult on the spot so short a time before.
Again with infinite care we crept down to the buildings, this time, however, without encountering a soul. The first was empty, so was the second, and so was the third. This result was quite unexpected, and rendered the situation even more mysterious than before.
By the time we had thoroughly explored the plateau and its surroundings it was nearly daylight, and still we had discovered no trace of the missing man. Just as the sun rose above the sea line we descended the hill again and commenced a second search along the beach, with no better luck, however, than on the previous occasion. Wetherell and our assailants seemed to have completely disappeared from the island.
About six o’clock, thoroughly worn out, we returned to the spot where the boat was waiting for us. What was to be done? We could not for obvious reasons leave the island and abandon the old gentleman to his fate, and yet it seemed useless to remain there looking for him, when he might have been spirited away elsewhere.
Suddenly one of the crew, who had been loitering behind, came into view waving something in his hand. As he approached we could see that it was a sheet of paper, and when he gave it into my hands I read as follows:﻿—
===Chinese===
我们蹚着水花很快折回海滩，随即一头扎进灌木丛，开始搜寻失踪的人。由于不知该往哪里找，这无异于大水捞针，但不一会儿一名水手记起曾看见他下山，我们便把注意力集中到那一侧。将近两个钟头，我们在山坡上来回搜寻，累得筋疲力尽，却一无所获。老先生连一点踪影也见不到。莫非他走错了路，此刻正在另一处海滩上找我们？为查明这一点，我们动身把他最可能走的方向上的两处海湾彻底搜了一遍，仍旧毫无结果。或许他已被俘，被押回那些小屋去了？若是那样，我们最好赶往那里设法救他。然而这可是严重得多的一桩举动，可以想见，我们再度逼近高地时，是何等小心翼翼。
到达那里时，那些小屋静得一如我初来结识它们的时候。除了崖脚棕榈间飒飒的风声，再没有一丝声息传上小崖顶来。很难相信，片刻之前这里还闹过那样一场骚乱。
我们再次以百般的小心潜到那几座屋子跟前，这一回却没有碰见一个人。第一座是空的，第二座也是空的，第三座还是空的。这结果全然出人意料，使局面变得比先前更加扑朔迷离。
等我们把高地和四周彻底搜遍，天已近破晓，我们仍未发现失踪者的半点踪迹。太阳刚升出海平线，我们便再次下山，沿着海滩开始第二轮搜寻，然而运气并不比上一轮好。韦瑟雷尔和袭击我们的人仿佛都已从岛上消失得无影无踪。
六点钟光景，我们精疲力竭地回到小艇候着我们的地方。该怎么办呢？出于显而易见的理由，我们不能离岛而去，任老先生自生自灭；可他也许早已被神不知鬼不觉地弄去了别处，留在这里找他又似乎全然无用。
忽然，一名落在后头的水手出现在视野里，手里挥着一样东西。他走近时，我们看清那是一张纸。他把纸交到我手上，只见上面写道：——

===Original===
> If you cross the island to the north beach you will find a small cliff in which is a large cave, a little above high-water mark. There you will discover the man for whom you are searching.
There was no signature to this epistle, and the writing was quite unfamiliar to me, but I had no reason to doubt its authenticity.
“Where did you discover this?” I enquired of the man who had brought it.
“Fastened to one of them prickly bushes up on the beach there, sir,” he answered.
“Well, the only thing for us to do now is to set off to the north shore and hunt for the cave. Two of you had better take the boat back to the yacht and ask the captain to follow us round.”
As soon as the boat was underway we picked up our rifles and set off for the north beach. It was swelteringly hot by this time, and, as may be imagined, we were all dead tired after our long night’s work. However, the men knew they would be amply rewarded if we could effect the rescue of the man for whom we had been searching, so they pushed on.
===Chinese===
> 你们若横穿此岛到北岸海滩，会见到一座小崖，崖里有一个大山洞，位置略高于满潮线。在那里，你们将找到你们要找的那个人。
这封短信没有署名，笔迹我也全然陌生，但我没有理由怀疑它的真实性。
“你在哪儿发现这东西的？”我问那个送信来的人。
“就钉在这上头海滩上的一棵刺灌木上，先生。”他答道。
“好吧，眼下我们唯一能做的就是动身去北岸，搜寻那个山洞。你们两位最好把艇划回游艇，请船长绕岛跟上来。”
小艇一开动，我们便提起步枪，动身前往北岸海滩。这时天气已酷热难当，可以想见，熬了一整夜之后我们都累得要死。不过水手们知道，只要能救出那位我们搜寻已久的人，他们必得重赏，因此都奋力向前。

===Original===
At last we turned the cape and entered the bay which constituted the north end of the island. It was not a large beach on this side, but it had, at its western end, a curious line of small cliffs, in the centre of which a small black spot could be discerned looking remarkably like the entrance to a cave. Towards this we pressed, forgetting our weariness in the excitement of the search.
It was a cave, and large one. So far the letter was correct. Preparing ourselves, in case of surprise, we approached the entrance, calling Mr. Wetherell’s name. As our shouts died away a voice came out in answer, and thereupon we rushed in.
A remarkable sight met our eyes. In the centre of the cave was a stout upright post, some six or eight feet in height, and securely tied to this was the Colonial Secretary of New South Wales.
In less time almost than it takes to tell, we had cast loose the ropes which bound him, and led him, for he was too weak to stand alone, out into the open air. While he was resting he enquired after his daughter, and having learned that she was safe, gave us the following explanation. Addressing himself to me he said:
“When you cried ‘Make for the boats,’ I ran up the hill with the others as fast as I could go; but I’m an old man and could not get along as quickly as I wanted to, and for this reason was soon left far behind. I must have been halfway down the hill when a tall man, dressed in white, stepped out from behind a bush, and raising a rifle bade me come to a standstill. Having no time to lift my own weapon I was obliged to do as he ordered me, and he thereupon told me to lay down my weapon and right-about face. In this fashion I was marched back to the huts we had just left, and then, another man having joined my captor, was conducted across the island to this beach, where a boat was in waiting. In it I was pulled out to a small schooner lying at anchor in the bay and ordered to board her; five minutes later I was conducted to the saloon, where two or three persons were collected.
===Chinese===
终于我们绕过海角，进入构成岛屿北端的那处海湾。这一侧的海滩不算大，但西端有一线形状古怪的小崖，崖壁正中隐约可辨一个黑色小点，看上去极像一处洞口。我们朝那里直赶过去，在搜寻的兴奋中忘却了疲惫。
那果然是个山洞，而且是个大山洞。信上说的到此不假。我们做好了应付意外的准备，逼近洞口，高声呼唤韦瑟雷尔先生的名字。喊声甫落，里面传出一个声音应答，我们便冲了进去。
一幅奇特的景象映入我们眼帘。洞穴中央立着一根粗壮的直木桩，高约六到八英尺，新南威尔士的殖民地秘书被结结实实地捆在上面。
不消片刻，我们便割断了捆他的绳索，搀着他走出洞外——他已虚弱得无法独自站立。趁他歇息的当口，他问起女儿，得知她安然无恙后，便向我们做了如下说明。他朝着我说道：
“听见你喊‘朝小艇跑’，我便同众人一起，能跑多快跑多快地往山上奔；可我上了年纪，赶不上想赶的速度，很快便被远远甩在了后面。大约下到半山，一个白衣高个的男人从一丛灌木后面闪出来，端起步枪喝令我站住。我来不及举起自己的武器，只得听他吩咐。他随即命我放下武器，来个向后转。就这样，我被押回了我们刚离开的那些小屋；随后，押我的人又添了一个帮手，我被带着横穿全岛，来到这片海滩，那里有一条小艇候着。他们用艇把我划到湾中抛锚停泊的一艘小纵帆船旁，命我登船；五分钟后，我被引进客厅，那里已经聚了两三个人。

===Original===
“ ‘Good evening, Mr. Wetherell. This is indeed a pleasure,’ said a man sitting at the further end of the table. He was playing with a big black cat, and directly I heard his voice I knew that I was in the presence of Dr. Nikola.
“ ‘And how do you think I am going to punish you, my friend, for giving me all this trouble?’ he said when I made no reply to his first remark.
“ ‘You dare not do anything to me,’ I answered. ‘I demand that you let me go this instant. I have a big score to settle with you.’
“ ‘If you will be warned by me you will cease to demand,’ he answered, his eyes the while burning like coals. ‘You are an obstinate man, but though you have put me to so much trouble and expense I will forgive you and come to terms with you. Now listen to me. If you will give me﻿—’
“At that moment the little vessel gave a heavy roll, and in trying to keep my footing on the sloping deck I fell over upon the table. As I did so the little Chinese stick slipped out of my pocket and went rolling along directly into Nikola’s hands. He sprang forward and seized it, and you may imagine his delight. With a cry of triumph that made the cat leap from his shoulder, he turned to a tall man by his side and said:
“ ‘I’ve got it at last! Now let a boat’s crew take this man ashore and tie him to the stake in the cave. Then devise some means of acquainting his friends of his whereabouts. Be quick, for we sail in an hour.’
===Chinese===
“‘晚上好，韦瑟雷尔先生。这真是莫大的荣幸。’坐在桌子另一头的一个男人说道。他正逗弄着一只大黑猫，一听见他的声音我便明白，站在我面前的正是尼古拉博士。
“‘那么你说说，我的朋友，你给我添了这许多麻烦，我该怎么罚你呢？’见我对他的第一句话不答腔，他说道。
“‘你不敢把我怎么样。’我答道，‘我要求你立刻放我走。我同你之间还有一笔大账要算。’
“‘你要是肯听我的劝，就趁早别提要求。’他答道，那双眼睛一直烧得像炭火一样。‘你是个固执的人，不过，尽管你害我耗费了这许多周折和钱财，我仍愿原谅你，同你讲和。现在听着。要是你肯把——’
“恰在此时，那艘小船猛地一侧，我在倾斜的甲板上立足不稳，一头栽倒在桌子上。就在这一跌之间，那根中国小木棒从我口袋里滑了出来，骨碌碌一路滚去，不偏不倚正滚进尼古拉手里。他一跃而起，一把攥住它，他那份狂喜你尽可以想象。他发出一声得胜的欢呼，惊得那只猫从他肩头蹿了下去，随即转头对身旁一个高个子男人吩咐道：
“‘我终于到手了！马上派一艇人把这个人送上岸去，捆在山洞里的木桩上。然后设法让他的朋友们知道他的下落。快些，一个钟头后我们就起航。’

===Original===
“Having given these orders he turned to me again and said:
“ ‘Mr. Wetherell, this is the last transaction we shall probably ever have together. All things considered, you are lucky in escaping so easily. It would have saved you a good deal if you had complied with my request at first. However, all’s well that ends well, and I congratulate you upon your charming daughter. Now, goodbye; in an hour I am off to effect a coup with this stick, the magnitude of which you would never dream. One last word of advice: pause a second time, I entreat, before you think of baulking Dr. Nikola.’
“I was going to reply, when I was twisted round and led up on deck, where that scoundrel Baxter had the impudence to make me a low bow. In less than a quarter of an hour I was fastened to the post in that cave. The rest you know. Now let us get on board; I see the boat is approaching.”
As soon as the surfboat had drawn up on the beach we embarked and were pulled out to the yacht. In a few moments we were on deck, and Phyllis was in her father’s arms again. Over that meeting, with its rapturous embraces and general congratulations, I must draw a curtain. Suffice it that by midday the island had disappeared under the sea line, and by nightfall we were well on our way back to Sydney. That evening, after dinner, Phyllis and I patrolled the deck together, and finally came to a standstill aft. It was as beautiful an evening as any man or woman could desire. All round us was the glassy sea, rising and falling as if asleep, while overhead the tropic stars shone down with their wonderful brilliance.
“Phyllis,” I said, taking my darling’s hand in mine and looking into her face, “what a series of adventures we have both passed through since that afternoon I first saw you in the Domain! Do you know that your father has at last consented to our marriage?”
“I do. And as it is to you, Dick, I owe my rescue,” she said, coming a little closer to me, “he could do nothing else; you have a perfect right to me.”
===Chinese===
“发完这道命令，他又转向我说道：
“‘韦瑟雷尔先生，这多半是我们之间做的最后一笔交易了。通盘算来，你能这样轻易脱身，实在算走运。当初你若一口应下我的请求，本可省去许多麻烦。不过，结局好便一切都好，我还要恭喜你有这样一位迷人的千金。好了，再见；一个钟头后，我就要带着这根木棒去成就一桩惊人之举，其规模之大，你做梦也想象不到。最后奉劝一句：往后你想阻挠尼古拉博士之前，我恳求你，务必先掂量掂量。’
“我正要答话，就被人拧过身去，押上了甲板；那恶棍巴克斯特竟有脸对我深深一躬。不到一刻钟，我便被捆上了那个山洞里的木桩。其余的事你们都知道了。上船吧，我看见小艇来了。”
冲浪艇一靠上沙滩，我们便登了上去，被划回游艇。片刻之后我们已站在甲板上，菲莉斯重新回到了父亲的怀抱里。那一场重逢，连同那些狂喜的拥抱和此起彼伏的道贺，我只好拉上一道帷幕，略过不表。只消说一句：到正午时分，那座岛已沉没在海平线之下；到夜幕降临时，我们已踏上返回悉尼的航程良久。那天晚饭后，我和菲莉斯在甲板上并肩踱步，最后在船尾停了下来。这是一个无论男人女人都无可挑剔的美妙夜晚。四围是镜面一般的海水，一起一伏，仿佛沉入了睡乡；头顶上，热带的星辰正以它们奇妙的光辉朗照下来。
“菲莉斯，”我说着，把心上人的手握进自己手中，凝视着她的脸，“自从那天下午我在多曼公园第一次见到你，你我两人经历了怎样一连串的奇遇啊！你可知道，你父亲终于应允我们的婚事了？”
“我知道。再说，迪克，我这条命是你救回来的。”她说着，向我身边挨近了些，“他不答应也由不得他；你对我是有着十足权利的。”

===Original===
“I have, and I mean to assert it!” I answered. “If I had not found you, I should never have been happy again.”
“But, Dick, there is one thing I don’t at all understand. At dinner this evening the captain addressed you as Sir Richard. What does that mean?”
“Why, of course you have not heard!” I cried. “Well, I think it means that though I cannot make you a marchioness, I can make you a baronet’s wife. It remains with you to say whether you will be Lady Hatteras or not.”
“But are you a baronet, Dick? How did that come about?”
“It’s a long story, but do you remember my describing to you the strange call I paid, when in England, on my only two relatives in the world?”
“The old man and his daughter in the New Forest? Yes, I remember.”
===Chinese===
“我有，而且我打算主张这份权利！”我答道，“倘若找不到你，我这辈子再也不会幸福了。”
“可是迪克，有一件事我全然不明白。今晚吃饭的时候，船长称呼你理查德爵士。那是什么意思？”
“哎呀，你自然还没听说！”我叫道，“是这样：我虽不能封你做侯爵夫人，却能让你做从男爵的妻子。做不做哈特拉斯爵士夫人，全凭你一句话。”
“可你真是个从男爵吗，迪克？这是怎么来的？”
“说来话长。不过你可记得我同你讲过，我在英国时去拜访世上仅有的两位亲人，那趟古怪的造访？”
“新森林里那位老人和他的女儿？记得，我记得。”

===Original===
“Well, they are both dead, and, as the next-of-kin, I have inherited the title and estates. What do you think of that?”
Her only reply was to kiss me softly on the cheek.
She had scarcely done so before her father and Beckenham came along the deck.
“Now, Phyllis,” said the former, leading her to a seat, “supposing you give us the history of your adventures. Remember we have heard nothing yet.”
“Very well. Where shall I begin? At the moment I left the house for the ball? Very good. Well, you must know that when I arrived at Government House I met Mrs. Mayford﻿—the lady who had promised to chaperone me﻿—in the cloakroom, and we passed into the ballroom together. I danced the first dance with Captain Hackworth, one of the aides, and engaged myself for the fourth to the Marquis of Beckenham.”
“The sham Marquis, unfortunately,” put in the real one.
===Chinese===
“是这样，他们两位都过世了，我作为最近血亲，继承了爵位和家产。你觉得如何？”
她只是在我脸颊上轻轻一吻，权作回答。
她刚吻完，她父亲和贝肯汉姆便沿着甲板走了过来。
“好了，菲莉斯，”前者说着，引她到一张座位跟前，“给我们讲讲你的历险经过吧。记住，我们还什么都没听说过呢。”
“好吧。从哪儿讲起呢？就从离家赴舞会那一刻讲起？很好。那么你们要知道，我到总督府时，在衣帽间遇见了梅福德太太（Mrs. Mayford）——就是答应做我女伴的那位夫人——我们便一同走进舞厅。第一支舞我同副官之一的哈克沃思上尉（Captain Hackworth）跳，第四支则答应了贝肯汉姆侯爵。”
“可惜那是位假侯爵。”真正的侯爵插嘴道。

===Original===
“It proved to be unfortunate for me also,” continued Phyllis. “As it was a square we sat it out in the anteroom leading off the drawing-room, and while we were there the young gentleman did me the honour of proposing to me. It was terribly embarrassing for me, but I allowed him to see, as unmistakably as possible, that I could give him no encouragement, and, as the introduction to the next waltz started, we parted the best of friends. About half an hour later, just as I was going to dance the lancers, Mrs. Mayford came towards me and drew me into the drawing-room. Mr. Baxter, his lordship’s tutor, was with her, and I noticed that they both looked supernaturally grave.
“ ‘What is the matter?’ I asked, becoming alarmed by her face.
“ ‘My dear,’ said she, ‘you must be brave. I have come to tell you that your father has been taken ill, and has sent for you.’
“ ‘Papa ill!’ I cried. ‘Oh, I must go home to him at once.’
“ ‘I have taken the liberty of facilitating that,’ said Mr. Baxter, ‘by ordering the servants to call up your carriage, which is now waiting for you at the door. If you will allow me, I will conduct you to it?’
“I apologised to my partner for being compelled to leave him, and then went to the cloakroom. As soon as I was ready I accompanied Mr. Baxter to the door, where the brougham was waiting. Without looking at the coachman I got in, at the same time thanking my escort for his kindness. He shut the door and cried ‘Home’ to the coachman. Next moment we were spinning down the drive.
===Chinese===
“事实证明，对我而言这也是桩不幸。”菲莉斯接着说，“那是一支方舞，我们便在客厅隔壁的前厅里坐着歇了一场；正坐在那儿，那位年轻绅士竟向我求婚，给了我天大的荣幸。这让我尴尬到了极点，但我让他尽可能明白无误地看出，我给不了他半点鼓励；待到下一支华尔兹的前奏响起，我们便以最好的朋友身份分了手。约莫半个钟头后，我正要去跳兰切斯舞（lancers），梅福德太太朝我走来，把我拉进客厅。侯爵大人的家庭教师巴克斯特先生同她在一起，我注意到他们两人都面色阴沉得异乎寻常。
“‘出什么事了？’我问道，她的脸色让我惊慌起来。
“‘亲爱的，’她说，‘你要坚强。我是来告诉你，你父亲病倒了，派人去叫你。’
“‘爸爸病了！’我叫道，‘噢，我必须马上回家看他。’
“‘我已斗胆把这件事替您办妥了。’巴克斯特先生说，‘我吩咐仆人去唤您的马车，此刻车已候在门口。您若允许，我送您上去？’
“我向舞伴道歉，说不得不失陪，随后去衣帽间取衣裳。我一收拾停当，便由巴克斯特先生送到门口，四轮轿式马车正候在那里。我没有看车夫一眼便上了车，一面感谢护送我的人一番好意。他关上车门，朝车夫喊了一声‘回家’。转瞬间，马车已沿着车道辘辘疾驰而去。

## IX Dr. Nikola Permits Us a Free Passage / 第九章 尼古拉博士恩准放行



===Original===

The old saying, “Don’t count your chickens before they’re hatched,” is as good a warning as any I know. Certainly it proved so in our case. For if we had not been so completely occupied filing through the staples of our collars we should not have omitted to take into consideration the fact that, even when we should have removed the chains that bound us, we would still be prisoners in the room. I’m very much afraid, however, even had we remembered this point, we should only have considered it of minor importance and one to be easily overcome. As it was, the unwelcome fact remained that the door was locked, and, what was worse, that the lock itself had, for security’s sake, been placed on the outside, so that there was no chance of our being able to pick it, even had our accomplishments lain in that direction. “Try the window,” whispered Beckenham, in answer to the heavy sigh which followed my last discovery.

Accordingly we crossed the room, and I put my hands upon one of the boards and pulled. But I might as well have tried to tow a troopship with a piece of cotton, for all the satisfactory result I got; the planks were trebly screwed to the window frame, and each in turn defied me. When I was tired Beckenham put his strength to it, but even our united efforts were of no avail, and, panting and exhausted, we were at length obliged to give it up as hopeless.

“This is a pretty fix we’ve got ourselves into,” I said as soon as I had recovered sufficient breath to speak. “We can’t remain here, and yet how on earth are we to escape?”

“I can’t say, unless we manage to burst that door open and fight our way out. I wonder if that could be done.”

“First, let’s look at the door.”

===Chinese===

俗话说得好，“鸡蛋未孵，莫数鸡雏”，我所知道的警语里，再没有比这更灵验的了。在我们这件事上，它果然应了验。因为倘若我们不是全副心思都用在锉断领圈上的卡钉上，本不该漏想这一层：即便锉断了拴着我们的锁链，我们仍旧是困在这间屋子里的囚徒。不过我很担心，即便想起了这一点，我们也多半只会把它当作无足轻重的小事，认为轻易便能解决。可眼前这个不受欢迎的事实明摆着：门是锁着的，更糟的是，那把锁为了保险起见，偏偏安在外侧，我们纵然有开锁的本事，也绝无下手的可能。贝肯汉姆听到我这番新发现后的一声长叹，低声道：“试试窗户。”

于是我们走到窗前，我双手扣住一块木板，用力猛拉。可我收获的满意结果，无异于想用一根棉线拖走一艘运兵船；那些板子都用三重螺钉钉死在窗框上，一块块轮番与我较劲。我拉乏了，贝肯汉姆又上来使劲，但即便我们合力，也是徒劳无功，末了我们气喘吁吁、筋疲力尽，只得认命作罢。

“咱们这回可陷得漂亮，”等我喘匀了气、能开口说话了，我说道，“待在这儿是死路一条，可我们究竟怎么逃出去呢？”

“我说不上来，除非想办法撞开那扇门，杀出一条路去。不知道这法子行不行得通。”

“先来看看那扇门吧。”

===Original===

We crossed the room again, and I examined the door carefully with my fingers. It was not a very strong one; but I was sufficient of a carpenter to know that it would withstand a good deal of pressure before it would give way.

“I’ve a good mind to try it,” I said; “but in that case, remember, it will probably mean a hand-to-hand fight on the other side, and, unarmed and weak as we are, we shall be pretty sure to get the worst of it.”

“Never mind that,” my intrepid companion replied, with a confidence in his voice that I was very far from feeling. “In for a penny, in for a pound; even if we’re killed it couldn’t be worse than being buried alive in here.”

“That’s so, and if fighting’s your idea, I’m your man,” I answered. “Let me first take my bearings, and then I’ll see what I can do against it. You get out of the way, but be sure to stand by to rush the passage directly the door goes.”

Again I felt the door and wall in order that I might be sure where it lay, and having done so crossed the room. My heart was beating like a Nasmyth hammer, and it was nearly a minute before I could pull myself together sufficiently for my rush. Then summoning every muscle in my body to my assistance, I dashed across and at it with all the strength my frame was capable of. Considering the darkness of the room, my steering was not so bad, for my shoulder caught the door just above its centre; there was a great crash﻿—a noise of breaking timbers﻿—and amid a shower of splinters and general debris I fell headlong through into the passage. By the time it would have taken me to count five, Beckenham was beside me helping me to rise.

===Chinese===

我们又折回屋子那头，我用手指仔细查验那扇门。门不算太结实；但我好歹懂些木匠活，知道不施加相当大的压力，它是不会松动的。

“我很想试它一试，”我说，“不过那样的话，你记住，门那头多半免不了一场白刃相搏，而我们手无寸铁、身子又虚，十有八九要吃大亏。”

“那算什么，”我这位无畏的同伴答道，话音里的那股自信，我实在远远及不上。“一不做，二不休；就算丢了性命，也总比活埋在这屋里强。”

“这话不假，你既要打，我奉陪，”我答道，“先让我认清方位，再看看怎么收拾它。你闪开些，但务必守在旁边，门一倒就立刻冲进过道去。”

我又摸了摸门和墙，确定门的准确位置，然后退回屋子这头。我的心跳得像一台内史密斯蒸汽锤（Nasmyth hammer），差不多过了一分钟，我才把心神收拢到足以发起冲锋。接着我调动全身每一块肌肉前来效力，拼尽这副身板所能挤出的全部力气，猛冲过去，直撞向那扇门。考虑到屋里一片漆黑，我这方向的拿捏还算不坏，肩膀正撞在门的中心偏上处；只听轰的一声巨响——木头碎裂之声大作——我在木屑和断片纷落如雨之中，一头栽进了过道。前后不过数完五下的工夫，贝肯汉姆已到我身旁，扶我起身。

===Original===

“Now stand by for big trouble!” I said, rubbing my shoulder, and every moment expecting to see a door open and a crowd of Prendergast’s ruffians come rushing out. “We shall have them on us in a minute.”

But to our intense astonishment it was all dead silence. Not a sound of any single kind, save our excited breathing, greeted our ears. We might have broken into an empty house for all we knew the difference.

For nearly five minutes we stood, side by side, waiting for the battle which did not come.

“What on earth does it meant” I asked my companion. “That crash of mine was loud enough to wake the dead. Can they have deserted the place, think you, and left us to starve?”

“I can’t make it out any more than you can,” he answered. “But don’t you think we’d better take advantage of their not coming to find a way out?”

===Chinese===

“这就要有大麻烦了，当心！”我揉着肩膀说，时时刻刻都以为会有一扇门豁然洞开，普伦德加斯特的一群匪徒冲将出来。“他们转眼就会扑到我们头上。”

然而令我们万分愕然的是，四周竟是死一般的寂静。除了我们自己激动的呼吸声，任何响动都没有传进我们耳中。单凭耳闻，我们还当是闯进了一座空宅。

我们并肩站了将近五分钟，等着一场迟迟不来的战斗。

“这究竟是怎么回事？”我问同伴，“我那一撞，响得足以把死人吵醒。你说，他们会不会弃了这地方，任凭我们饿死？”

“我跟你一样摸不着头脑，”他答道，“不过他们既不来，我们何不趁这个空子找条出路？”

===Original===

“Of course. One of us had better creep down the passage and discover how the land lies. As I’m the stronger, I’ll go. You wait here.”

I crept along the passage, treading cautiously as a cat, for I knew that both our lives depended on it. Though it could not have been more than sixty feet, it seemed of interminable length, and was as black as night. Not a glimmer of light, however faint, met my eyes.

On and on I stole, expecting every moment to be pounced upon and seized; but no such fate awaited me. If, however, our jailers did not appear, another danger was in store for me.

In the middle of my walk my feet suddenly went from under me, and I found myself falling I knew not where. In reality it was only a drop of about three feet down a short flight of steps. Such a noise as my fall made, however, was surely never heard, but still no sound came. Then Beckenham fumbled his way cautiously down the steps to my side, and whispered an enquiry as to what had happened. I told him in as few words as possible, and then struggled to my feet again.

Just as I did so my eyes detected a faint glimmer of light low down on the floor ahead of us. From its position it evidently emanated from the doorway of a room.

===Chinese===

“当然。咱们最好有一个顺着过道摸过去，探探虚实。我力气大些，我去。你在这儿等着。”

我顺着过道匍匐前行，脚步轻得像猫，因为我知道我们两条性命都系于此。那段路充其量不过六十英尺，走起来却似无穷无尽，而且黑得如深夜一般。哪怕一丝最微弱的光，也不曾映入我的眼帘。

我一路潜行，时时刻刻提防被人猛扑按住；但那样的厄运并没有等着我。不过，看守我们的人虽未现身，另一种危险却在前面候着我。

走到中途，我的脚下一空，整个人朝着一个不知深浅的地方跌了下去。其实那不过是一段矮台阶，落差约三英尺。可我这一跤跌出的动静，恐怕天下少有，却依旧没有任何声息。随后贝肯汉姆摸索着小心下了台阶，到我身边低声问出了什么事。我用最简短的话告诉了他，然后挣扎着重新站起来。

就在这时，我看见前方贴着地面的地方透出一点微光。从位置判断，那光显然是从某个房间的门口漏出来的。

===Original===

“Oh! if only we had a match,” I whispered.

“It’s no good wishing,” said Beckenham. “What do you advise?”

“It’s difficult to say,” I answered; “but I should think we’d better listen at that door and try to discover if there is anyone inside. If there is, and he is alone, we must steal in upon him, let him see that we are desperate, and, willy-nilly, force him to show us a way out. It’s ten chances to one, if we go on prowling about here, we shall stumble upon the whole nest of them﻿—then we’ll be caught like rats in a trap. What do you think?”

“I agree with you. Go on.”

Without further ado we crept towards the light, which, as I expected, came from under a door, and listened. Someone was plainly moving about inside; but though we waited for what seemed a quarter of an hour, but must in reality have been less than a minute and a half, we could hear no voices.

===Chinese===

“唉！这时要有一根火柴就好了。”我低声道。

“空想无益，”贝肯汉姆说，“你有什么主意？”

“很难说，”我答道，“不过我想，咱们最好先在那扇门外听听，弄清里面有没有人。倘若有人，而且只有他一个，我们就得悄悄摸进去，让他瞧瞧我们是拼了命的，不由他愿不愿，硬逼他给我们指出一条出路。要是咱们在这儿继续乱撞，十有八九会一头撞进他们的老窝——那时就成了瓮中之鳖。你看呢？”

“我赞成。走吧。”

我们不再多话，朝那光亮爬了过去。果然不出我所料，光是从一扇门底下透出来的。我们贴门细听：里面分明有人在走动；但我们等了仿佛一刻钟之久——实际上必定不到一分半钟——却听不见一点人声。

===Original===

“Whoever he is, he’s alone﻿—that’s certain,” whispered my companion. “Open the door softly, and we’ll creep in upon him.”

In answer, and little by little, a cold shiver running down my back lest it should creak and so give warning to the person within, I turned the handle, pushed open the door, and we looked inside. Then﻿—but, my gracious! if I live to be a thousand I shall never forget even the smallest particular connected with the sight that met my eyes.

The room itself was a long and low one: its measurements possibly sixty feet by fifteen. The roof﻿—for there was no ceiling﻿—was of wood, crossed by heavy rafters, and much begrimed with dirt and smoke. The floor was of some highly polished wood closely resembling oak and was completely bare. But the shape and construction of the room itself were as nothing compared with the strangeness of its furniture and occupants. Words would fail me if I tried to give you a true and accurate description of it. I only know that, strong man as I was, and used to the horrors of life and death, what I saw before me then made my blood run cold and my flesh creep as it had never done before.

===Chinese===

“不管他是谁，反正只有他一个——这可以断定，”我的同伴低声道，“轻轻把门推开，咱们摸进去制住他。”

我应了一声，一点一点地旋动把手——一股寒意顺着脊背直淌下来，唯恐门轴吱呀一响，惊动里面的人——推开门，我们朝里望去。接着——天哪！哪怕我活上一千岁，也忘不了映入我眼帘的那幅景象的任何细枝末节。

这间屋子狭长而低矮：尺寸大约是六十英尺长、十五英尺宽。屋顶——因为屋里没有吊顶——是木头的，架着一根根粗大的椽子，积满污垢烟尘。地板是某种打磨得锃亮的木头，很像橡木，空荡荡一无所有。然而屋子本身的形制构造，比起它那些陈设和居住者之诡异来，简直不值一提。若想给你一个忠实而准确的描绘，我的笔墨是无能为力的。我只知道，我虽是个硬汉，见惯了生生死死的惨状，可当时眼前所见，仍使我血冷肌栗，远非从前任何经历可比。

===Original===

To begin with, round the walls were arranged, at regular intervals, more than a dozen enormous bottles, each of which contained what looked, to me, only too much like human specimens pickled in some light-coloured fluid resembling spirits of wine. Between these gigantic but more than horrible receptacles were numberless smaller ones holding other and even more dreadful remains; while on pedestals and stands, bolt upright and reclining, were skeletons of men, monkeys, and quite a hundred sorts of animals. The intervening spaces were filled with skulls, bones, and the apparatus for every kind of murder known to the fertile brain of man. There were European rifles, revolvers, bayonets, and swords; Italian stilettos, Turkish scimitars, Greek knives, Central African spears and poisoned arrows, Zulu knobkerries, Afghan yataghans, Malay krises, Sumatra blowpipes, Chinese dirks, New Guinea head-catching implements, Australian spears and boomerangs, Polynesian stone hatchets, and numerous other weapons the names of which I cannot now remember. Mixed up with them were implements for every sort of wizardry known to the superstitious; from old-fashioned English love charms to African Obi sticks, from spiritualistic planchettes to the most horrible of Fijian death potions.

In the centre of the wall, opposite to where we stood, was a large fireplace of the fashion usually met with in old English manor-houses, and on either side of it a figure that nearly turned me sick with horror. That on the right hand was apparently a native of Northern India, if one might judge by his dress and complexion. He sat on the floor in a constrained attitude, accounted for by the fact that his head, which was at least three times too big for his body, was so heavy as to require an iron tripod with a ring or collar in the top of it to keep it from overbalancing him and bringing him to the floor. To add to the horror of this awful head, it was quite bald; the skin was drawn tensely over the bones, and upon this great veins stood out as large as macaroni stems.

On the other side of the hearth was a creature half-ape and half-man﻿—the like of which I remember once to have seen in a museum of monstrosities in Sydney, where, if my memory serves me, he was described upon the catalogue as a Burmese monkey-boy. He was chained to the wall in somewhat the same fashion as we had been, and was chattering and scratching for all the world like a monkey in a Zoo.

===Chinese===

首先，沿墙等距离地陈放着十几只硕大无朋的玻璃瓶，依我看，每只瓶里泡着的东西，都太像浸在某种近似酒精的浅色液体里的人体标本。这些庞大得骇人的容器之间，还有无数小瓶，盛着别的、甚至更加可怖的残余之物；台座和架子之上，或直挺挺立着、或斜倚着男人、猴子和足足上百种动物的骸骨。空隙处堆满了骷髅、白骨，以及人类那颗富于创见的头脑所能发明的每一种谋杀器具：欧洲的步枪、左轮枪、刺刀和刀剑；意大利的短剑（stiletto）、土耳其的弯刀（scimitar）、希腊的匕首、中非的长矛与毒箭、祖鲁的圆头战棒（knobkerrie）、阿富汗的亚塔甘弯刀（yataghan）、马来波刃短剑（kris）、苏门答腊的吹箭筒、中国的短刀、新几内亚的猎头器具、澳大利亚的长矛与回旋镖、波利尼西亚的石斧，还有许许多多如今我已叫不出名字的兵器。与它们混杂一处的，是迷信之人所知的每一种巫术的法器：从老式的英国求爱符到非洲的奥比巫棒（Obi stick），从通灵术的灵应板（planchette）到斐济最骇人的夺命毒药。

正对我们所站位置的墙中央，是一座老式英国庄园宅邸里常见样式的大壁炉，壁炉两侧各有一个身影，险些把我恶心得吐出来。右边那个，从衣着和肤色判断，显然是北印度土人。他别扭地坐在地上，这姿势是有缘故的：他的脑袋对身子来说至少大了三倍，沉重得必须用一架顶端带环圈或领箍的铁三脚架撑住，才不致把他整个掀翻在地。这颗骇人脑袋更叫人胆寒的是，它寸发不生；皮肉紧绷绷地蒙在骨头上，上面暴起的一条条大血管，粗得如同通心粉梗。

壁炉另一侧是个半猿半人的活物——我记得曾在悉尼一家畸形人博物馆里见过同类；如果我没记错，目录上把他唤作缅甸猴童。他被链子拴在墙上，那方式同我们方才受的待遇差不多，正吱吱喋喋、抓耳搔腮，活脱脱一只动物园里的猴子。

===Original===

But, horrible as these things were, the greatest surprise of all was yet to come. For, standing at the heavy oaken table in the centre of the room, was a man I should have known anywhere if I had been permitted half a glance at him. It was Dr. Nikola.

When we entered he was busily occupied with a scalpel, dissecting an animal strangely resembling a monkey. On the table, and watching the work upon which his master was engaged, sat his constant companion, the same fiendish black cat I have mentioned elsewhere. While at the end nearest us, standing on tiptoe, the better to see what was going on, was an albino dwarf, scarcely more than two feet eight inches high.

Now, though it has necessarily taken me some time to describe the scene which greeted our eyes, it must not be supposed that anything like the same length of time had really elapsed since our entry. Three seconds at the very most would have sufficed to cover the whole period.

So stealthily, however, had our approach been made, and so carefully had I opened the door, that we were well into the room before our appearance was discovered, and also before I had realized into whose presence we had stumbled. Then my foot touched a board that creaked, and Dr. Nikola looked up from the work upon which he was engaged.

His pale, thin face did not show the slightest sign of surprise as he said, in his usual placid tone﻿—

===Chinese===

然而，这些东西纵然骇人，最大的意外还在后头。只见屋子中央那张厚实的橡木桌旁，站着一个人——哪怕只许我瞥上他半眼，无论天涯海角我也认得出来。那正是尼古拉博士。

我们进来时，他正忙着用一把解剖刀剖一只要说像猴子的古怪动物。桌上蹲着他那形影不离的伙伴——就是我在别处提到过的那只恶魔般的黑猫，看着他主人手上的活计。而离我们最近的桌端，踮着脚尖以便看清动静的，是一个患白化症的侏儒，身高几乎不足二英尺八英寸。

话虽如此，尽管描绘这幅映入我们眼帘的景象难免费去我一些笔墨，但切不可因此以为，自我们进屋起真过去了同样长的时间。这段全程，至多三秒钟便尽够了。

不过，我们潜行得如此隐秘，我开门又开得如此小心，直到深入屋内，我们的现身才被人察觉——我也是到了那时才意识到，自己闯进的是什么人的面前。接着我的脚踩上一块咯吱作响的地板，尼古拉博士从他手头的活计上抬起了头。

他苍白瘦削的脸上没有露出一丝一毫的惊讶，只以他惯常那种平和的口气说道——

===Original===

“So you have managed to escape from your room, gentlemen. Well, and pray what do you want with me?”

For a moment I was so much overcome with surprise that my tongue refused to perform its office. Then I said, advancing towards him as I spoke, closely followed by the Marquis﻿—

“So, Dr. Nikola, we have met at last!”

“At last, Mr. Hatteras, as you say,” this singular being replied, still without showing a sign of either interest or embarrassment. “All things considered, I suppose you would deem me ironical if I ventured to say that I am pleased to see you about again. However, don’t let me keep you standing, won’t you sit down? My lord, let me offer you a chair.”

All this time we were edging up alongside the table, and I was making ready for a rush at him. But he was not to be taken off his guard. His extraordinary eyes had been watching me intently, taking in my every movement; and a curious effect their steady gaze had upon me.

“Dr. Nikola,” I said, pulling myself together, “the game is up. You beat me last time; but now you must own I come out on top. Don’t utter a word or call for assistance﻿—if you do you’re a dead man. Now drop that knife you hold in your hand, and show us the way out!”

===Chinese===

“两位先生，你们到底还是从那间屋子里逃出来了。好啊，那么请问，找我有什么事？”

一时间我惊愕得失了神，舌头竟不肯听使唤。随后我开了口，一边说一边朝他走去，侯爵紧随我身后——

“好啊，尼古拉博士，我们终究还是碰面了！”

“终究碰面了，哈特拉斯先生，正如你所说，”这个奇特的人物答道，依旧不见半点兴致或窘迫之色。“通盘考虑起来，我若冒昧说一句，很高兴看见你们又能走动走动了，你们大概要当我是在说反话。不过，别叫我老让你们站着——两位不坐坐吗？爵爷，请容我奉你一把椅子。”

这一番话的工夫，我们一直沿着桌边悄悄蹭近，我也在酝酿朝他猛扑。可他不容自己有半点疏懈。那双异乎寻常的眼睛一直紧盯着我，我的一举一动尽收眼底；而它们那凝定的注视，对我产生着一种奇妙的作用。

“尼古拉博士，”我振作精神说道，“这局棋你输了。上一回你赢了我；可这一回你得承认，占上风的是我。一个字也别讲，也别喊人——敢喊你就是死路一条。放下你手里那把刀，给我们指条出路！”

===Original===

The Marquis was on his right, I was on his left, and we were close upon him as I spoke. Still he showed no sign of fear, though he must have known the danger of his position. But his eyes glowed in his head like living coals.

You will ask why we did not rush at him? Well, if I am obliged to own it, I must﻿—the truth was, such was the power that emanated from this extraordinary man, that though we both knew the crucial moment of our enterprise had arrived, while his eyes were fixed upon us, neither of us could stir an inch. When he spoke his voice seemed to cut like a knife.

“So you think my game is up, Mr. Hatteras, do you? I’m afraid once more I must differ from you. Look behind you.”

I did so, and that glance showed me how cleverly we’d been trapped. Leaning against the door, watching us with cruel, yet smiling eyes, was our old enemy Prendergast, revolver in hand. Just behind me were two powerful Sudanese, while near the Marquis was a man looking like a Greek﻿—and a very stalwart Greek at that. Observing our discomfiture, Nikola seated himself in a big chair near the fireplace and folded his hands in the curious fashion I have before described; as he did so his black cat sprang to his shoulder and sat there watching us all. Dr. Nikola was the first to speak.

===Chinese===

侯爵在他右侧，我在他左首，说话间我们已逼到他跟前。他依旧毫无惧色，尽管他必定明白自己处境之险。只是他那双眼睛在头颅里灼灼发亮，宛如两团活炭。

你要问，我们为什么不扑上去？唉，事到如今我也只得承认——实情是，这个异人身上散发出的威慑之力如此强大，虽然我们两人都明白此番行事的紧要关头已到，可只要他那双眼盯住我们，我们谁也挪不动一寸。他一开口，那声音仿佛刀子一般割人。

“这么说，哈特拉斯先生，你以为我这局棋输了？恐怕我又得与你所见相左了。回头看看你们身后。”

我回头一看，这一眼便让我明白了我们中计中得多么巧妙。我们的老对头普伦德加斯特正倚在门边，手握左轮枪，用残忍而含笑的眼睛望着我们。我身后近处立着两个剽悍的苏丹人，侯爵身旁则站着一个貌似希腊人的汉子——而且是条非常壮硕的希腊大汉。眼见我们狼狈失措，尼古拉在壁炉边一张大椅子上落了座，双手摆成我先前描写过的那种古怪姿势交叠起来；他一坐定，那只黑猫便窜上他的肩头，蹲在那里注视着我们全体。尼古拉博士先开了口。

===Original===

“Mr. Hatteras,” he said, with devilish clearness and deliberation, “you should really know me better by this time than to think you could outwit me so easily. Is my reputation after all so small? And, while I think of it, pray let me have the pleasure of returning to you your five pound note and your letters. Your mice were perfect messengers, were they not?” As he spoke he handed me the selfsame Bank of England note I had dispatched through the pipe that very evening in payment for the file; then he shook from a box he had taken from the chimneypiece all the communications I had written imploring assistance from the outside world. To properly estimate my chagrin and astonishment would be very difficult. I could only sit and stare, first at the money and then at the letters, in blankest amazement. So we had not been rescued by the cripple after all. Was it possible that while we had been so busy arranging our escape we had in reality been all the time under the closest surveillance? If that were so, then this knowledge of our doings would account for the silence with which my attack upon the door had been received. Now we were in an even worse position than before. I looked at Beckenham, but his head was down and his right hand was picking idly at the table edge. He was evidently waiting for what was coming next. In sheer despair I turned to Nikola.

“Since you have outwitted us again, Dr. Nikola, do not play with us﻿—tell us straight out what our fate is to be.”

“If it means going back to that room again,” said Beckenham, in a voice I hardly recognised, “I would far rather die and be done with it.”

===Chinese===

“哈特拉斯先生，”他说道，口齿清晰从容得近乎恶毒，“到了这个地步，你实在应该更了解我一点，不至于以为能这样轻易地智胜我。难道我的名声终究这样微不足道？哦，趁我想起来，请容我把你的五镑纸币和信件奉还，这也算是我的荣幸。你的那些耗子，可是十全十美的信差，是不是？”说话间，他把恰恰正是那天晚上我从管子里递出去、作为锉刀酬金的那张英格兰银行纸币递还给我；随后又从壁炉台上取下一只小匣，把我写的那些恳求外界援手的信，尽数抖落出来。要恰如其分地估量我那时的懊恼与惊愕，实在很难。我只得呆坐着，先瞪着那钞票，再瞪着那些信，惊得目瞪口呆。这么说，把我们救出去的根本不是那个瘸子。难道我们忙着筹划脱身的时候，实际上自始至终都处在最严密的监视之下？倘若如此，他们对我们一举一动的了如指掌，便解释了我撞门时那一声为何无人应答。如今我们的处境比先前更加不妙。我看看贝肯汉姆，他垂着头，右手正无意识地抠着桌沿。他显然在等着下一幕开演。我在万般绝望中转向尼古拉。

“你既然又赢了我们一局，尼古拉博士，就别再戏弄我们了——痛痛快快告诉我们，我们的下场究竟如何。”

“要是还得回那间屋里去，”贝肯汉姆用一种我几乎认不出的声音说，“我宁可死了干净。”

===Original===

“Do not fear, my lord, you shall not die,” Nikola said, turning to him with a bow. “Believe me, you will live to enjoy many happier hours than those you have been compelled to spend under my roof!”

“What do you mean?”

The doctor did not answer for nearly a moment; then he took what looked to me suspiciously like a cablegram form from his pocket and carefully examined it. Having done so, he said quietly﻿—

“Gentlemen, you ask what I mean? Well, I mean this﻿—if you wish to leave this house this very minute, you are free to do so on one condition!”

“And that condition is?”

“That you allow yourselves to be blindfolded in this room and conducted by my servants to the harbour side. I must furthermore ask your words of honour that you will not seek to remove your bandages until you are given permission to do so. Do you agree to this?”

===Chinese===

“不必担心，爵爷，你死不了，”尼古拉说着，转身朝他欠身一礼。“请相信我，你还会活下去，还会享到许多比你在我屋檐下被迫消磨的时辰快活得多的光阴呢！”

“你这是什么意思？”

博士约有片刻没有作答；随后他从口袋里掏出一样在我看来形迹十分可疑、像是越洋电报纸的东西，仔细端详了一番。看罢，他平静地说——

“两位先生，你们问我是什么意思？好，我的意思是——倘若你们愿意此刻立刻离开这座宅子，尽可以自便，只消答应一个条件！”

“什么条件？”

“就是你们在这间屋里让我的仆人蒙上眼睛，由他们押送到港口边。此外我还须请二位以名誉担保：未得许可，绝不设法解下蒙眼的布带。你们可同意？”

===Original===

Needless to say we both signified our assent.

This free permission to leave the house was a second surprise, and one for which we were totally unprepared.

“Then let it be so. Believe me, my lord Marquis, and you, Mr. Hatteras, it is with the utmost pleasure I restore your liberty to you again!”

He made a sign to Prendergast, who instantly stepped forward. But I had something to say before we were removed.

“One word first, Dr. Nikola. You have﻿—”

===Chinese===

不必说，我们两人都表示了同意。

这放我们离宅的慷慨许诺，是第二重意外，我们全然没有料到。

“那就这么办吧。请相信我，侯爵爵爷，还有你，哈特拉斯先生，能把自由重新归还二位，我实在感到无上的欣悦！”

他朝普伦德加斯特打了个手势，那人立刻上前。但在我们被带走之前，我还有话要说。

“先容我说一句，尼古拉博士。你已经——”

===Original===

“Mr. Hatteras, if you will be guided by me, you will keep a silent tongue in your head. Let well alone. Take warning by the proverb, and beware how you disturb a sleeping dog. Why I have acted as I have done towards you, you may some day learn; in the meantime rest assured it was from no idle motive. Now take me at my word, and go while you have the chance. I may change my mind in a moment, and then﻿—”

He stopped and did not say any more. At a sign, Prendergast clapped a thick bandage over my eyes, while another man did the same for Beckenham; a man on either side of me took my arms, and next moment we had passed out of the room, and before I could have counted fifty were in the cool air of the open street.

How long we were walking, after leaving the house, I could not say, but at last our escort called a halt. Prendergast was evidently in command, for he said﻿—

“Gentlemen, before we leave you, you will renew your words of honour not to remove your bandages for five full minutes?”

We complied with his request, and instantly our arms were released; a moment later we heard our captors leaving us. The minutes went slowly by. Presently Beckenham said﻿—

===Chinese===

“哈特拉斯先生，你若肯听我一句，就把舌头牢牢管住。见好就收。记住那句谚语，莫去惊动睡着的狗。我为什么这样对待你，也许有朝一日你会明白；眼下你只管放心，我绝非出于无聊的兴致。趁我现在说话算数，得机会就走。我说不定片刻之间便改变主意，到那时——”

他戛然而止，没有再往下说。随着一个手势，普伦德加斯特把一条厚布带蒙上我的双眼，另一个人如法炮制了贝肯汉姆；我两侧各有一人架住我的手臂，转瞬间我们已出了那间屋子，还不等我数到五十，就已站在露天街道凉爽的空气里了。

离开那座宅子后我们走了多久，我说不上来，但押送我们的人终于叫了停。领头的显然是普伦德加斯特，只听他说道——

“两位先生，我们分手之前，请再以名誉担保一次：整整五分钟之内，绝不摘下蒙眼布？”

我们照他的要求担保了，两条手臂随即获释；片刻之后，我们听见掳我们的人离去了。时间一分一分慢吞吞地挨过去。过了一会儿，贝肯汉姆开口——

===Original===

“How long do you think we’ve been standing here?”

“Nearly the stipulated time, I should fancy,” I answered. “However, we’d better give them a little longer, to avoid any chance of mistake.”

Again a silence fell on us. Then I tore off my bandage, to find Beckenham doing the same.

“They’re gone, and we’re free again,” he cried. “Hurrah!”

We shook hands warmly on our escape, and having done so looked about us. A ship’s bell out in the stream chimed half an hour after midnight, and a precious dark night it was. A number of vessels were to be seen, and from the noise that came from them it was evident they were busy coaling.

===Chinese===

“你估摸咱们在这儿站了多久？”

“我估摸着，差不多到约定的时限了，”我答道，“不过，还是多给他们留一点工夫稳妥，免得出什么差错。”

我们又是一阵沉默。随后我一把扯下蒙眼布，发现贝肯汉姆也正扯下他的。

“他们走了，咱们又自由了！”他喊道，“好哇！”

我们为脱险热烈握手，握罢环顾四周。锚地远处一艘船上的钟敲响了，报的是午夜过后半个钟头，这夜色黑得着实可以。看得见不少船只，从那边传来的动静听得出，它们正忙着上煤。

===Original===

“What’s to be done now?” asked Beckenham.

“Find an hotel, I think,” I answered; “get a good night’s rest, and first thing in the morning hunt up our consul and the steamship authorities.”

“Come along, then. Let’s look for a place. I noticed one that should suit us close to where we came ashore that day.”

Five minutes’ walking brought us to the house we sought. The proprietor was not very fastidious, and whatever he may have thought of our appearances he took us in without demur. A bath and a good meal followed, and then after a thorough overhauling of all the details connected with our imprisonment we turned into bed, resolved to thrash it out upon the morrow.

Next morning, true to our arrangement, as soon as breakfast was over, I set off for the steamship company’s office, leaving the Marquis behind me at the hotel for reasons which had begun to commend themselves to me, and which will be quite apparent to you.

===Chinese===

“现在怎么办？”贝肯汉姆问。

“我看先找家旅馆，”我答道，“好好睡上一夜，明儿一早头一件事，就去寻我们的领事和轮船公司。”

“那就走吧。找个地方去。那天我们上岸的地方附近，我记得有一家挺合适。”

走了五分钟，我们便到了要找的那家店。店主为人不甚讲究，无论他看我们这副模样作何感想，反正二话没说便收留了我们。接着洗了个澡，饱餐了一顿，然后把我们被囚前后的种种细节从头到尾细细梳理了一遍，才上床就寝，打定主意明日再彻查个水落石出。

第二天早晨，我们依计而行，早膳一毕，我便动身去轮船公司的办事处，把侯爵留在旅馆里——这样做的缘故，我已渐渐觉得顺理成章，而且你们也自会一目了然。

===Original===

I found the *Saratoga*’s agent hard at work in his private office. He was a tall, thin man, slightly bald, wearing a pair of heavy gold pincenez, and very slow and deliberate in his speech.

“I beg your pardon,” he began, when I had taken possession of his proffered chair, “but did I understand my clerk to say that your name was Hatteras?”

“That is my name,” I answered. “I was a passenger in your boat the *Saratoga* for Australia three weeks ago, but had the misfortune to be left behind when she sailed.”

“Ah! I remember the circumstances thoroughly,” he said. “The young Marquis of Beckenham went ashore with you, I think, and came within an ace of being also left behind.”

“Within an ace!” I cried; “but he was left behind.”

===Chinese===

我在他的私人办公室里找到了*「萨拉托加」号*的代理人，他正埋头办公。那是个瘦高个儿，头顶微秃，鼻梁上架着一副厚重的金质夹鼻眼镜，说话慢条斯理、字斟句酌。

“恕我冒昧，”我在他推过来的椅子上落座后，他开口道，“我好像听我的职员说，您名叫哈特拉斯？”

“正是我的名字，”我答道，“三个星期前，我搭乘贵公司的*「萨拉托加」号*去澳大利亚，不幸在开船时被落下了。”

“啊！那桩事我记得清清楚楚，”他说，“年轻的贝肯汉姆侯爵当时跟您一同上了岸，我想，也差点儿被落下。”

“岂止是差点儿！”我喊道，“他是实实在在被落下了。”

===Original===

“No, no! there you are mistaken,” was the astounding reply; “he would have been left behind had not his tutor and I gone ashore at the last moment to look for him and found him wandering about on the outskirts of Arab Town. I don’t remember ever to have seen a man more angry than the tutor was, and no wonder, for they only just got out to the boat again as the gangway was being hauled aboard.”

“Then you mean to tell me that the Marquis went on to Australia after all!” I cried. “And pray how did this interesting young gentleman explain the fact of his losing sight of me?”

“He lost you in a crowd, he said,” the agent continued. “It was a most extraordinary business altogether.”

It certainly was, and even more extraordinary than he imagined. I could hardly believe my ears. The world seemed to be turned upside down. I was so bewildered that I stumbled out a few lame enquiries about the next boat sailing for Australia, and what would be done with my baggage on its arrival at the other end, and then made my way as best I could out of the office.

===Chinese===

“不，不！这您可弄错了，”他的回答令人骇然，“要不是他的家庭教师和我在最后一刻上岸寻他、在阿拉伯城区边上找到正乱转的他，他才会被落下呢。我从没见过比那位教师更恼火的人，这也难怪，他们赶回船上时，跳板正被拉上船去，简直是千钧一发。”

“那么你是说，侯爵到底还是去了澳大利亚！”我叫道，“那么请问，这位有趣的年轻绅士对于跟丢了我这件事，又是怎么交代的？”

“他说，他在人群中把您挤散了，”代理人接着说，“整桩事实在透着蹊跷。”

何止蹊跷，比他想象的还要离奇得多。我几乎不敢相信自己的耳朵。整个世界仿佛翻了个个儿。我昏头涨脑，结结巴巴问了几个下一班开往澳大利亚的船期、以及我的行李到了那头会怎么处置之类的敷衍问题，便尽力定了定神，走出了办事处。

===Original===

Hastening back to the hotel, I told my story from beginning to end to my astonished companion, who sat on his bed listening open-mouthed. When I had finished he said feebly﻿—

“But what does it all mean? Tell me that! What does it mean?”

“It means,” I answered, “that our notion about Nikola’s abducting us in order to blackmail your father was altogether wrong, and, if you ask me, I should say not half picturesque enough. No, no! this mystery is a bigger one by a hundred times than even we expected, and there are more men in it than those we have yet seen. It remains with you to say whether you will assist in the attempt to unravel it or not.”

“What do you mean by saying it remains with me? Do I understand that you intend following it up?”

“Of course I do. Nikola and Baxter between them have completely done me﻿—now I’m going to do my best to do them. By Jove!”

===Chinese===

我赶回旅馆，把事情原原本本讲给我那位惊愕的同伴听，他坐在床沿，张着嘴听完了。听完他才有气无力地说——

“可这一切到底是什么意思？你告诉我！到底是什么意思？”

“这意思是，”我答道，“我们原先猜尼古拉绑票是为了敲诈你父亲，这个想法完全错了；你要问我的看法，依我说，这猜想连实情离奇程度的一半都够不上。不，不！这个谜比我们预想的还要大上百倍，参与其中的人，也比我们眼下见过的要多。要不要助我一臂之力去解开它，就看你的一句话了。”

“你说看你的一句话，这是什么意思？我是否可以理解为，你打算追查到底？”

“当然要追。尼古拉和巴克斯特联手把我整惨了——如今我要竭尽全力回敬他们。天哪！”

===Original===

“What is it now?”

“I see it all as plain as a pikestaff. I understand exactly now why Baxter came for you, why he telegraphed that the train was laid, why I was drugged in Plymouth, why you were seasick between Naples and this place, and why we were both kidnapped so mysteriously!”

“Then explain, for mercy’s sake!”

“I will. See here. In the first place, remember your father’s peculiar education of yourself. If you consider that, you will see that you are the only young nobleman of high rank whose face is not well known to his brother peers. That being so, Nikola wants to procure you for some purpose of his own in Australia. Your father advertises for a tutor; he sends one of his agents﻿—Baxter﻿—to secure the position. Baxter, at Nikola’s instruction, puts into your head a desire for travel. You pester your father for the necessary permission. Just as this is granted I come upon the scene. Baxter suspects me. He telegraphs to Nikola ‘The train is laid,’ which means that he has begun to sow the seeds of a desire for travel, when a third party steps in﻿—in other words, I am the new danger that has arisen. He arranges your sailing, and all promises to go well. Then Dr. Nikola finds out I intend going in the same boat. He tries to prevent me; and I﻿—by Jove! I see another thing. Why did Baxter suggest that you should cross the continent and join the boat at Naples? Why, simply because if you started from Plymouth you would soon have got over your sickness, if you had ever been ill at all, and in that case the passengers would have become thoroughly familiar with your face by the time you reached Port Said. That would never have done, so he takes you to Naples, drugs you next morning﻿—for you must remember you were ill after the coffee he gave you﻿—and by that means keeps you ill and confined to your cabin throughout the entire passage to Port Said. Then he persuades you to go ashore with me. You do so, with what result you know. Presently he begins to bewail your non-return, invites the agent to help in the search. They set off, and eventually find you near the Arab quarter. You must remember that neither the agent, the captain, nor the passengers have seen you, save at night, so the substitute, who is certain to have been well chosen and schooled for the part he is to play, is not detected. Then the boat goes on her way, while we are left behind languishing in durance vile.”

===Chinese===

“你又想到什么了？”

“这下全明白了，明明白白，一清二楚。现在我完全懂了：巴克斯特为什么去找你，他为什么打电报说引线已经铺好，我为什么在普利茅斯被下了药，你为什么在那不勒斯到此地之间一路晕船，还有我们两人为什么被这么神秘地绑了票！”

“那看在老天的份上，快说说！”

“我这就说。你听着。首先，想想你父亲对你那套与众不同的教养。你只要琢磨这一点就会发现，你是唯一一位相貌不为同侪贵族熟识的年少显贵。既然如此，尼古拉要把你弄到手，为的是他在澳大利亚的某个图谋。你父亲登报招家庭教师，他便派出手下一个爪牙——巴克斯特——去谋得这个位子。巴克斯特奉尼古拉之命，往你脑袋里灌游历的念想。你缠着父亲讨必要的许可。许可刚一到手，我便登场了。巴克斯特起了疑心，便致电尼古拉‘引线已经铺好’，意思是播撒游历念想的种子的活儿已经开局，却有第三者插了进来——换句话说，我便是新冒出来的危险。他安排你登船的事宜，一切眼看顺顺当当。随后尼古拉博士发觉我打算搭同一条船，便设法阻挠；而我——天哪！我又想通了一节。巴克斯特为什么建议你横穿大陆、到那不勒斯再上船？嘿，道理很简单：你若是从普利茅斯上船，晕船也早该缓过来了——倘若你真的晕过的话——那样一来，等你到塞得港时，船上旅客早把你的脸看熟了。那是万万使不得的，于是他带你去那不勒斯，第二天一早给你下药——你总记得喝过他给的咖啡便不舒服吧——借此让你一路病恹恹地闷在舱里，直抵塞得港。然后他怂恿你跟我一同上岸。你去了，结果如何你也知道了。转眼他便开始哀叹你迟迟不归，请代理人帮忙寻找。他们出发，最终在阿拉伯城区附近‘找到’了你。你务必记住，无论代理人、船长还是旅客，都没在白天见过你，所以那个替身——必定是精心挑选、又为要演的戏码悉心调教过的——才没有被识破。随后船继续航程，我们两个却被撇在身后，在卑污的囹圄中憔悴度日。”

===Original===

“Do you really think those are the facts of the case?”

“Upon my word, I do!”

“Then what do you advise me to do? Remember, Baxter has letters to the different Governors from my father.”

“I know what I should do myself!”

“Go to the consul and get him to warn the authorities in Australia, I suppose?”

===Chinese===

“你当真觉得实情就是如此？”

“我可以拿名誉担保，正是如此！”

“那么你劝我怎么办？别忘了，巴克斯特手里有我父亲写给各位总督的引荐信。”

“要是我自己，可知道该怎么办！”

“我猜是——去找领事，让他通知澳大利亚当局，是不是？”

===Original===

“No. That would do little or no good﻿—remember, they’ve three weeks’ start of us.”

“Then what shall we do? I’m in your hands entirely, and whatever you advise I promise you I’ll do.”

“If I were you I should doff my title, take another name, and set sail with me for Australia. Once there, we’ll put up in some quiet place and set ourselves to unmask these rascals and to defeat their little game, whatever it may be. Are you prepared for so much excitement as that?”

“Of course I am. Come what may, I’ll go with you, and there’s my hand on it.”

“Then we’ll catch the next boat﻿—not a mail-steamer﻿—that sails for an Australian port, and once ashore there we’ll set the ball a-rolling with a vengeance.”

===Chinese===

“不。那顶多聊胜于无——别忘了，他们抢先了我们三个星期。”

“那我们该怎么办？我全凭你做主，你无论出什么主意，我保证照办。”

“我要是你，就摘下头衔，换个名字，跟我扬帆去澳大利亚。一到那儿，我们寻个清静处住下，一门心思揭穿这伙恶棍的假面，管他们葫芦里卖的什么药，都叫它砸锅。这么刺激的差事，你可准备好了？”

“当然准备好了。不管出什么事，我都跟你去，一言为定，握个手。”

“那我们就赶下一班开往澳大利亚港口的船——不坐邮轮——一上岸，就痛痛快快大干一场。”

===Original===

“That scoundrel Baxter! I’m not vindictive as a rule, but I feel I should like to punish him.”

“Well, if they’ve not flown by the time we reach Australia, you’ll probably be able to gratify your wish. It’s Nikola, however, I want.”

Beckenham shuddered as I mentioned the Doctor’s name. So to change the subject I said﻿—

“I’m thinking of taking a little walk. Would you care to accompany me?”

“Where are you going?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“巴克斯特那个恶棍！我平日并不记仇，可这回我觉得真想收拾收拾他。”

“嗯，等我们到澳大利亚时，他们若还没远走高飞，你这心愿多半能了。不过，我要的是尼古拉。”

我一提到博士的名字，贝肯汉姆便打了个寒噤。为换个话头，我说——

“我想出去走走。你愿不愿意陪我？”

“你要去哪儿？”他问。

===Original===

“I’m going to try and find the house where we were shut up,” I answered. “I want to be able to locate it for future reference, if necessary.”

“Is it safe to go near it, do you think?”

“In broad daylight, yes! But, just to make sure, we’ll buy a couple of revolvers on the way. And, what’s more, if it becomes necessary we’ll use them.”

“Come along, then.”

With that we left our hotel and set off in the direction of the Casino, stopping, however, on the way to make the purchases above referred to.

===Chinese===

“我想去找找囚禁我们的那座宅子，”我答道，“得能把它的位置认下来，往后万一用得着。”

“你看，靠近那儿安全吗？”

“光天化日之下，不怕！不过保险起见，路上我们买两把左轮枪。而且，真到万不得已，我们就用它们。”

“那就走吧。”

说罢我们出了旅馆，朝赌场方向出发，不过路上照方才说的，先停下来置办了那两件家伙。

===Original===

On arrival at the place we sought, we halted and looked about us. I pointed to a street on our right.

“That was the way we came from the mosque,” I said. Then, pointing to a narrow alley way almost opposite where we stood, I continued, “And that was where I saw Nikola standing watching us. Now when we came out of this building we turned to our left hand, and, if I mistake not, went off in that direction. I think, if you’ve no objection, we’ll go that way now.”

We accordingly set off at a good pace, and after a while arrived at the spot where the guide had caught us up. It looked a miserably dirty neighbourhood in the bright sunlight. Beckenham gazed about him thoughtfully, and finally said﻿—

“Now we turn to our right, I think.”

===Chinese===

到了我们要找的地方，我们停下脚步，环顾四周。我指了指右手边的一条街。

“我们那天就是从清真寺那边来的，”我说。接着，我指着几乎正对我们所站之处的一条窄巷，续道，“那就是我看见尼古拉站着窥伺我们的地方。我们出这座楼时朝左手拐，如果我没记错，便是往那个方向去的。你若不反对，我们这就走那条路。”

于是我们快步出发，走了一阵，到了向导当年赶上我们的那个地点。在朗朗日光下，那一带显得龌龊不堪、破败至极。贝肯汉姆若有所思地四下张望，终于开口——

“我想，这回该往右手拐了。”

===Original===

“Quite so. Come along!”

We passed down one thoroughfare and up another, and at last reached the spot where I had commented on the signboards, and where we had been garrotted. Surely the house must be near at hand now? But though we hunted high and low, up one street and down another, not a single trace of any building, answering the description of the one we wanted, could we discover. At last, after nearly an hour’s search, we were obliged to give it up, and return to our hotel, unsuccessful.

As we finished lunch a large steamer made her appearance in the harbour, and brought up opposite the town. When we questioned our landlord, who was an authority on the subject, he informed us that she was the S.S. *Pescadore*, of Hull, bound to Melbourne.

Hearing this we immediately chartered a boat, pulled off to her, and interviewed the captain. As good luck would have it, he had room for a couple of passengers. We therefore paid the passage money, went ashore again and provided ourselves with a few necessaries, rejoined her, and shortly before nightfall steamed into the Canal. Port Said was a thing of the past. Our eventful journey was resumed﻿—what was the end of it all to be?

===Chinese===

“正是。走吧！”

我们穿过一条大街，又走过另一条，终于到了我当初议论招牌、也是我们遭人勒脖打闷棍的地方。那座宅子想必就在近前了吧？可我们里里外外、一条街一条街地搜了个遍，却找不到半点与我们要找的那座宅子对得上号的建筑踪影。折腾了将近一个钟头，我们只得罢手，悻悻然回旅馆去。

我们午饭刚吃完，一艘大轮船驶进港来，在城对面的水面抛了锚。我们请教店主——他在这方面是个权威——他告诉我们，那是赫尔港的*「佩斯卡多尔」号*（Pescadore）汽轮，开往墨尔本。

一听这话，我们立刻雇了条小船，划到大船跟前，求见船长。运气不错，他那儿还容得下两名乘客。于是我们付了船资，再上岸置办了些必需品，回到船上，日落前不久便驶进了运河。塞得港从此成了过往旧事。我们这段变故迭起的旅程重新开始了——它的结局究竟会怎样呢？

## Prologue Dr. Nikola / 序幕 帝国餐厅的晚宴

===Original===
The manager of the new Imperial Restaurant on the Thames Embankment went into his luxurious private office and shut the door. Having done so, he first scratched his chin reflectively, and then took a letter from the drawer in which it had reposed for more than two months and perused it carefully. Though he was not aware of it, this was the thirtieth time he had read it since breakfast that morning. And yet he was not a whit nearer understanding it than he had been at the beginning. He turned it over and scrutinised the back, where not a sign of writing was to be seen; he held it up to the window, as if he might hope to discover something from the watermark; but there was evidently nothing in either of these places of a nature calculated to set his troubled mind at rest. Then, though he had a clock upon his mantelpiece in good working order, he took a magnificent repeater watch from his waistcoat pocket and glanced at the dial; the hands stood at half-past seven. He immediately threw the letter on the table, and as he did so his anxiety found relief in words.
“It’s really the most extraordinary affair I ever had to do with,” he remarked to the placid face of the clock above mentioned. “And as I’ve been in the business just three-and-thirty years at eleven a.m. next Monday morning, I ought to know something about it. I only hope I’ve done right, that’s all.”
As he spoke, the chief bookkeeper, who had the treble advantage of being tall, pretty, and just eight-and-twenty years of age, entered the room. She noticed the open letter and the look upon her chief’s face, and her curiosity was proportionately excited.
“You seem worried, Mr. McPherson,” she said tenderly, as she put down the papers she had brought in for his signature.
“You have just hit it, Miss O’Sullivan,” he answered, pushing them farther on to the table. “I am worried about many things, but particularly about this letter.”
===Chinese===
泰晤士河堤（Thames Embankment）上新开张的帝国餐厅（Imperial Restaurant），其经理走进自己那间豪华的私人办公室，随手把门关上。关好之后，他先若有所思地挠了挠下巴，然后从抽屉里取出一封信——它已在里面安卧了两个多月——仔仔细细地读了起来。他自己虽未察觉，这已是那天早餐以来他第三十次读这封信了。然而，他对这封信的理解，并不比开初多分毫。他把信翻过来，细细察看背面，那里见不到一丝笔迹；又把信举到窗口，仿佛指望从水印里发现点什么；可这两处显然都没有任何能让他那颗忐忑之心安定下来的东西。随后，尽管壁炉架上明明立着一座走时良好的时钟，他却从马甲口袋里掏出一块华贵的打簧表，瞥了一眼表盘；指针停在七点半。他立刻把信摔在桌上，焦虑也随之化作言语，得到了宣泄。
“这真是我平生经手过的最离奇的一件事，”他对着上文提到的那座神色安详的时钟说道。“下周一上午十一点，我干这一行就整三十三年了，这种事我总该懂一点。我只盼自己做对了，仅此而已。”
他说话的当口，会计主任走了进来——她身兼三重优势：个子高挑，模样标致，芳龄又恰好二十八岁。她注意到那封摊开的信和上司脸上的神色，好奇心便也随之被勾了起来。
“您像是遇上了烦心事，麦克弗森先生（Mr. McPherson），”她一面把带来待签的文件放下，一面温柔地说。
“您一语中的，奥沙利文小姐（Miss O'Sullivan），”他答道，把文件往桌子那头推了推。“让我烦心的事多得很，可最烦的就是这封信。”

===Original===
He handed the epistle to her, and she, being desirous of impressing him with her business capabilities, read it with ostentatious care. But it was noticeable that when she reached the signature she too turned back to the beginning, and then deliberately read it over again. The manager rose, crossed to the mantelpiece, and rang for the head waiter. Having relieved his feelings in this way, he seated himself again at his writing-table, put on his glasses, and stared at his companion, while waiting for her to speak.
“It’s very funny,” she said at length, seeing that she was expected to say something. “Very funny indeed!”
“It’s the most extraordinary communication I have ever received,” he replied with conviction. “You see it is written from Cuyaba, Brazil. The date is three months ago to a day. Now I have taken the trouble to find out where and what Cuyaba is.”
He made this confession with an air of conscious pride, and having done so, laid himself back in his chair, stuck his thumbs into the armholes of his waistcoat, and looked at his fair subordinate for approval.
Nor was he destined to be disappointed. He was a bachelor in possession of a snug income, and she, besides being a pretty woman, was a lady with a keen eye to the main chance.
===Chinese===
他把信递给她。她一心想让他见识自己的办事才干，便煞有介事地细读起来。不过可以注意到，读到署名处时，她也把信翻回开头，又从容不迫地从头读了一遍。经理站起身，走到壁炉架前，按铃唤领班侍者。如此这般宣泄过情绪之后，他重新在写字台前坐下，戴上眼镜，盯着女伴，等她开口。
“真是古怪，”她见对方指望自己说点什么，终于开口道。“真是古怪透顶！”
“这是我平生收到的最不寻常的一封信，”他深信不疑地答道。“您瞧，信是从巴西的库亚巴（Cuyaba）寄出的。落款日期距今不多不少，恰好三个月。我特意费了番功夫，查明了库亚巴是个什么地方。”
他带着一股自知高明的得意神气说出了这番话，说完便往椅背上一靠，两根大拇指插进马甲袖孔，望着他那位美丽的下属，等她称许。
他也不会失望。他是个收入殷实的单身汉；而她呢，除了是个漂亮女人之外，还是位一双眼睛时刻盯着切身利益的小姐。

===Original===
“And where is Cuyaba?” she asked humbly.
“Cuyaba,” he replied, rolling his tongue with considerable relish round his unconscious mispronunciation of the name, “is a town almost on the western or Bolivian border of Brazil. It is of moderate size, is situated on the banks of the river Cuyaba, and is considerably connected with the famous Brazilian Diamond Fields.”
“And does the writer of this letter live there?”
“I cannot say. He writes from there﻿—that is enough for us.”
“And he orders dinner for four﻿—here, in a private room overlooking the river, three months ahead﻿—punctually at eight o’clock, gives you a list of the things he wants, and even arranges the decoration of the table. Says he has never seen either of his three friends before; that one of them hails from (here she consulted the letter again) Hangchow, another from Bloemfontein, while the third resides, at present, in England. Each one is to present an ordinary visiting card with a red dot on it to the porter in the hall, and to be shown to the room at once. I don’t understand it at all.”
===Chinese===
“那么库亚巴在哪儿呢？”她虚心求教。
“库亚巴，”他答道，舌头津津有味地卷动着那个自己浑然不觉念错的地名，“是巴西西部、也就是靠近玻利维亚边境的一座城镇。规模中等，坐落在库亚巴河畔，与著名的巴西钻石矿区颇有渊源。”
“那么写信人就住在那儿吗？”
“这我说不上。信是从那儿寄出的——对我们来说，这就够了。”
“而且他订的是四人份的晚餐——就在这儿，一间俯瞰河面的包间，提前三个月——八点整准时入席，还开列了他想要的菜品清单，甚至连餐桌的装点都一一安排妥当。信里说，他从未见过那三位朋友中的任何一位；其中一位来自（说到这里她又看了一眼信）杭州，另一位来自布隆方丹（Bloemfontein），第三位眼下住在英格兰。每位来客都须向门厅的门房递上一张带红点的普通名片，随即被立即引到房间。我压根儿弄不明白。”

===Original===
The manager paused for a moment, and then said deliberately﻿—
“Hangchow is in China, Bloemfontein is in South Africa.”
“What a wonderful man you are, to be sure, Mr. McPherson! I never can think how you manage to carry so much in your head.”
There spoke the true woman. And it was a move in the right direction, for the manager was susceptible to her gentle influence, as she had occasion to know.
At this juncture the head waiter appeared upon the scene, and took up a position just inside the doorway, as if he were afraid of injuring the carpet by coming further.
===Chinese===
经理顿了片刻，然后一字一顿地说道——
“杭州在中国，布隆方丹在南非。”
“您可真是个了不起的人，麦克弗森先生！我怎么也想不明白，您脑子里怎么能装下这么多东西。”
这才是一个地地道道的女人说的话。而且这一步走得对路，因为这位经理吃她温言软语的那一套——这一点，她是有机会领教过的。
正当此时，领班侍者登场了。他在门口内侧站定，仿佛生怕再往里走一步就会踩坏地毯似的。

===Original===
“Is No 22 ready, Williams?”
“Quite ready, sir. The wine is on the ice, and cook tells me he’ll be ready to dish punctual to the moment.”
“The letter says, ‘no electric light; candles with red shades.’ Have you put on those shades I got this morning?”
“Just seen it done this very minute, sir.”
“And let me see, there was one other thing.” He took the letter from the chief bookkeeper’s hand and glanced at it.
“Ah, yes, a porcelain saucer, and a small jug of new milk upon the mantelpiece. An extraordinary request, but has it been attended to?”
===Chinese===
“ 22 号包间备好了吗，威廉斯（Williams）？”
“全都备好了，先生。酒正冰着，厨师长告诉我，到时他保准一秒不差地准时出菜。”
“信上写着：‘不用电灯；蜡烛须带红色灯罩。’我今天早上弄来的那些灯罩，你给他们罩上了吗？”
“就在这一分钟还亲眼看着弄妥了，先生。”
“还有，让我想想，另外还有一件事。”他从会计主任手里接过信，扫了一眼。
“啊，对了：壁炉架上放一只瓷碟和一小罐鲜牛奶。这要求是够离奇的，不过照办了吗？”

===Original===
“I put it there myself, sir.”
“Who wait?”
“Jones, Edmunds, Brooks, and Tomkins.”
“Very good. Then I think that will do. Stay! You had better tell the hall porter to look out for three gentlemen presenting plain visiting cards with a little red spot on them. Let Brooks wait in the hall, and when they arrive tell him to show them straight up to the room.”
“It shall be done, sir.”
The head waiter left the room, and the manager stretched himself in his chair, yawned by way of showing his importance, and then said solemnly﻿—
===Chinese===
“是我亲手放上去的，先生。”
“派谁侍应？”
“琼斯（Jones）、埃德蒙兹（Edmunds）、布鲁克斯（Brooks）和汤普金斯（Tomkins）。”
“很好。我想这样就齐了。慢着！你最好吩咐门房，让他留神三位递上带小红点素名片的先生。叫布鲁克斯守在门厅，客人一到，就让他们径直上楼到包间去。”
“这就去办，先生。”
领班侍者退出房间。经理在椅子里舒展身子，打了个哈欠以显威仪，然后郑重其事地说——

===Original===
“I don’t believe they’ll any of them turn up; but if they do, this Dr. Nikola, whoever he may be, won’t be able to find fault with my arrangements.”
Then, leaving the dusty high road of business, he and his companion wandered in the shady bridle-paths of love to the end that when the chief bookkeeper returned to her own department she had forgotten the strange dinner party about to take place upstairs, and was busily engaged upon a calculation as to how she would look in white satin and orange blossoms, and, that settled, fell to wondering whether it was true, as Miss Joyce, a subordinate, had been heard to declare, that the manager had once shown himself partial to a certain widow with reputed savings and a share in an extensive egg and dairy business.
At ten minutes to eight precisely a hansom drew up at the steps of the hotel. As soon as it stopped, an undersized gentleman, with a clean-shaven countenance, a canonical corporation, and bow legs, dressed in a decidedly clerical garb, alighted. He paid and discharged his cabman, and then took from his ticket pocket an ordinary white visiting card, which he presented to the gold-laced individual who had opened the apron. The latter, having noted the red spot, called a waiter, and the reverend gentleman was immediately escorted upstairs.
Hardly had the attendant time to return to his station in the hall, before a second cab made its appearance, closely followed by a third. Out of the second jumped a tall, active, well-built man of about thirty years of age. He was dressed in evening dress of the latest fashion, and to conceal it from the vulgar gaze, wore a large Inverness cape of heavy texture. He also in his turn handed a white card to the porter, and, having done so, proceeded into the hall, followed by the occupant of the last cab, who had closely copied his example. This individual was also in evening dress, but it was of a different stamp. It was old-fashioned and had seen much use. The wearer, too, was taller than the ordinary run of men, while it was noticeable that his hair was snow-white, and that his face was deeply pitted with smallpox. After disposing of their hats and coats in an anteroom, they reached room No. 22, where they found the gentleman in clerical costume pacing impatiently up and down.
Left alone, the tallest of the trio, who for want of a better title we may call the Best Dressed Man, took out his watch, and having glanced at it, looked at his companions.
===Chinese===
“我看他们一个也不会露面；不过真要是来了，这位尼古拉博士（Dr. Nikola）——不管他究竟是什么来头——也休想在我的安排上挑出半点毛病。”
随后，两人撇下正经生意的尘土大路，双双踱进了爱情的绿荫幽径。结果是，会计主任回到自己的部门时，已经把楼上即将举行的那场古怪晚宴忘了个干净，正专心致志地盘算起自己穿上白缎礼服、戴上香橙花会是何等模样；这桩心事有了定论，她又转而琢磨起一桩传闻——据说她手下一个叫乔伊斯小姐（Miss Joyce）的下属曾放话，说经理先生从前对某位寡妇颇有意趣，而那位寡妇传闻攒有一笔积蓄，还在一家规模不小的禽蛋乳品行里占着一股——不知是真是假。
八点差十分，一分不差，一辆双轮马车停在了饭店台阶前。车刚停稳，一位身材矮小的先生便下了车。他面庞剃得精光，肚子浑圆，一副教士的经典体格，双腿罗圈，一身地道的教士装束。他付了车钱，打发走车夫，然后从票袋里取出一张普通的白色名片，呈给那位替他掀开挡帘、身着金绦号衣的门房。后者看清了那个红点，唤来一名侍者，这位可敬的教士立刻被恭迎上楼。
那门房还没来得及回到门厅的岗位上，第二辆马车便出现了，紧接着又是第三辆。从第二辆车上跳下一名男子，三十岁上下，身材高大，行动敏捷，体格健壮。他穿着款式最新的晚礼服，为了不让它落入俗人的眼帘，外面还罩了一件质地厚重的宽大因弗内斯斗篷（Inverness cape）。他也照样向门房递了一张白名片，随后举步走进大厅；走在他后面的是最后一辆车上的乘客，此人把他的一举一动依样照搬。这位也身着晚礼服，但路数迥异：款式陈旧，久经穿用。这位穿者同样比寻常人高出许多，而且惹眼的是，他一头白发如雪，脸上布满天花留下的深深麻点。两人在前厅存置好帽子大衣，便到了 22 号包间，只见那位教士装束的先生正在屋里焦躁地踱来踱去。
只剩他们三人之后，三人中最高的那位——姑且因找不到更妥帖的称呼，我们不妨称他“衣着最讲究的人”——掏出怀表，看了一眼，然后望向两位同伴。

===Original===
“Gentlemen,” he said, with a slight American accent, “it is three minutes to eight o’clock. My name is Eastover!”
“I’m glad to hear it, for I’m most uncommonly hungry,” said the next tallest, whom I have already described as being so marked by disease. “My name is Prendergast!”
“We only wait for our friend and host,” remarked the clerical gentleman, as if he felt he ought to take a share in the conversation, and then, as if an afterthought had struck him, he continued, “My name is Baxter!”
They shook hands all round with marked cordiality, seated themselves again, and took it in turns to examine the clock.
“Have you ever had the pleasure of meeting our host before?” asked Mr. Baxter of Mr. Prendergast.
“Never,” replied that gentleman, with a shake of his head. “Perhaps Mr. Eastover has been more fortunate?”
===Chinese===
“诸位，”他带着轻微的美国口音说道，“现在是八点差三分。我叫伊斯特弗（Eastover）！”
“听您这么说我很高兴，因为我饿得实在不轻，”第二高的那位说——就是我方才描绘过的、被病痕深深刻损的那位。“我叫普伦德加斯特（Prendergast）！”
“我们只是在等我们的朋友兼主人，”教士装束的先生搭话说，仿佛觉得自己也该在谈话里凑一份，接着又像猛然想起什么似的补了一句，“我叫巴克斯特（Baxter）！”
三人热络地轮番握手，重新落座，又轮流端详起那座时钟来。
“您以前可有幸见过咱们这位主人？”巴克斯特先生问普伦德加斯特先生。
“从未见过，”那位先生摇摇头答道。“或许伊斯特弗先生的运气好些？”

===Original===
“Not I,” was the brief rejoinder. “I’ve had to do with him off and on for longer than I care to reckon, but I’ve never set eyes on him up to date.”
“And where may he have been the first time you heard from him?”
“In Nashville, Tennessee,” said Eastover. “After that, Tahupapa, New Zealand; after that, Papeete, in the Society Islands; then Peking, China. And you?”
“First time, Brussels; second, Monte Video; third, Mandalay, and then the Gold Coast, Africa. It’s your turn, Mr. Baxter.”
The clergyman glanced at the timepiece. It was exactly eight o’clock.
“First time, Cabul, Afghanistan; second, Nijni Novgorod, Russia; third, Wilcannia, Darling River, Australia; fourth, Valparaiso, Chile; fifth, Nagasaki, Japan.”
===Chinese===
“我可没那运气，”回答得简短干脆。“我跟他断断续续打交道的时间长得我都懒得去算，可直到今天，我连他一面都没见过。”
“那您头一回得到他的音讯时，是在什么地方？”
“在田纳西州的纳什维尔（Nashville），”伊斯特弗说。“之后是新西兰的塔胡帕帕（Tahupapa）；再之后是社会群岛的帕皮提（Papeete）；然后是中国北京。你呢？”
“头一回在布鲁塞尔（Brussels）；第二回在蒙得维的亚（Monte Video）；第三回在曼德勒（Mandalay），随后又到了非洲黄金海岸。该您了，巴克斯特先生。”
教士瞥了一眼那座钟。恰好八点整。
“头一回在阿富汗的喀布尔（Cabul）；第二回在俄罗斯的下诺夫哥罗德（Nijni Novgorod）；第三回在澳大利亚达令河畔的威尔坎尼亚（Wilcannia）；第四回在智利的瓦尔帕莱索（Valparaiso）；第五回在日本长崎（Nagasaki）。”
===Original===
“He is evidently a great traveller and a most mysterious person.”
“He is more than that,” said Eastover with conviction; “he is late for dinner!”
Prendergast looked at his watch.
“That clock is two minutes fast. Hark, there goes Big Ben! Eight exactly.”
As he spoke the door was thrown open and a voice announced “Dr. Nikola.”
The three men sprang to their feet simultaneously, with exclamations of astonishment, as the man they had been discussing made his appearance.
===Chinese===
“他显然是位了不起的旅行家，而且是个极其神秘的人物。”
“岂止如此，”伊斯特弗笃定地说，“他赴宴要迟到了！”
普伦德加斯特看了看自己的怀表。
“那口钟快了两分钟。听，大本钟（Big Ben）响了！正好八点。”
话音未落，门被猛地推开，一个声音通报：“尼古拉博士到。”
三人同时霍然起身，惊呼连连——他们方才议论的那个人，现身了。

===Original===
It would take more time than I can spare the subject to give you an adequate and inclusive description of the person who entered the room at that moment. In stature he was slightly above the ordinary, his shoulders were broad, his limbs perfectly shaped and plainly muscular, but very slim. His head, which was magnificently set upon his shoulders, was adorned with a profusion of glossy black hair; his face was destitute of beard or moustache, and was of oval shape and handsome moulding; while his skin was of a dark olive hue, a colour which harmonised well with his piercing black eyes and pearly teeth. His hands and feet were small, and the greatest dandy must have admitted that he was irreproachably dressed, with a neatness that bordered on the puritanical. In age he might have been anything from eight-and-twenty to forty; in reality he was thirty-three. He advanced into the room and walked with outstretched hand directly across to where Eastover was standing by the fireplace.
“Mr. Eastover, I feel certain,” he said, fixing his glittering eyes upon the man he addressed, and allowing a curious smile to play upon his face.
“That is my name, Dr. Nikola,” the other answered with evident surprise. “But how on earth can you distinguish me from your other guests?”
“Ah! it would surprise you if you knew. And Mr. Prendergast, and Mr. Baxter. This is delightful; I hope I am not late. We had a collision in the Channel this morning, and I was almost afraid I might not be up to time. Dinner seems ready; shall we sit down to it?”
They seated themselves, and the meal commenced. The Imperial Restaurant has earned an enviable reputation for doing things well, and the dinner that night did not in any way detract from its lustre. But delightful as it all was, it was noticeable that the three guests paid more attention to their host than to his excellent menu. As they had said before his arrival, they had all had dealings with him for several years, but what those dealings were they were careful not to describe. It was more than possible that they hardly liked to remember them themselves.
When coffee had been served and the servants had withdrawn, Dr. Nikola rose from the table, and went across to the massive sideboard. On it stood a basket of very curious shape and workmanship. This he opened, and as he did so, to the astonishment of his guests, an enormous cat, as black as his master’s coat, leaped out on to the floor. The reason for the saucer and jug of milk became evident.
===Chinese===
