“我敢说他准以为你所谓的私事就是去见裁缝，”我笑着回答，一心要让她知道，对于女人的门道我并非一无所知。

“恐怕他真是这么想的，”她红着脸答道，“单为这个缘故，我就觉得心虚得不得了。可是我的心告诉我，无论出了什么事，我都必须马上见你一面。”

世上还有哪个男人能指望听到比这更动听的话呢？倘若有，反正我不是那个男人。这时我们已进了大楼，正沿着宽阔的大楼梯往上走。一间间展厅和走廊里随处可见衣着漂亮的姑娘来来往往，却没有一个能与我身边这位白皙秀美的澳大利亚姑娘相比。走进楼梯顶头那间展厅时，我觉得这正是提出那个我久已渴望问她的问题的好机会。

“菲莉斯（Phyllis），我的心上人，”我开口道，声音里几乎带着颤音，“从我向你倾诉衷肠到如今，已经过去两个星期了。你有充裕的时间来掂量我们的处境。你可曾后悔把你的爱给了我？”

===Original===
We came to a standstill, and leant over a case together, but what it contained I’m sure I haven’t the very vaguest idea.

She looked up into my face with a sweet smile.

“Not for one single instant, Dick! Having once given you my love, is it likely I should want it back again?”

“I don’t know. Somehow I can’t discover sufficient reason for your giving it to me at all.”

“Well, be sure I’m not going to tell you. You might grow conceited. Isn’t it sufficient that I do love you, and that I am not going to give you up, whatever happens?”

“More than sufficient,” I answered solemnly. “But, Phyllis, don’t you think I can induce your father to relent? Surely as a good parent he must be anxious to promote your happiness at any cost to himself?”

===Chinese===
我们停住脚步，一同俯身在一只陈列柜上，至于那柜子里陈列的究竟是什么，说实在的，我连一点最模糊的概念也说不上来。

她抬起头，带着甜甜的微笑仰望我的脸。

“一刹那也不曾后悔过，迪克！既然已经把爱给了你，难道我还可能再讨回去吗？”

“那可说不准。不知怎么的，我总想不出你有什么充分的理由把它给到我身上。”

“哼，放心吧，我才不告诉你呢。告诉了你，你怕不飘飘然起来。我确确实实地爱着你，而且无论出了什么事都不打算放弃你，这难道还不够吗？”

“岂止是够了，”我郑重其事地答道，“可是，菲莉斯，你不觉得我能让你父亲回心转意吗？身为一位好父亲，他必定巴不得不惜牺牲自己来成全你的幸福，这不是理所当然的吗？”

===Original===
“I can’t understand it at all. He has been so devoted to me all my life that his conduct now is quite inexplicable. Never once has he denied me anything I really set my heart upon, and he always promised me that I should be allowed to marry whomsoever I pleased, provided he was a good and honourable man, and one of whom he could in any way approve. And you are all that, Dick, or I shouldn’t have loved you, I know.”

“I don’t think I’m any worse than the ordinary run of men, dearest, if I am no better. At any rate I love you with a true and honourable love. But don’t you think he will come round in time?”

“I’m almost afraid not. He referred to it only yesterday, and seemed quite angry that I should have dared to entertain any thought of you after what he said to me on board ship. It was the first time in my life he ever spoke to me in such a tone, and I felt it keenly. No, Dick, there is something behind it all that I cannot understand. Some mystery that I would give anything to fathom. Papa has not been himself ever since we started for England. Indeed, his very reason for coming at all is an enigma to me. And now that he is here, he seems in continual dread of meeting somebody﻿—but who that somebody is, and why my father, who has the name and reputation of being such a courageous, determined, honourable man, should be afraid, is a thing I cannot understand.”

“It’s all very mysterious and unfortunate. But surely something can be done? Don’t you think if I were to see him again, and put the matter more plainly before him, something might be arranged?”

“It would be worse than useless at present, I fear. No, you must just leave it to me, and I’ll do my best to talk him round. Ever since my mother died I have been as his right hand, and it will be strange if he does not listen to me and see reason in the end.”

Seeing who it was that would plead with him I did not doubt it.

===Chinese===
“这事我全然想不通。他一辈子对我那样慈爱，如今这举动实在没法解释。凡是我真心想要的东西，他从不曾驳回过一件；他还总应许我说，日后我爱嫁给谁便可以嫁给谁，只要那是个品行端正、光明磊落的男子汉，是他多少看得中意的。而这些你样样都占全了，迪克，不然我也不会爱上你，这我心里有数。”

“亲爱的，就算我不比寻常男子高明，总也不见得比他们差。无论如何，我是怀着一片真诚磊落的爱情爱你的。可你不觉得他早晚总会想通吗？”

“我倒怕他不会。就在昨天他还提起这事，看那样子恼火得很，怪我在船上听了他那番话之后，竟还敢对你存着念头。那是我平生头一遭听他用这种口气同我说话，我心里着实难受。不，迪克，这背后准有什么我参不透的缘由。有个谜团，叫我拿什么都愿意，只求能探它个水落石出。自从动身来英国，爸爸就总是失魂落魄，不像平日的他了。说实在的，他这趟究竟为什么要来，对我也像个哑谜。如今人既然到了这里，他倒像是时时刻刻都在提防撞见什么人——可那人到底是谁，我父亲素来有勇敢、果决、正直的名声，为什么会害怕，这就更不是我能想明白的事了。”

“这件事真是又离奇又糟糕。可总该想个法子才好？你看，要是我再见他一面，把事情原原本本、清清楚楚摆在他面前，是不是或许能有转圜？”

“眼下这么办，恐怕非但无益，反而更糟。不，这件事你只管交给我，我尽力劝转他。自从母亲去世，我就一直充当他的左右手，他若到头来还不肯听我的、想不通这层道理，那才怪呢。”

想到要去恳求他的是这样一个人，我便毫不怀疑了。

===Original===
By this time we had wandered through many rooms, and now found ourselves in the Egyptian Department, surrounded by embalmed dead folk and queer objects of all sorts and descriptions. There was something almost startling about our lovemaking in such a place, among these men and women, whose wooings had been conducted in a country so widely different to ours, and in an age that was dead and gone over two thousand years ere we were born. I spoke of this to Phyllis. She laughed and gave a little shiver.

“I wonder,” she said, looking down on the swathed-up figure of a princess of the royal house of Egypt, lying stretched out in the case beside which we sat, “if this great lady, who lies so still and silent now, had any trouble with her love affair?”

“Perhaps she had more than one beau to her string, and not being allowed to have one took the other,” I answered; “though from what we can see of her now she doesn’t look as if she were ever capable of exercising much fascination, does she?”

As I spoke I looked from the case to the girl and compared the swaddled-up figure with the healthy, living, lovely creature by my side. But I hadn’t much time for comparison. My sweetheart had taken her watch from her pocket and was glancing at the dial.

“A quarter to twelve!” she cried in alarm. “Oh, Dick, I must be going. I promised to meet papa at twelve, and whatever happens I must not keep him waiting.”

She rose and was about to pull on her gloves. But before she had time to do so I had taken a little case from my pocket and opened it. When she saw what it contained she could not help a little womanly cry of delight.

===Chinese===
这时我们已逛过了许多展厅，来到埃及展厅，四下尽是涂抹香料防腐的死人，还有各式各样千奇百怪的物件。在这样一个地方、在这样一些男男女女中间谈情说爱，总觉得有几分骇人——这些人当年的求爱，发生在一个同我们截然不同的国度，发生在一个在我们降生前两千多年便已湮灭的时代。我把这感想对菲莉斯说了。她笑了笑，微微打了个寒噤。

“真不知道，”她说，低头望着陈列柜里那具直挺挺躺着的、裹满殓布的埃及王室公主遗身——我们就坐在这只柜子旁边，“这位如今躺得这样安静、这样沉默的贵妇人，当年她自己的那段情事，可也曾有过波折么？”

“说不定她裙下之臣不止一位，家里不许她要这个，她便索性要了那个，”我答道，“不过照我们现在看到的这副模样，她仿佛从来不曾有过多大的迷人功夫，你说是不是？”

说话间，我的目光从陈列柜移到姑娘身上，把那具裹布的身影同我身旁这个健康、鲜活、可爱的人儿比了又比。只是容我比较的工夫并不多。我的心上人已经从口袋里掏出表来，正看着表盘。

“差一刻就十二点了！”她惊呼道，“哎呀，迪克，我得走了。我答应十二点去见爸爸，无论如何也不能叫他等我。”

她站起身来，正要戴手套。不等她来得及戴上，我已从口袋里摸出一只小盒子打了开来。她看清里面装的是什么，忍不住发出一声女人家欢喜的轻呼。


===Original===
“Oh, Dick! you naughty, extravagant boy!”

“Why, dearest? Why naughty or extravagant to give the woman I love a little token of my affection?” As I spoke I slipped the ring over her pretty finger and raised the hand to my lips.

“Will you try,” I said, “whenever you look at that ring, to remember that the man who gave it to you loves you with his whole heart and soul, and will count no trouble too great, or no exertion too hard, to make you happy?”

“I will remember,” she said solemnly, and when I looked I saw that tears stood in her eyes. She brushed them hastily away, and after an interlude which it hardly becomes me to mention here, we went down the stairs again and out into the street, almost in silence.

Having called a cab, I placed her in it and nervously asked the question that had been sometime upon my mind:﻿—

“When shall I see you again?”

===Chinese===
“哦，迪克！你这个又淘气又乱花钱的坏小子！”

“怎么啦，最亲爱的？送给我心爱的女人一件小小的爱情纪念物，怎么就算淘气、算乱花钱了？”说话间，我把戒指套上她那秀美的手指，又把那只手举到唇边一吻。

“你愿不愿意答应我，”我说，“往后每当望着这枚戒指，就记起送你戒指的这个人正全心全意地爱着你，为了叫你幸福，天大的艰辛他也不嫌重，再难的操劳他也不怕苦？”

“我会记着的。”她郑重地说。我一看，她的眼里已经噙着泪水。她急忙把泪拭去，随后经过一段不宜在此细述的小插曲，我们重又下楼出了大门走到街上，一路上几乎谁也没有开口。

叫来一辆马车，我扶她上了车，然后忐忑不安地问出了那个在心里盘桓已久的问题：——

“我什么时候能再见到你？”

===Original===
“I cannot tell,” she answered. “Perhaps next week. But I’ll let you know. In the meantime don’t despair; all will come right yet! Goodbye.”

“Goodbye and God bless you!”

I lifted my hat, she waved her hand, and next moment the hansom had disappeared round the corner.

Having seen the last of her I wandered slowly down the pavement towards Oxford Street, then turning to my left hand, made my way citywards. My mind was full of my interview with the sweet girl who had just left me, and I wandered on and on, wrapped in my own thoughts, until I found myself in a quarter of London into which I had never hitherto penetrated. The streets were narrow, and, as if to be in keeping with the general air of gloom, the shops were small and their wares of a peculiarly sordid nature; handcarts, barrows, and stalls lined the grimy pavements, and the noise was deafening.

A church clock somewhere in the neighbourhood struck “One,” and as I was beginning to feel hungry, and knew myself to be a long way from my hotel, I cast about me for a lunching-place. But it was some time before I encountered the class of restaurant I wanted. When I did it was situated at the corner of two streets, carried a foreign name over the door, and, though considerably the worse for wear, presented a cleaner appearance than any other I had as yet experienced.

Pushing the door open I entered. An unmistakable Frenchman, whose appearance, however, betokened long residence in England, stood behind a narrow counter polishing an absinthe glass. He bowed politely and asked my business.

===Chinese===
“这我说不准，”她答道，“也许下个星期。到时候我一定捎信给你。眼下你可别灰心，一切终归会有转机的！再见。”

“再见，愿上帝保佑你！”

我举了举帽子，她挥了挥手，转眼间那辆双轮马车已在街角后头消失了。

望到再也望不见她之后，我沿着人行道慢慢朝牛津街（Oxford Street）走去，随后向左手一拐，往伦敦城的方向信步而行。满脑子都是刚才同那位可爱姑娘的会面，我一路想着心事走了又走，直到发觉自己已置身于伦敦一处从未到过的地段。街道狭窄，仿佛特意要与四周阴沉黯淡的气氛相称，铺面都很小，卖的货色也格外寒酸破败；手推车、独轮货车和街头摊子沿着污秽的人行道排成一列，喧闹声震耳欲聋。

附近什么地方的教堂钟敲了一点，我渐渐觉得饿了，又明知这里离旅馆很远，便四下张望，想寻个吃午饭的地方。可是过了一阵才碰上我想要找的那一类馆子。那馆子坐落在两条街的拐角上，门楣上挂着一个外国字号，虽然颇有些年久失修的破败相，看上去却比我一路所见过的任何一家都体面干净。

我推门进去。柜台后面站着一个一看便知是法国人的汉子——不过他那副模样，倒显出他在英国已经住得很久了——正在擦拭一只苦艾酒杯。他客气地鞠了一躬，问我来意。

===Original===
“Can I have lunch?” I asked.

“Oui, monsieur! Cer-tain-lee. If monsieur will walk upstairs I will take his order.”

Waving his hand in the direction of a staircase in the corner of the shop he again bowed elaborately, while I, following the direction he indicated, proceeded to the room above. It was long and lofty, commanded an excellent view of both thoroughfares, and was furnished with a few inferior pictures, a much worn oilcloth, half a dozen small marble-top tables, and four times as many chairs.

When I entered three men were in occupation. Two were playing chess at a side table, while the third, who had evidently no connection with them, was watching the game from a distance, at the same time pretending to be absorbed in his paper. Seating myself at a table near the door, I examined the bill of fare, selected my lunch, and in order to amuse myself while it was preparing, fell to scrutinizing my companions.

Of the chess-players, one was a big, burly fellow, with enormous arms, protruding rheumy eyes, a florid complexion, and a voluminous red beard. His opponent was of a much smaller build, with pale features, a tiny moustache, and watery blue eyes. He wore a pince-nez, and from the length of his hair and a dab of crimson lake upon his shirt cuff, I argued him an artist.

Leaving the chess-players, my eyes lighted on the stranger on the other side. He was more interesting in every way. Indeed, I was surprised to see a man of his stamp in the house at all. He was tall and slim, but exquisitely formed, and plainly the possessor of enormous strength. His head, if only from a phrenological point of view, was a magnificent one, crowned with a wealth of jet black hair. His eyes were dark as night, and glittered like those of a snake. His complexion was of a decidedly olive hue, though, as he sat in the shadow of the corner, it was difficult to tell this at first sight.

===Chinese===
“这儿能开午饭吗？”我问。

“Oui, monsieur!（是，先生！）Cer-tain-lee（当——然——行）。先生肯请上楼，我这就来听您点菜。”

他朝店堂角落里一道楼梯的方向摆一摆手，又郑重其事地鞠了一躬，我便顺着他指的方向上了楼，走进楼上那间屋子。屋子长而高敞，两条大街的街景尽收眼底，屋里陈设着几幅蹩脚的图画、一块磨得斑驳的油地毡、六张带大理石面的小桌，椅子的数目则是桌子的四倍。

我进去的时候，屋里已坐了三个人。有两个正在旁边一张桌上下国际象棋，第三个显然与他们毫不相干，远远地望着那盘棋，同时装作专心致志地看报。我在靠门的一张桌旁坐下，看了看菜单，点好午饭，趁等菜的当口细细打量起我这几位同伴来。

下棋的两个当中，一个是个五大三粗的壮汉，胳膊粗壮，一双凸出的泪汪汪的眼睛，面色红润，蓄着一大部浓密的红色胡须。他的对手身量小得多，脸色苍白，留着一撇小胡子，一双水汪汪的蓝眼睛，鼻梁上架着一副夹鼻眼镜。从他头发的长度，再看他衬衫袖口上沾着的一点胭脂红颜料，我断定他是个画家。

且撇开那两个下棋的，我的目光又落到另一边那个陌生人身上。此人从哪方面看都更有意思。说实话，这家馆子里居然会有他这路人物的光临，倒叫我诧异。他身量高挑，却生得匀称精美之至，分明膂力过人。他那颗头颅，哪怕单从骨相学的眼光看，也称得上一颗极佳的头颅，满头覆着浓密的乌黑头发。他的一双眼睛黑得像夜，闪出蛇眼一般的光。他的肤色分明是明显的橄榄色，只是他坐在角落的阴影里，乍看之下不易看出来。

===Original===
But what most fascinated me about this curious individual was the interest he was taking in the game the other men were playing. He kept his eyes fixed upon the board, looked anxiously from one to the other as a move trembled in the balance, smiled sardonically when his desires were realised, and sighed almost aloud when a mistake was made.

Every moment I expected his anxiety or disappointment to find vent in words, but he always managed to control himself in time. When he became excited I noticed that his whole body quivered under its influence, and once when the smaller of the players made an injudicious move a look flew into his face that was full of such malignant intensity that I’ll own I was influenced by it. What effect it would have had upon the innocent cause of it all, had he seen it, I should have been sorry to conjecture.

Just as my lunch made its appearance the game reached a conclusion, and the taller of the two players, having made a remark in German, rose to leave. It was evident that the smaller man had won, and in an excess of pride, to which I gathered his nature was not altogether a stranger, he looked round the room as if in defiance.

Doing so, his eyes met those of the man in the corner. I glanced from one to the other, but my gaze rested longest on the face of the smaller man. So fascinated did he seem to be by the other’s stare that his eyes became set and stony. It was just as if he were being mesmerised. The person he looked at rose, approached him, sat down at the table and began to arrange the men on the board without a word. Then he looked up again.

“May I have the pleasure of giving you a game?” he asked in excellent English, bowing slightly as he spoke, and moving a pawn with his long white fingers.

The little man found voice enough to murmur an appropriate reply, and they began their game, while I turned to my lunch. But, in spite of myself, I found my eyes continually reverting to what was happening at the other table. And, indeed, it was a curious sight I saw there.

===Chinese===
不过，这个古怪人物身上最叫我着迷的，还是他对另外两人那盘棋的兴致。他目不转睛地盯着棋盘，一步棋悬而未决时，便焦灼地把目光从这一个移到那一个；心愿得偿，便讥诮地一笑；有人走了昏着，便几乎要叹息出声。

我时时刻刻都以为他的焦灼或失望就要脱口而出，可他总能及时克制住自己。我注意到，他一激动起来，浑身上下都会跟着发颤；有一回，个子较小的那个棋手走了一步失策的棋，他脸上倏地掠过一种神情，狠毒之气咄咄逼人，连我自己也得承认受了它的震慑。倘若这一幕的无辜祸首瞧见了这副神情，会落下什么后果，我真不忍心去揣测。

恰在我的午饭端上来的时候，那盘棋见了分晓。两人中个子较高的那个用德语说了句什么，起身要走。分明是个子较小的那个赢了；他一时得意忘形——据我看，这种得意劲儿对他的性情来说倒也不算全然陌生——朝屋子里环顾四周，仿佛存心向人挑衅似的。

这一看不要紧，他的目光正撞上角落里那人的目光。我在两人之间来回打量，目光却在小个子的脸上停留得最久。他似乎被对方那凝视勾住了魂，两眼发直，僵若顽石，活脱脱像是正被人施行催眠术。被他盯着的那人站起身，走到他跟前，在桌旁坐下，一言不发地动手在棋盘上排起棋子来。然后他重新抬起头来。

“可否赏光，容我陪您下一局？”他用一口漂亮地道的英语问道，说话时微微欠身，修长白皙的手指随即推动一枚卒子。

小个子好不容易挤出一句话来，咕哝了一句得体的应答，两人便对弈起来，我则转身吃我的午饭。可是我不由自主，眼睛总是一再溜回邻桌那边的动静。而且说实在的，那边呈现的景象也着实古怪。

===Original===
The tall man had thrown himself into the business of the game, heart and soul. He half sat, half crouched over the board, reminding me more of a hawk hovering over a poultry yard than anything else I can liken him to.

His eyes were riveted first on the men before him and then on his opponent﻿—his long fingers twitched and twined over each move, and seemed as if they would never release their hold. Not once did he speak, but his attitude was more expressive than any words.

The effect on the little man, his companion, was overwhelming. He was quite unable to do anything, but sat huddled up in his chair as if terrified by his demoniacal companion. The result even a child might have foreseen. The tall man won, and the little man, only too glad to have come out of the ordeal with a whole skin, seized his hat and, with a half-uttered apology, darted from the room.

For a moment or two his extraordinary opponent sat playing with the chessmen. Then he looked across at me and without hesitation said, accompanying his remark with a curious smile, for which I could not at all account:﻿—

“I think you will agree with me that the limitations of the fool are the birth gifts of the wise!”

Not knowing what reply to make to this singular assertion, I wisely held my tongue. This brought about a change in his demeanour; he rose from his seat, and came across to where I sat. Seating himself in a chair directly opposite me, he folded his hands in his lap, after the manner of a demure old spinster, and, having looked at me earnestly, said with an almost indescribable sweetness of tone:﻿—

===Chinese===
高个子把全副身心都扑进了这盘棋的营生里。他半坐半蹲地俯在棋盘上，那副模样首先叫我想到的，是一只盘旋在鸡场上空的鹞鹰，此外再难找出更贴切的比方。

他的目光先是死死钉在面前的棋子上，继而又钉在对手脸上——修长的手指随着每一步棋抽动绞扭，仿佛一旦抓住什么便再不肯撒手。他自始至终一言不发，可他那份情态比任何言语都更有表现力。

这一切对他那位同伴小个子的作用，却是排山倒海。他全然动弹不得，缩成一团窝在椅子里，仿佛被身边这个魔鬼附身似的伙伴吓破了胆。结局连小孩子也料得到：高个子赢了。小个子只庆幸自己总算囫囵着脱了身，一把抓起帽子，道歉的话说了一半，便一头窜出了屋子。

有那么一两分钟，他那位异乎寻常的对手坐在原处摆弄着棋子。随后他隔桌望向我，毫不迟疑地开了口，说话时脸上带着一种我全然捉摸不透的古怪微笑：——

“我想您会同意我的看法：愚人的种种局限，正是智者与生俱来的禀赋！”

对这句奇谈怪论，我不知该作何答对，便明智地缄口不言。这一来他的做派便变了；他从座位上站起，走到我坐的桌前来，在我正对面的一把椅子上坐下，像一位端庄的老小姐那样把双手交叠搁在膝上，恳切地端详了我一阵，然后用一种几乎难以言传的甜美声调说道：——

===Original===
“I think you will allow, Mr. Hatteras, that half the world is born for the other half to prey upon!”

For a moment I was too much astonished to speak; how on earth had he become aware of my name? I stumbled out some sort of reply, which evidently did not impress him very much, for he began again:

“Our friend who has just left us will most certainly be one of those preyed upon. I pity him because he will not have the smallest grain of pleasure in his life. You, Mr. Hatteras, on the other hand, will, unwittingly, be in the other camp. Circumstances will arrange that for you. Some have, of course, no desire to prey; but necessity forces it on them. Yourself, for instance. Some only prey when they are quite sure there will be no manner of risk. Our German friend who played the previous game is an example. Others, again, never lose an opportunity. Candidly speaking, to which class should you imagine I belong?”

He smiled as he put the question, and, his thin lips parting, I could just catch the glitter of the short teeth with which his mouth was furnished. For the third time since I had made his acquaintance I did not know which way to answer. However, I made a shot and said something.

“I really know nothing about you,” I answered. “But from your kindness in giving our artist friend a game, and now in allowing me the benefit of your conversation, I should say you only prey upon your fellow-men when dire extremity drives you to it.”

“And you would be wrong. I am of the last class I mentioned. There is only one sport of any interest to me in life, and that is the opportunity of making capital out of my fellow humans. You see, I am candid with you, Mr. Hatteras!”

===Chinese===
“我想您会承认，哈特拉斯先生（Mr. Hatteras），这世上一半人生下来，就是供另一半人猎食的！”

一时间我惊愕得说不出话来；他到底从哪儿得知了我的名字？我结结巴巴敷衍了几句，显然没给他留下多深的印象，因为他又说了起来：

“咱们那位刚刚离去的朋友，注定要归入被猎食的一类。我可怜他，因为他这一生连一星半点儿的乐趣也尝不着。您呢，哈特拉斯先生，恰恰相反，将会身不由己地站到另一个营垒里去。情势自会替您安排妥当。有些人当然无意猎食他人，可境遇偏逼着他们下手——譬如说，您本人。有些人只在确信毫无风险时才肯下口，方才下头一盘棋的那位德国朋友便是一例。还有些人呢，则从不肯错过任何机会。坦白地讲，您猜我属于哪一类？”

他提出这个问题时莞尔一笑，薄唇启处，我恰好瞥见他口中那口短牙闪烁的微光。自同他结识以来，这已是第三回我不知道该往哪边答话了。不过，我还是胡乱猜着说了一句。

“我对您实在一无所知，”我答道，“不过，从您好心陪我们的画家朋友下一盘棋，又承蒙赏光同我交谈来看，依我说，您只有在山穷水尽的绝境里，才会向自己的同类下口。”

“那您可就猜错了。我属于方才说的最后一类。人世间只有一种玩法能叫我提起兴致，那就是瞅准时机从我的同类身上牟利取乐。您瞧，我对您够坦白的了吧，哈特拉斯先生！”

===Original===
“Pray excuse me. But you know my name! As I have never, to my knowledge, set eyes on you before, would you mind telling me how you became acquainted with it?”

“With every pleasure. But before I do so I think it only fair to tell you that you will not believe my explanation. And yet it should convince you. At any rate we’ll try. In your right-hand top waistcoat pocket you have three cards.” Here he leant his head on his hands and shut his eyes. “One is crinkled and torn, but it has written on it, in pencil, the name of Edward Braithwaite, Macquarrie Street, Sydney. I presume the name is Braithwaite, but the *t* and *e* are almost illegible. The second is rather a high sounding one﻿—the Hon. Sylvester Wetherell, Potts Point, Sydney, New South Wales, and the third is, I take it, your own, Richard Hatteras. Am I right?”

I put my fingers in my pocket, and drew out what it contained﻿—a half-sovereign, a shilling, a small piece of pencil, and three cards. The first, a well-worn piece of pasteboard, bore, surely enough, the name of Edward Braithwaite, and was that of the solicitor with whom I transacted my business in Sydney; the second was given me by my sweetheart’s father the day before we left Australia; and the third was certainly my own.

Was this witchcraft or only some clever conjuring trick? I asked myself the question, but could give it no satisfactory answer. At any rate you may be sure it did not lessen my respect for my singular companion.

“Ah! I am right then!” he cried exultingly. “Isn’t it strange how the love of being right remains with us, when we think we have safely combatted every other self-conceit. Well, Mr. Hatteras, I am very pleased to have made your acquaintance. Somehow I think we are destined to meet again﻿—where I cannot say. At any rate, let us hope that that meeting will be as pleasant and successful as this has been.”

But I hardly heard what he said. I was still puzzling my brains over his extraordinary conjuring trick﻿—for trick I am convinced it was. He had risen and was slowly drawing on his gloves when I spoke.

===Chinese===
“请恕我冒昧。可是，您知道我的姓名！就我所知，我先前从未同您照过面，可否请您告诉我，您是从何处得知它的？”

“乐意之至。不过在奉告之前，我认为先向您声明一句才算公道：您是不会相信我的解释的。然而它本该叫您信服才是。也罢，我们不妨一试。您马甲右手边上头的口袋里，装着三张名片。”说到这里，他双手托住头，闭上了眼睛。“一张已经又皱又破，上面用铅笔写着一个名字：爱德华·布雷思韦特（Edward Braithwaite），悉尼麦夸里街（Macquarrie Street）。我想那名字是布雷思韦特，只是字母 *t* 和 *e* 都快认不出来了。第二张的名头可响亮些——尊敬的西尔维斯特·韦瑟雷尔（the Hon. Sylvester Wetherell），新南威尔士（New South Wales）悉尼波茨角（Potts Point）。第三张呢，我料想是您自己的——理查德·哈特拉斯（Richard Hatteras）。我说得可对？”

我把手伸进那口袋，掏出了里面的东西——一枚半镑金币、一先令、一小截铅笔，还有三张名片。头一张，一块磨旧了的纸板片，上面印着的果然是爱德华·布雷思韦特的名字，正是我在悉尼打交道的那位律师；第二张是临离澳大利亚的前一天我心上人的父亲给我的；第三张，确确实实是我自己的。

这是妖法，还是不过一套高明的戏法？我心中自问，却给不出一个称心的答案。无论如何，您可以料想得到，这丝毫没有减少我对这位奇特的同伴的敬意。

“啊哈！这么说我是对的了！”他扬扬得意地叫道，“真是怪事，我们自以为把别的种种自负都稳稳斗倒之后，独独这种‘自己一贯正确’的自负，倒还牢牢地留在心里。好了，哈特拉斯先生，结识您我很高兴。不知怎么的，我总觉得我们命中注定还要再见——在哪里，我可说不上。无论如何，但愿那一回的重逢，也像这一回一样愉快，一样有成果。”

可是他的话我几乎没有听进去。我还在为那个离奇的戏法绞脑汁——因为我确信那终归是个戏法。他已站起身来，正慢慢戴手套，这时我开了口。

===Original===
“I have been thinking over those cards,” I said, “and I am considerably puzzled. How on earth did you know they were there?”

“If I told you, you would have no more faith in my powers. So with your permission I will assume the virtue of modesty. Call it a conjuring trick, if you like. Many curious things are hidden under that comprehensive term. But that is neither here nor there. Before I go would you like to see one more?”

“Very much, indeed, if it’s as good as the last!” I replied.

In the window stood a large glass dish, half full of water and having a dark brown fly paper floating on the surface. He brought it across to the table at which I sat, and having drained the water into a jug near by, left the paper sticking to the bottom.

This done, he took a tiny leather case from his pocket and a small bottle out of that again. From this bottle he poured a few drops of some highly pungent liquid on to the paper, with the result that it grew black as ink and threw off a tiny vapour, which licked the edges of the bowl and curled upwards in a faint spiral column.

“There, Mr. Hatteras, this is a﻿—well, a trick﻿—I learned from an old woman in Benares. It is a better one than the last and will repay your interest. If you will look on that paper for a moment, and try to concentrate your attention, you will see something that will, I think, astonish you.”

===Chinese===
“我一直在琢磨那几张名片，”我说，“实在是给弄糊涂了。您究竟是怎么知道它们在那儿的？”

“我若告诉了您，您从此就不会再信服我的本领了。所以，恕我僭越，权且假装一回谦虚的美德。您乐意，只管叫它戏法。在这一个无所不包的名目底下，可藏着许许多多稀奇事儿呢。不过这话无关紧要。我临走之前，您愿不愿再看一个？”

“要是赶得上刚才那个精彩，那可太愿意了！”我答道。

窗台上摆着一只大玻璃盆，盛着半盆水，水面上漂着一张深褐色的捕蝇纸。他把盆端到我坐的桌前，将水滗进旁边一只壶里，让那张纸牢牢粘在盆底。

做完这个，他从口袋里掏出一只小小的皮套，又从皮套里取出一个小瓶。他往纸上滴了几滴气味极其辛烈刺鼻的液体，那张纸顿时变得墨黑，腾起一缕细细的蒸汽，蒸汽舔着盆沿，袅袅向上，卷成一道淡淡的螺旋。

“您瞧，哈特拉斯先生，这是我在贝拿勒斯（Benares）跟一位老妇人学来的一个——唔，就说是戏法吧。它比刚才那个更妙，值得您费神一看。您只要对着那张纸望上一会儿，尽力把心思收拢集中，我想您就会看见一样叫您吃惊的东西。”

===Original===
Hardly believing that I should see anything at all I looked. But for some seconds without success. My scepticism, however, soon left me. At first I saw only the coarse grain of the paper and the thin vapour rising from it. Then the knowledge that I was gazing into a dish vanished, I forgot my companion and the previous conjuring trick. I saw only a picture opening out before me﻿—that of a handsomely furnished room, in which was a girl sitting in an easy chair crying as if her heart were breaking. The room I had never seen before, but the girl I should have known among a thousand. She was Phyllis, my sweetheart!

I looked and looked, and as I gazed at her I heard her call my name. “Oh, Dick! Dick! come to me!”

Instantly I sprang to my feet, meaning to cross the room to her. Next moment I became aware of a loud crash. The scene vanished, my senses came back to me, and to my astonishment I found myself standing alongside the overturned restaurant table. The glass dish lay on the floor shattered into a thousand fragments. My friend, the conjuror, had disappeared.

Having righted the table again, I went downstairs and explained my misfortune. When I had paid my bill I took my departure, more troubled in mind than I cared to confess. That it was only what he had called it, a conjuring trick, I felt I ought to be certain, but still it was clever and uncanny enough to render me very uncomfortable.

In vain I tried to drive the remembrance of the scene I had witnessed from my brain, but it would not be dispelled. At length, to satisfy myself, I resolved that if the memory of it remained with me so vividly in the morning I would take the bull by the horns and call at the Metropole to make enquiries.

===Chinese===
我并不大相信当真能看见什么，可还是看了。头几秒钟一无所见。然而我的怀疑不久便烟消云散。起初我只看见纸的粗粝纹理和纸上袅袅升起的细弱蒸汽。随后，我正对着一只盆看的这层意识消失了，我忘了同伴，也忘了先前的戏法。我只看见一幅画面在眼前徐徐展开——一间陈设华丽的房间，房中一个姑娘坐在安乐椅里，哭得柔肠寸断。那房间我从未见过，可那姑娘，纵使混在千人之中我也认得出来。她正是菲莉斯，我的心上人！

我看了又看，正当我凝望她时，我听见她唤我的名字。“哦，迪克！迪克！快到我这里来！”

我霍地跳起身来，想穿过房间奔到她跟前。紧接着我便察觉一声巨响。幻景消散了，神志回来了，我惊愕地发现自己正站在掀翻的餐桌旁边。玻璃盆摔在地板上，碎成了千百片。我那位戏法师朋友，已经无影无踪。

把桌子重新扶正之后，我下楼说明了我的这场变故。付过账，我便离了那家馆子，心里的烦乱远比我肯承认的为甚。我本该认定那不过是他自称的戏法，可它终究又精妙又邪门，搅得我心里极不踏实。

我竭力想把亲眼所见的那一幕从脑子里驱走，它却怎么也不肯消散。末了，为求安心，我打定主意：倘若明天早晨这记忆仍这样历历鲜明地留在我心头，我就索性硬着头皮，径直到大都会旅馆去问个明白。

===Original===
I returned to my hotel in time for dinner, but still I could not rid myself of the feeling that some calamity was approaching. Having sent my meal away almost untouched, I called a hansom and drove to the nearest theatre, but the picture of Phyllis crying and calling for me in vain kept me company throughout the performance, and brought me home more miserable at the end than I had started. All night long I dreamed of it, seeing the same picture again and again, and hearing the same despairing cry, “Oh, Dick! Dick! come to me!”

In the morning there was only one thing to be done. Accordingly, after breakfast I set off to make sure that nothing was the matter. On the way I tried to reason with myself. I asked how it was that I, Dick Hatteras, a man who thought he knew the world so well, should have been so impressed with a bit of wizardry as to be willing to risk making a fool of myself before the two last people in the world I wanted to think me one. Once I almost determined to turn back, but while the intention held me the picture rose again before my mind’s eye, and on I went more resolved to solve the mystery than before.

Arriving at the hotel, I paid my cabman and entered the hall. A gorgeously caparisoned porter stood on the steps, and of him I enquired where I could find Miss Wetherell. Imagine my surprise when he replied:﻿—

“They’ve left sir. Started yesterday afternoon, quite suddenly, for Paris, on their way back to Australia!”

===Chinese===
我赶回旅馆，正赶上晚饭，可那种大祸将临的感觉仍旧缠着我，摆脱不开。饭菜几乎原封不动地被打发了回去，我叫了一辆双轮马车，驶往最近的一家戏院；可是菲莉斯哭喊着徒劳地唤我的那幅景象，整场戏都陪着我，散了场把我送回家时，我比出门时更加愁苦。一整夜我都在梦里见到它，一遍又一遍地看见同一幅画面，听见同一声绝望的呼喊：“哦，迪克！迪克！快到我这里来！”

第二天早晨，只有一件事可做。于是吃过早饭，我便动身去弄个明白，看看究竟出了事没有。一路上我试着同自己讲道理。我问自己，我，迪克·哈特拉斯，一个自以为把世事摸得透熟的人，怎么会被一点巫术邪法弄得这般神魂颠倒，竟甘愿在全天下我最不愿被他们看做傻瓜的那两个人面前丢丑露乖。有一阵我几乎下定决心掉头回去，可就在那念头还攥着我的时候，那幅画面又在我眼前升了起来，我便继续向前，要解开这个谜的决心比先前更坚定了。

到了旅馆，我付了车钱，走进门厅。台阶上站着一名衣着鲜亮华丽的门房，我便向他打听，到哪里可以找到韦瑟雷尔小姐。请想想，他这样回答时我是何等惊讶：——

“他们已经走了，先生。昨天下午动身的，走得非常突然，去巴黎，取道回澳大利亚！”

## III Lord Beckenham’s Story / 第三章 贝肯汉姆侯爵的自述

===Original===
“When you left me, Mr. Hatteras, to visit Miss Wetherell at Potts Point, I remained in the house for half an hour or so reading. Then, thinking no harm could possibly come of it, I started out for a little excursion on my own account. It was about half-past eleven then.
“Leaving the hotel I made for the ferry and crossed Darling Harbour to Millers Point; then, setting myself for a good ramble, off I went through the city, up one street and down another, to eventually bring up in the botanical gardens. The view was so exquisite that I sat myself down on a seat and resigned myself to rapturous contemplation of it. How long I remained there I could not possibly say. I only know that while I was watching the movements of a man-o’-war in the cove below me I became aware, by intuition﻿—for I did not look at him﻿—that I was the object of close scrutiny by a man standing some little distance from me. Presently I found him drawing closer to me, until he came boldly up and seated himself beside me. He was a queer-looking little chap, in some ways not unlike my old tutor Baxter, with a shrewd, clean-shaven face, grey hair, bushy eyebrows, and a long and rather hooked nose. He was well dressed, and when he spoke did so with some show of education. When we had been sitting side by side for some minutes he turned to me and said﻿—
“ ‘It is a beautiful picture we have spread before us, is it not?’
“ ‘It is, indeed,’ I answered. ‘And what a diversity of shipping?’
“ ‘You may well say that,’ he continued. ‘It would be an interesting study, would it not, to make a list of all the craft that pass in and out of this harbour in a day﻿—to put down the places where they were built and whence they hail, the characters of their owners and commanders, and their errands about the world. What a book it would make, would it not? Look at that man-o’-war in Farm Cove; think of the money she cost, think of where that money came from﻿—the rich people who paid without thinking, the poor who dreaded the coming of the tax collector like a visit from the Evil One; imagine the busy dockyard in which she was built﻿—can’t you seem to hear the clang of the riveters and the buzzing of the steam saws? Then take that Norwegian boat passing the fort there; think of her birthplace in far Norway, think of the places she has since seen, imagine her masts growing in the forests on the mountainside of lonely fjords, where the silence is so intense that a stone rolling down and dropping into the water echoes like thunder. Then again, look at that emigrant vessel steaming slowly up the harbour; think of the folk aboard her, every one with his hopes and fears, confident of a successful future in this terra incognita, or despondent of that and everything else. Away to the left there you see a little island schooner making her way down towards the blue Pacific; imagine her in a few weeks among the islands﻿—tropical heavens dropped down into sunlit waters﻿—buying such produce as perhaps you have never heard of. Yes, it is a wonderful picture﻿—a very wonderful picture?’
===Chinese===
“您离开我去波茨角看望韦瑟雷尔小姐之后，哈特拉斯先生，我在屋里读了半个钟头左右的书。随后我心想，出去走走总不至于惹出什么祸事，便自作主张，出门小小游览一番。那时大约是十一点半。
“出了旅馆，我便朝渡口走去，乘船横渡达令港（Darling Harbour），到米勒斯角（Millers Point）上岸；随后打定主意好好逛上一逛，穿城而行，走了一条街又一条街，最后在植物园里歇下了脚。那景致美不胜收，我便在一条长椅上坐下，一心沉醉地观赏起来。在那儿坐了多久，我实在说不上来。只记得，我正望着下方海湾里一艘战舰的动静时，忽然凭直觉意识到——因为我并没有朝他看——自己正被站在不远处的一个男人细细打量。不多时，我便发觉他朝我这边挪近，直到干脆大着胆子走上前来，在我身旁坐下。他是个模样古怪的小个子，某些地方竟有几分像我从前的家庭教师巴克斯特：一张刮得干干净净的精明脸孔，灰白头发，浓眉，一只又长又略带鹰钩的鼻子。他衣着体面，开口谈话，也颇见几分教养。我们并肩坐了几分钟，他转过脸来对我说——
“‘咱们眼前铺开的这幅图画真美，不是吗？’
“‘确实很美，’我答道，‘这往来的船只又是何等五光十色！’
“‘您这么说也在理，’他接着道，‘要是把一天之内进出这座港口的船只统统开列成册，记下它们各在何处建造、来自何方，船主和船长是何等人物，又在世上办着什么差使——这该是多么有意味的学问，不是吗？这又能写成一部多好的书啊，您说是不是？瞧农场湾（Farm Cove）里那艘战舰；想想它耗费的钱财，想想那钱从何而来——富人们掏起钱来想也不想，穷人们却怕收税官上门，如同怕魔鬼驾到；再想想建造它的那座繁忙船厂——您难道仿佛听得见铆钉工的叮当和蒸汽锯的嗡鸣？再看那边驶过炮台的挪威船；想想它远在挪威的出生之地，想想它此后见过的世面，想象它的桅杆还长在幽僻峡湾（fjord）山腰的森林里——那里静得出奇，一块石头滚落水中，回声竟如雷鸣。再瞧那艘缓缓驶进港来的移民船；想想船上的众人，个个怀着各自的希望与惶恐，有的笃信能在这片未知的疆土（terra incognita）上搏出前程，有的却对它、对世间万事都灰了心。再往左边看，那儿有一艘小岛纵帆船正朝碧蓝的太平洋驶去；想象几个星期后它置身群岛之间的模样——那是一座座坠入阳光海水里的热带天堂——采办着您也许闻所未闻的物产。是啊，这是一幅奇妙的图画——一幅非常奇妙的图画，不是吗？’

===Original===
“ ‘You seem to have studied it very carefully,’ I said, after a moment’s silence.
“ ‘Perhaps I have,’ he answered. ‘I am deeply interested in the life of the sea﻿—few more so. Are you a stranger in New South Wales?’
“ ‘Quite a stranger,’ I replied. ‘I only arrived in Australia a few days since.’
“ ‘Indeed! Then you have to make the acquaintance of many entrancing beauties yet. Forgive my impertinence, but if you are on a tour, let me recommend you to see the islands before you return to your home.’
“ ‘The South-Sea Islands, I presume you mean?’ I said.
“ ‘Yes; the bewitching islands of the Southern Seas! The most entrancingly beautiful spots on God’s beautiful earth! See them before you go. They will amply repay any trouble it may cost you to reach them.’
===Chinese===
“‘您对这幅图画想必下过一番细细的功夫，’默然片刻之后，我说道。
“‘也许下过，’他答道，‘我对海上生活深感兴趣——比我更甚的人寥寥无几。您在新南威尔士是生客吧？’
“‘十足的生客，’我答道，‘我几天前才到澳大利亚。’
“‘是吗！那您还有许多迷人的景致要一一结识呢。恕我冒昧，您若是在游历，我劝您回国之前务必去看看那些岛屿。’
“‘您说的是南洋诸岛吧？’我说。
“‘正是；南方大海上那些勾魂摄魄的岛屿！上帝这美丽大地上最教人心醉神迷的所在！趁走之前去看看吧。哪怕路途再费周折，它们也必不让您白跑一场。’

===Original===
“ ‘I should like to see them very much,’ I answered, feeling my enthusiasm rising at his words.
“ ‘Perhaps you are interested in them already,’ he continued.
“ ‘Very much indeed,’ I replied.
“ ‘Then, in that case, I may not be considered presumptuous if I offer to assist you. I am an old South-Sea merchant myself, and I have amassed a large collection of beautiful objects from the islands. If you would allow me the pleasure I should be delighted to show them to you.’
“ ‘I should like to see them very much indeed,’ I answered, thinking it extremely civil of him to make the offer.
“ ‘If you have time we might perhaps go and overhaul them now. My house is but a short distance from the Domain, and my carriage is waiting at the gates.’
===Chinese===
“‘我倒是巴不得去看看，’我答道，他的话让我心头热了起来。
“‘也许您现在就已对它们心怀关切，’他接着说道。
“‘实在关切得很，’我答道。
“‘既然如此，我若自告奋勇助您一臂之力，想必不算僭越。我自己就是个做了多年的南洋商人，从各岛搜罗了一大批精美的物件。您若肯赏光，我非常乐意拿给您开开眼界。’
“‘那我可真是非常想看看，’我答道，心想他提出这个，实在是客气极了。
“‘您若得空，咱们这会儿便可以去过过目。舍下离多曼公园不远，我的马车正在园门口候着。’

===Original===
“ ‘I shall be delighted,’ I said, thinking there could be no possible harm in my accepting his courteous invitation.
“ ‘But before we go, may I be allowed to introduce myself,’ the old gentleman said, taking a card-case from his pocket and withdrawing a card. This he handed to me, and on it I read﻿—
> “ ‘Mr. Mathew Draper.
“ ‘I am afraid I have no card to offer you in return,’ I said; ‘but I am the Marquis of Beckenham.’
“ ‘Indeed! Then I am doubly honoured,’ the old gentleman said with a low bow. ‘Now shall we wend our way up towards my carriage?’
===Chinese===
“‘那我求之不得，’我说，心想接受他这番殷勤的邀约，断不至于有什么祸事。
“‘不过动身之前，可否容我自我引见？’老先生说着，从口袋里取出一只名片盒，抽出一张名片。他把名片递到我手上，只见上面写着——
> “马修·德雷珀先生
“‘可惜我没法回敬您一张名片，’我说，‘不过我是贝肯汉姆侯爵。’
“‘是吗！那我真是倍感荣幸了，’老先生说着深深一躬，‘那么现在，咱们这就往马车那边走？’

===Original===
“We did so, chatting as we went. At the gates a neat brougham was waiting for us, and in it we took our places.
“ ‘Home,’ cried my host, and forthwith we set off down the street. Up one thoroughfare and down another we passed, until I lost all count of our direction. Throughout the drive my companion talked away in his best style; commented on the architecture of the houses, had many queer stories to tell of the passersby, and in many other ways kept my attention engaged till the carriage came to a standstill before a small but pretty villa in a quiet street.
“Mr. Draper immediately alighted, and when I had done so, dismissed his coachman, who drove away as we passed through the little garden and approached the dwelling. The front door was opened by a dignified manservant, and we entered. The hall, which was a spacious one for so small a dwelling, was filled with curios and weapons, but I had small time for observing them, as my host led me towards a room at the back. As we entered it he said, ‘I make you welcome to my house, my lord. I hope, now that you have taken the trouble to come, I shall be able to show you something that will repay your visit.’ Thereupon, bidding me seat myself for a few moments, he excused himself and left the room. When he returned he began to do the honours of the apartment. First we examined a rack of Australian spears, nulla-nullas, and boomerangs, then another containing New Zealand hatchets and clubs. After this we crossed to a sort of alcove where reposed in cases a great number of curios collected from the further islands of the Pacific. I was about to take up one of these when the door on the other side of the room opened and someone entered. At first I did not look round, but hearing the newcomer approaching me I turned, to find myself, to my horrified surprise, face to face with no less a person than Dr. Nikola. He was dressed entirely in black, his coat was buttoned and displayed all the symmetry of his peculiar figure, while his hair seemed blacker and his complexion even paler than before. He had evidently been prepared for my visit, for he held out his hand and greeted me without a sign of astonishment upon his face.
===Chinese===
“我们依言而行，一路闲谈。园门口正候着一辆整洁的四轮轿式马车，我们便上车坐定。
“‘回家，’我的主人喊了一声，马车随即沿街启程。我们穿了一条大街又一条大街，直到把方向全然迷失。一路上，我的同伴施展浑身解数侃侃而谈：品评沿路房屋的建筑式样，讲了许多关于行人的古怪轶事，变着法儿把我的注意力牢牢拴住，直到马车在一条僻静小街上一座小巧精致的别墅前停了下来。
“德雷珀先生随即下车，待我下来之后，便打发走了车夫；车夫驾车离去时，我们正穿过小花园，朝宅子走去。前门由一位仪表庄重的男仆打开，我们进了屋。门厅对这样一座小宅而言可谓宽敞，四下摆满了古玩和兵器，我却无暇细看，因为主人领着我朝后部一个房间走去。进门时他说：‘欢迎光临寒舍，大人。您既劳步前来，但愿我能让您看到些东西，不枉此一行。’说罢，他请我稍坐片刻，便告个便离了房间。待他回来，便开始尽地主之谊。我们先看了一架澳大利亚的长矛、努拉努拉木棒（nulla-nulla）和回力镖，又看了另一架，里面是新西兰的短斧和棍棒。看完这些，我们走向一处类似壁龛的所在，玻璃柜里安放着大批采自太平洋更远岛屿的古玩。我正要拿起其中一件，房间另一头的门开了，有人走了进来。起初我没有回头，可听得来人朝我走近，我便转过身去，又惊又骇地发现，站在我面前的不是别人，正是尼古拉博士。他通身黑衣，外套扣得严严实实，衬出他那奇特身形的全部匀称；他的头发似乎比先前更黑，面色也比先前更加苍白。他显然早有准备、专候我的来访，只见他伸出手来，脸上不见一丝惊讶地招呼我。

===Original===
“ ‘This is indeed a pleasure, my lord,’ he said, still with his hand outstretched, looking hard at me with his peculiar catlike eyes. ‘I did not expect to see you again so soon. And you are evidently a little surprised at meeting me.’
“ ‘I am more than surprised,’ I answered bitterly, seeing how easily I had been entrapped. ‘I am horribly mortified and angry. Mr. Draper, you had an easy victim.’
“Mr. Draper said nothing, but Dr. Nikola dropped into a chair and spoke for him.
“ ‘You must not blame my old friend Draper,’ he said suavely. ‘We have been wondering for the last twenty-four hours how we might best get hold of you, and the means we have employed so successfully seemed the only possible way. Have no fear, my lord, you shall not be hurt. In less than twenty-four hours you will enjoy the society of your energetic friend Mr. Hatteras again.’
“ ‘What is your reason for abducting me like this?’ I asked. ‘You are foolish to do so, for Mr. Hatteras will leave no stone unturned to find me.’
===Chinese===
“‘这真是幸会，大人，’他说道，手依旧伸着，用他那双古怪的猫眼紧紧盯着我，‘没想到这么快就又见到您了。看来，同我重逢让您颇为吃惊。’
“‘何止是吃惊，’我辛酸地答道，眼看自己这么轻易就落入了圈套，‘我是又羞又恼，难受极了。德雷珀先生，您下手的这个目标，可真容易。’
“德雷珀先生一言不发，尼古拉博士却在一张椅子上落了座，替他开了口。
“‘您可不能怪我的老朋友德雷珀，’他温文尔雅地说，‘这二十四小时里，我们一直在盘算怎样把您弄到手最妥当，而这条已经大获成功的计策，看来是唯一可行的法子。别怕，大人，您不会受到伤害。不出二十四小时，您就又能同您那位精力过人的朋友哈特拉斯先生做伴了。’
“‘你们这样绑走我，究竟为的什么？’我问道，‘你们这么做未免愚蠢，因为哈特拉斯先生为了找我，会把每一块石头都翻过来。’

===Original===
“ ‘I do not doubt that at all,’ said Dr. Nikola quietly; ‘but I think Mr. Hatteras will find he will have all his work cut out for him this time.’
“ ‘If you imagine that your plans are not known in Sydney you are mistaken,’ I cried. ‘The farce you are playing at Government House is detected, and Mr. Hatteras, directly he finds I am lost, will go to Lord Amberley and reveal everything.’
“ ‘I have not the slightest objection,’ returned Dr. Nikola quietly. ‘By the time Mr. Hatteras can take those steps﻿—indeed, by the time he discovers your absence at all, we shall be beyond the reach of his vengeance.’
“I could not follow his meaning, of course, but while he had been speaking I had been looking stealthily round me for a means of escape. The only way out of the room was, of course, by the door, but both Nikola and his ally were between me and that. Then a big stone hatchet hanging on the wall near me caught my eye. Hardly had I seen it before an idea flashed through my brain. Supposing I seized it and fought my way out. The door of the room stood open, and I noticed with delight that the key was in the lock on the outside. One rush, armed with the big hatchet, would take me into the passage; then before my foes could recover their wits I might be able to turn the key, and, having locked them in, make my escape from the house before I could be stopped.
===Chinese===
“‘这一点我毫不怀疑，’尼古拉博士平静地说，‘不过我想，哈特拉斯先生这回会发现，他要应付的差事可不轻。’
“‘你们要是以为自己的图谋在悉尼无人知晓，那就错了，’我喊道，‘你们在总督府演的那出把戏已经败露，哈特拉斯先生一发现我失踪，立刻就会去见安伯利勋爵，把一切和盘托出。’
“‘我毫无异议，’尼古拉博士平静地答道，‘等哈特拉斯先生来得及采取那些步骤时——不，等他发现您失踪时——我们早已在他复仇之力所能及的范围之外了。’
“他的话我自然听不懂，可趁他说话的当儿，我一直在偷偷四下打量，寻找脱身之计。出屋的路当然只有那扇门，可尼古拉和他的同党正守在我与门之间。这时，挂在我近旁墙上的一柄大石斧映入我的眼帘。我一见它，脑中便闪过一个念头：假如我抄起它，杀出一条路去呢？房门敞着，而且我欣喜地注意到，钥匙就插在外面的锁眼里。握着这柄大石斧一冲，便能冲进过道；趁仇敌还没回过神来，我也许来得及转动钥匙，把他们反锁在屋里，然后赶在被拦下之前逃出宅子。

===Original===
“Without another thought I made up my mind, sprang to the wall, wrenched down the hatchet, and prepared for my rush. But by the time I had done it both Nikola and Draper were on their feet.
“ ‘Out of my way!’ I cried, raising my awful weapon aloft. ‘Stop me at your peril!’
“With my hatchet in the air I looked at Nikola. He was standing rigidly erect, with one arm outstretched, the hand pointing at me. His eyes glared like living coals, and when he spoke his voice came from between his teeth like a serpent’s hiss.
“ ‘Put down that axe!’ he said.
“With that the old horrible fear of him which had seized me on board ship came over me again. His eyes fascinated me so that I could not look away from them. I put down the hatchet without another thought. Still he gazed at me in the same hideous fashion.
“ ‘Sit down in that chair,’ he said quietly. ‘You cannot disobey me.’ And indeed I could not. My heart was throbbing painfully, and an awful dizziness was creeping over me. Still I could not get away from those terrible eyes. They seemed to be growing larger and fiercer every moment. Oh! I can feel the horror of them even now. As I gazed his white right hand was moving to and fro before me with regular sweeps, and with each one I felt my own will growing weaker and weaker. That I was being mesmerised, I had no doubt, but if I had been going to be murdered I could not have moved a finger to save myself.
===Chinese===
“我不再多想，拿定主意，纵身扑向墙壁，一把拽下石斧，便要冲锋。可不等我动作完，尼古拉和德雷珀都已站了起来。
“‘让开！’我喊道，把那件可怕的武器高高举起，‘要拦我，就自己掂量掂量！’
“我举着石斧，眼睛盯住尼古拉。他直挺挺地站着，一条胳膊伸出，手直指着我。他双目灼灼，如烧红的炭火，开口时，声音从齿缝里挤出来，好似毒蛇吐信。
“‘把斧子放下！’他说。
“这一声一出，在船上曾攫住我的那种对他的可怕恐惧，又笼罩了我。他的眼睛像施了魔法，教我无法把目光移开。我想也不想，便放下了石斧。他依旧用那副骇人的模样凝视着我。
“‘到那把椅子上坐下，’他平静地说，‘您不能违抗我。’而我也当真不能。我的心怦怦狂跳，痛得难受，一阵可怕的眩晕悄悄袭上身来。我依旧摆脱不开那双可怕的眼睛，它们仿佛时时刻刻都在变大、变凶。唉！直到现在，我一想还能觉出那份恐怖。我望着望着，他那只白皙的右手开始在我面前规律地来回摆动，每摆一下，我便觉得自己的意志又弱了一分。我毫不怀疑，自己正被人催眠，可即便接下来等着我的是谋杀，我也动不了一根手指自救。

===Original===
“Then there came a sudden but imperative knock at the door, and both Nikola and Draper rose. Next moment the man whom we had noticed in the train as we came up from Melbourne, and against whom you, Mr. Hatteras, had warned me in Sydney, entered the room. He crossed and stood respectfully before Nikola.
“ ‘Well, Mr. Eastover, what news?’ asked the latter. ‘Have you done what I told you?’
“ ‘Everything,’ the man answered, taking an envelope from his pocket. ‘Here is the letter you wanted.’
“Nikola took it from his subordinate’s hand, broke the seal, and having withdrawn the contents, read it carefully. All this time, seeing resistance was quite useless, I did not move. I felt too sick and giddy for anything. When he had finished his correspondence Nikola said something in an undertone to Draper, who immediately left the room. During the time he was absent none of us spoke. Presently he returned, bringing with him a wine glass filled with water, which he presented to Nikola.
“ ‘Thank you,’ said that gentleman, feeling in his waistcoat pocket. Presently he found what he wanted and produced what looked like a small silver scent-bottle. Unscrewing the top, he poured from it into the wine glass a few drops of some dark-coloured liquid. Having done this he smelt it carefully and then handed it to me.
“ ‘I must ask you to drink this, my lord,’ he said. ‘You need have no fear of the result: it is perfectly harmless.’
===Chinese===
“就在这时，门口响起一阵急促而不容分说的叩门声，尼古拉和德雷珀双双起身。紧接着，那个人——我们从墨尔本北上的火车上就留意到的、您在悉尼还特意提醒我提防的那个人，哈特拉斯先生——走进了房间。他径直走到尼古拉面前，恭恭敬敬地站定。
“‘怎么样，伊斯特弗先生，有什么消息？’尼古拉问道，‘我吩咐的事都办妥了？’
“‘都办妥了，’那人答道，从口袋里掏出一只信封，‘您要的信在这儿。’
“尼古拉从手下人手里接过信，拆开封口，抽出信纸，仔细读了一遍。这其间，我看出抵抗全然无用，便一动没动；我只觉得恶心眩晕，什么事也做不得。他读完信，低声对德雷珀吩咐了一句，德雷珀立刻出了房间。他不在的这段时间，我们谁都没有说话。不久他回来了，端着一只斟满清水的酒杯，呈给尼古拉。
“‘多谢，’那位先生说着，往马甲口袋里摸。不一会儿，他摸到了要找的东西，掏出一只看上去像小银香水瓶的物件。拧开瓶盖，他往酒杯里滴了几滴深色的液体。做完这个，他仔细嗅了嗅，然后把杯子递给我。
“‘我必须请您把这个喝下去，大人，’他说，‘结果如何您全不必担心：它绝对无害。’

===Original===
“Did ever man hear such a cool proposition? Very naturally I declined to do as he wished.
“ ‘You must drink it!’ he reiterated. ‘Pray do so at once. I have no time to waste bandying words with you.’
“ ‘I will not drink it!’ I cried, rising to my feet, and prepared to make a fight for it if need should be.
“Once more those eyes grew terrible, and once more that hand began to make the passes before my face. Again I felt the dizziness stealing over me. His will was growing every moment too strong for me. I could not resist him. So when he once more said, ‘Drink!’ I took the glass and did as I was ordered. After that I remember seeing Nikola, Draper, and the man they called Eastover engaged in earnest conversation on the other side of the room. I remember Nikola crossing to where I sat and gazing steadfastly into my face, and after that I recollect no more until I came to my senses in this room, to find myself bound and gagged. For what seemed like hours I lay in agony, then I heard footsteps in the verandah, and next moment the sound of voices. I tried to call for help, but could utter no words. I thought you would go away without discovering me, but fortunately for me you did not do so. Now, Mr. Hatteras, I have told you everything; you know my story from the time you left me up to the present moment.”
===Chinese===
“天底下可有人听过这样冷冰冰的提议？我自然一口回绝，不肯照办。
“‘您必须喝下去！’他又说了一遍，‘请立刻就喝。我可没有工夫跟您磨嘴皮子。’
“‘我绝不喝！’我喊道，站起身来，准备必要时拼上一场。
“那双眼睛又一次变得凶怖，那只手又开始在我面前比划施术。眩晕又一次悄悄爬上我的头顶，他的意志一分一秒地压过我的。我抗拒不了他。于是，当他再说一声‘喝’时，我便接过杯子，奉命喝了下去。此后我记得的，是尼古拉、德雷珀和那个他们称作伊斯特弗的人在房间另一头凝神交谈；记得尼古拉走到我坐的地方，久久凝视我的脸；再往后，我就什么也不记得了，直到我在这间屋子里苏醒过来，发现自己被捆了手脚、堵了嘴。我在剧痛中躺着，仿佛熬了几个钟头，随后听见游廊里响起脚步声，紧接着是人声。我想呼救，却发不出一点声音。我还当你们会发现不了我、就此走掉了呢，所幸你们没有。好了，哈特拉斯先生，我把一切都告诉您了；从我同您分手那时起，直到此刻，我的经历您全都知道了。”

===Original===
For some time after the Marquis had concluded his strange story both the Inspector and I sat in deep thought. That Beckenham had been kidnapped in order that he should be out of the way while the villainous plot for abducting Phyllis was being enacted there could be no doubt. But why had he been chosen? and what clues were we to gather from what he had told us? I turned to the Inspector and said﻿—
“What do you think will be the best course for us to pursue now?”
“I have been wondering myself. I think, as there is nothing to be learned from this house, the better plan would be for you two gentleman to go back to Mr. Wetherell, while I return to the detective office and see if anything has been discovered by the men there. As soon as I have found out I will join you at Potts Point. What do you think?”
I agreed that it would be the best course; so, taking the Marquis by the arms (for he was still too weak to walk alone), we left the house and were about to step into the street when I stopped, and asking them to wait for me ran back into the room again. In the corner, just as it had been thrown down, lay the rope with which Beckenham had been bound and the pad which had been fitted over his mouth. I picked both up and carried them into the verandah.
===Chinese===
侯爵讲完他这段离奇的经历之后，我和督察都沉入了深深的思索，好一会儿没有作声。贝肯汉姆之所以遭绑，为的是在绑架菲莉斯的毒计得逞之时把他支开，这一点毫无疑义。可为什么偏偏挑中他？从他的讲述里，我们又能摸到什么线索？我转向督察，说道——
“依你看，咱们现在最好采取什么步骤？”
“我自己也正犯嘀咕。我想，这宅子里既然再问不出什么，你们两位先生不如回韦瑟雷尔先生那儿去，我则回侦探处，看看那边的人查出什么名堂没有。一有结果，我立刻到波茨角与你们会合。你们看如何？”
我赞成这确是上策；于是我们架着侯爵的双臂（他仍旧虚弱得独自行走不得），离开了那所宅子。正要跨到街上时，我却停了下来，请他们稍等，自己跑回房间里去。墙角里，捆过贝肯汉姆的那根绳子和塞他嘴的那块垫布，还照原样丢在那儿。我把两样都拾起来，拿到游廊上。

===Original===
“Come here, Mr. Inspector,” I cried. “I thought I should learn something from this. Take a look at this rope and this pad, and tell me what you make of them.”
He took each up in turn and looked them over and over. But he only shook his head.
“I don’t see anything to guide us,” he said as he laid them down again.
“Don’t you?” I cried. “Why, they tell me more than I have learnt from anything else I’ve seen. Look at the two ends of this.” (Here I took up the rope and showed it to him.) “They’re seized!”
I looked triumphantly at him, but he only stared at me in surprise, and said, “What do you mean by ‘seized?’ ”
“Why, I mean that the ends are bound up in this way﻿—look for yourself. Now not one landsman in a hundred seizes a rope’s end. This line was taken from some ship in the harbour, and﻿—By Jove! here’s another discovery!”
===Chinese===
“督察先生，请过来，”我喊道，“我就琢磨着能从这里面看出点门道。瞧瞧这根绳子、这块垫布，告诉我您能看出什么。”
他把两样东西轮流拿起，翻来覆去看了又看，却只是摇头。
“我看不出什么能指路的门道。”他把东西放下时说道。
“您看不出？”我喊道，“嗐，它们告诉我的，比我到现在为止见到的任何东西都多。瞧这绳子的两头。”（说着，我拾起绳子递给他看）“两头都缠扎过！”
我得意地望着他，他却只一脸惊讶地瞪着我，说道：“您说的‘缠扎’（seized）是什么意思？”
“嗐，我是说，绳头是照这样捆缠起来的——您自己瞧。一百个岸上人里，也未必有一个会这样缠扎绳头。这根绳是从港里哪条船上取下来的，而且——天哪！又有新发现了！”

===Original===
“What now?” he cried, being by this time almost as excited as I was myself.
“Why, look here,” I said, holding the middle of the rope up to the light, so that we could get a better view of it. “Not very many hours ago this rope was running through a block, and that block was rather an uncommon one.”
“How do you know that it was an uncommon one?”
“Because it has been newly painted, and what’s funnier still, painted green, of all other colours. Look at this streak of paint along the line; see how it’s smudged. Now let’s review the case as we walk along.”
So saying, with the Marquis between us, we set off down the street, hoping to be able to pick up an early cab.
===Chinese===
“又发现什么了？”他喊道，这时他差不多已经和我一样兴奋了。
“嗐，您瞧这儿，”我说着，把绳子的中段举到光线下，好教我们看得更清楚，“不出几个钟头之前，这根绳子还穿在一只滑车里跑动，而那只滑车，相当少见。”
“您怎么知道它少见？”
“因为它刚上过漆，而更古怪的是，偏偏漆成了绿色，什么颜色不好选。瞧绳身上这道漆痕；瞧它蹭花了的样子。来，咱们边走边把案情捋一遍。”
说罢，我们让侯爵居中，沿街出发，指望能赶早截到一辆马车。

===Original===
“First and foremost,” I said, “remember old Draper’s talk of the South Seas﻿—remember the collection of curios he possessed. Probably he owns a schooner, and it’s more than probable that this line and this bit of canvas came from it.”
“I see what you’re driving at,” said the Inspector. “It’s worth considering. Directly I get to the office I will set men to work to try and find this mysterious gentleman. You would know him again, my lord?”
“I should know him anywhere,” was Beckenham’s immediate reply.
“And have you any idea at all where this house, to which he conducted you, is located?”
“None at all. I only know that it was about halfway down a street of which all the houses, save the one at the corner﻿—which was a grocer’s shop﻿—were one-storeyed villas.”
“Nothing a little more definite, I suppose?”
===Chinese===
“头一件，”我说，“别忘了老德雷珀那些关于南洋的谈论——别忘了他那批古玩收藏。他多半自己有条纵帆船，这根绳、这块帆布，八成就是那船上来的。”
“我明白您的意思了，”督察说，“这值得琢磨。我一到署里，就派人去查这位神秘的先生。大人，您再见着他，还认得出来吧？”
“他走到哪儿我都认得。”贝肯汉姆应声答道。
“那他领您去的那所宅子，您可有一点印象，究竟坐落在什么地方？”
“一点没有。我只记得，那街约莫走到一半才是那宅子，沿街的房子，除了街角上一家——那是家杂货铺——清一色是单层的小别墅。”
“再具体些的，就想不起来了？”

===Original===
“Stay! I remember that there was an empty house with broken windows almost opposite, and that on either side of the steps leading up to the front door were two stone eagles with outstretched wings. The head of one of the eagles﻿—the left, I think﻿—was missing.”
The Inspector noted these things in his pocketbook, and just as he had finished we picked up a cab and called it to the sidewalk. When we had got in and given the driver Mr. Wetherell’s address, I said to the Inspector:
“What are you going to do first?”
“Put some men on to find Mr. Draper, and some more to find an island schooner with her blocks newly painted green.”
“You won’t be long in letting us know what you discover, will you?” I said. “Remember how anxious we are.”
“You may count on my coming to you at once with any news I may procure,” he answered.
===Chinese===
“等等！我想起来了，差不多正对面有座空宅，窗子都破了；通向正门的台阶两侧，立着两只石头雕的鹰，翅膀张开。其中一只鹰的头——我记得是左边那只——没了。”
督察把这些记进袖珍记事本，刚记完，我们便拦下一辆马车，把它唤到人行道旁。上车之后，我们把韦瑟雷尔先生的地址报给车夫，我对督察说：
“您打算先做什么？”
“派些人去找德雷珀先生，再派些人去找一条滑车刚漆成绿色的岛际纵帆船。”
“有什么发现，您可不会捂着不告诉我们吧？”我说，“别忘了我们多么心急如焚。”
“一有消息，我必定立刻来找你们，这点尽管放心。”他答道。

===Original===
A few moments later we drew up at Mr. Wetherell’s door. Bidding the Inspector goodbye we went up the steps and rang the bell. By the time the cab was out in the street again we were in the house making our way, behind the butler, to Mr. Wetherell’s study.
The old gentleman had not gone to bed, but sat just as I had left him so many hours before. As soon as we were announced he rose to receive us.
“Thank God, Mr. Hatteras, you have come back!” he said. “I have been in a perfect fever waiting for you. What have you to report?”
“Not very much, I’m afraid,” I answered. “But first let me have the pleasure of introducing the real Marquis of Beckenham to you, whom we have had the good fortune to find and rescue.”
Mr. Wetherell bowed gravely and held out his hand.
“My lord,” he said, “I am thankful that you have been discovered. I look upon it as one step towards the recovery of my poor girl. I hope now that both you and Mr. Hatteras will take up your abode with me during the remainder of your stay in the colony. You have had a scurvy welcome to New South Wales. We must see if we can’t make up to you for it. But you look thoroughly worn out; I expect you would like to go to bed.”
===Chinese===
片刻之后，马车停在了韦瑟雷尔先生门前。与督察道过别，我们走上台阶按铃。等马车重新驶上街心，我们已进了宅子，跟在管家身后，朝韦瑟雷尔先生的书房走去。
老先生没有就寝，还坐在我几个钟头前离开他时的原处。一听人通报，他便起身相迎。
“感谢上帝，哈特拉斯先生，您总算回来了！”他说，“等你们等得我心急如焚。查到什么了没有？”
“恐怕不算多，”我答道，“不过先容我有幸向您引见真正的贝肯汉姆侯爵——我们侥幸把他寻获并救了出来。”
韦瑟雷尔先生郑重一躬，伸出手来。
“大人，”他说，“您能被寻获，我感激不尽。我把这看作寻回我可怜的女儿的第一步。现在我希望，您和哈特拉斯先生在殖民地逗留的余下日子里，都搬到我这儿来住。您这趟新南威尔士，受到的款待实在寒碜，我们得想法子补偿您。不过您看上去已疲惫不堪，我猜您怕是想就寝了吧。”

===Original===
He rang the bell, and when his butler appeared, gave him some instructions about preparing rooms for us.
Ten minutes later the man returned and stated that our rooms were ready, whereupon Mr. Wetherell himself conducted Beckenham to the apartment assigned to him. When he returned to me, he asked if I would not like to retire too, but I would not hear of it. I could not have slept a wink, so great was my anxiety. Seeing this, he seated himself and listened attentively while I gave him an outline of Beckenham’s story. I had hardly finished before I heard a carriage roll up to the door. There was a ring at the bell, and presently the butler, who, like ourselves, had not dreamt of going to bed, though his master had repeatedly urged him to do so, entered and announced the Inspector.
Wetherell hobbled across to receive him with an anxious face.
“Have you any better tidings for me?” he asked.
“Not very much, I’m afraid, sir,” the Inspector said, shaking his head. “The best I have to tell you is that your carriage and horse have been found in the yard of an empty house off Pitt Street.”
===Chinese===
他摇了铃，管家应声而来，他便吩咐为我们收拾房间。
十分钟后，管家回话说房间已经备好，韦瑟雷尔先生便亲自领贝肯汉姆去分派给他的那间。他回到我这里，问我要不要也去歇息，我却坚决不肯。我心焦成那样，一分钟也合不上眼。他见状便坐下，聚精会神地听我把贝肯汉姆的遭遇大略讲了一遍。我话音刚落，就听得一辆马车辚辚驶到门前。门铃一响，不多时管家进来通报督察到了——这位管家和我们一样，压根儿没想过上床，虽说主人已再三催他去歇着。
韦瑟雷尔挂着满脸焦灼，蹒跚着过去迎他。
“有更好的消息给我吗？”他问。
“恐怕不太多，先生，”督察摇着头说，“我这里最好的消息是：您的马车和马，在皮特街（Pitt Street）旁边一座空宅的院子里找到了。”

===Original===
“Have you been able to discover any clue as to who put them there?”
“Not one! The horse was found out of the shafts tied to the wall. There was not a soul about the place.”
Wetherell sat down again and covered his face with his hands. At that instant the telephone bell in the corner of the room rang sharply. I jumped up and went across to it. Placing the receivers to my ears, I heard a small voice say, “Is that Mr. Wetherell’s house, Potts Point?”
“Yes,” I answered.
“Who is speaking?”
“Mr. Hatteras. Mr. Wetherell, however, is in the room. Who are you?”
===Chinese===
“查出什么线索没有，是谁把它们弄到那儿的？”
“一点没有！马已卸下车辕，拴在墙上。那地方一个人影也没有。”
韦瑟雷尔重新坐下，双手掩面。就在这时，屋角的电话铃声尖锐地响起。我跳起身走过去，把听筒贴上耳朵，听见一个细小的声音说：“是波茨角的韦瑟雷尔先生府上吗？”
“是，”我答道。
“您是哪位？”
“哈特拉斯。韦瑟雷尔先生就在房里。你是谁？”

===Original===
“Detective officer. Will you tell Mr. Wetherell that Mr. Draper’s house has been discovered?”
I communicated the message to Mr. Wetherell, and then the Inspector joined me at the instrument and spoke.
“Where is the house?” he enquired.
“83, Charlemagne Street﻿—north side.”
“Very good. Inspector Murdkin speaking. Let plain clothes men be stationed at either end of the street, and tell them to be on the look out for Draper, and to wait for me. I’ll start for the house at once.”
“Very good, sir.”
===Chinese===
“侦探警官。请转告韦瑟雷尔先生，德雷珀先生的宅子已经找到了。”
我把话转告韦瑟雷尔先生，督察也走到电话机前接过话头。
“宅子在哪儿？”他问道。
“查理曼街（Charlemagne Street） 83 号——北侧。”
“很好。我是默德金督察（Murdkin）。派便衣守住街两头，叫他们盯紧德雷珀，等我来。我马上动身去那宅子。”
“是，先生。”

===Original===
He rang off and then turned to me.
“Are you too tired to come with me, Mr. Hatteras?” he enquired.
“Of course not,” I answered. “Let us go at once.”
“God bless you!” said Wetherell. “I hope you may catch the fellow.”
Bidding him goodbye, we went downstairs again, and jumped into the cab, which was directed to the street in question.
===Chinese===
他挂断电话，转过身来。
“哈特拉斯先生，您累不累，还能随我去一趟吗？”他问道。
“当然去，”我答道，“咱们马上动身。”
“上帝保佑你们！”韦瑟雷尔说，“但愿你们能逮住那家伙。”
与他道别后，我们再次下楼，跳上马车，直奔所说的那条街。

===Original===
Though it was a good distance from our starting-point, in less than half an hour we had pulled up at the corner. As the cab stopped, a tall man, dressed in blue serge, who had been standing near the lamppost, came forward and touched his hat.
“Good morning, Williams,” said the Inspector. “Any sign of our man?”
“Not one, sir. He hasn’t come down the street since I’ve been here.”
“Very good. Now come along and we’ll pay the house a visit.”
So saying he told the cabman to follow us slowly, and we proceeded down the street. About halfway along he stopped and pointed to a house on the opposite side.
“That is the house his lordship mentioned, with the broken windows, and this is where Mr. Draper dwells, if I am not much mistaken﻿—see the eagles are on either side of the steps, just as described.”
===Chinese===
虽然离出发的地方颇有一段路，不到半个钟头，我们已在街角停了下来。马车一停，一个穿蓝色哔叽的高个子从路灯柱旁迎上前来，碰了碰帽檐。
“早上好，威廉斯，”督察说，“看见咱们要找的人了吗？”
“一点没有，先生。打我守在这儿起，他没在街上露过面。”
“很好。这就来吧，咱们去拜访那座宅子。”
说罢，他吩咐车夫慢慢跟着，我们便沿街走去。走到半途，他停住脚步，指向对面的一座房子。
“那就是大人提到的那座破窗空宅，而这一座，如果我没大看错，便是德雷珀先生的住处——瞧，台阶两边正是一对石鹰，同描述的分毫不差。”

===Original===
It was exactly as Beckenham had told us, even to the extent of the headless eagle on the left of the walk. It was a pretty little place, and evidently still occupied, as a maid was busily engaged cleaning the steps.
Pushing open the gate, the Inspector entered the little garden and accosted the girl.
“Good morning,” he said politely. “Pray, is your master at home?”
“Yes, sir; he’s at breakfast just now.”
“Well, would you mind telling him that two gentlemen would like to see him?”
“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
一切正如贝肯汉姆所言，连小路左边那只缺头的鹰也对得上。这是座漂亮的小宅，而且显然仍有人住，因为一个女佣正忙着刷洗台阶。
督察推开院门，走进小花园，上前同那姑娘搭话。
“早上好，”他客气地说，“请问，您家主人在家吗？”
“在的，先生；他这会儿正用早餐。”
“那么，可否劳驾禀报一声，说有两位先生想见他？”
“好的，先生。”

===Original===
The girl rose to her feet, and, wiping her hands on her apron, led the way into the house. We followed close behind her. Then, asking us to wait a moment where we were, she knocked at a door on the right and opening it, disappeared within.
“Now,” said the Inspector, “our man will probably appear, and we shall have him nicely.”
The Inspector had scarcely spoken before the door opened again, and a man came out. To our surprise, however, he was very tall and stout, with a round, jovial face, and a decided air of being satisfied with himself and the world in general.
“To what do I owe the honour of this visit?” he said, looking at the Inspector.
===Chinese===
姑娘站起身，在围裙上擦了擦手，领路进屋。我们紧随其后。她请我们在原地稍候，便敲了敲右边一扇门，推门进去了。
“好了，”督察说，“咱们的人多半就要露面了，正好把他稳稳拿住。”
话音未落，门又开了，走出来一个男人。不料他却又高又壮，一张圆滚滚的和气脸，浑身透着一股对自己和整个世界都心满意足的派头。
“不知二位今日枉驾，有何贵干？”他望着督察说。

===Original===
“I am an Inspector of Police, as you see,” answered my companion, “and we are looking for a man named Draper, who yesterday was in possession of this house.”
“I am afraid you have made some little mistake,” returned the other.
“I am the occupier of this house, and have been for some months past. No Mr. Draper has anything at all to do with it.”
The Inspector’s face was a study for perfect bewilderment. Nor could mine have been much behind it. The Marquis had given such a minute description of the dwelling opposite and the two stone birds on the steps, that there could be no room for doubt that this was the house. And yet it was physically impossible that this man could be Draper; and, if it were the place where Beckenham had been drugged, why were the weapons, etc., he had described not in the hall?
“I cannot understand it at all,” said the Inspector, turning to me. “This is the house, and yet where are the things with which it ought to be furnished?”
===Chinese===
“您也看见了，我是警察督察，”我的同伴答道，“我们在找一个姓德雷珀的人，昨天这宅子还在他手里。”
“恐怕您是有点儿弄错了，”对方答道。
“我才是这宅子的住户，好几个月以来一直是。什么德雷珀先生，跟它毫无干系。”
督察脸上那份彻底的茫然，简直就是“不知所措”四字的最佳注脚。我的脸色恐怕也相去不远。侯爵把对面的宅子和台阶上那两只石鸟描述得那样细致，断无认错宅子的道理。可这人又无论如何不可能是德雷珀；再说，倘若这就是贝肯汉姆被下药的所在，他描述过的那些兵器等等，怎么都不在门厅里呢？
“我实在闹不明白了，”督察转向我说，“宅子是这座没错，可那些该摆着的物件，都上哪儿去了？”

===Original===
“You have a description of the furniture, then?” said the owner. “That is good, for it will enable me to prove to you even more clearly that you are mistaken. Pray come and see my sitting-rooms for yourselves.”
He led the way into the apartment from which he had been summoned, and we followed him. It was small and nicely furnished, but not a South-Sea curio or native weapon was to be seen in it. Then we followed him to the corresponding room at the back of the house. This was upholstered in the latest fashion; but again there was no sign of what Beckenham had led us to expect we should find. We were completely nonplussed.
“I am afraid we have troubled you without cause,” said the Inspector, as we passed out into the hall again.
“Don’t mention it,” the owner answered; “I find my compensation in the knowledge that I am not involved in any police unpleasantness.”
“By the way,” said the Inspector suddenly, “have you any idea who your neighbours may be?”
===Chinese===
“这么说，你们连屋里家具的样子都说得上？”主人说，“那倒好，正好让我把话向二位说得更明白：你们弄错了。请亲自来看看我的起居室吧。”
他领路走进方才被叫出来的那个房间，我们跟着。房间不大，陈设雅致，却连一件南洋古玩、一件土著兵器也看不到。接着我们又跟他去看宅子后部相对的那间，那里装潢入时，却依旧不见贝肯汉姆让我们指望找到的任何踪影。我们全给弄懵了。
走回门厅时，督察说道：“恐怕是我们无端搅扰了您。”
“哪里的话，”主人答道，“得知自己没有沾上什么警务上的麻烦，于我就是补偿了。”
“对了，”督察突然说，“您的左邻右舍都是些什么人，您可知道？”

===Original===
“Oh, dear, yes!” the man replied. “On my right I have a frigidly respectable widow of Low Church tendencies. On my left, the Chief Teller of the Bank of New Holland. Both very worthy members of society, and not at all the sort of people to be criminally inclined.”
“In that case we can only apologize for our intrusion and wish you good morning.”
“Pray don’t apologise. I should have been glad to have assisted you. Good morning.”
We went down the steps again and out into the street. As we passed through the gate, the Inspector stopped and examined a mark on the right hand post. Then he stooped and picked up what looked like a pebble. Having done so we resumed our walk.
“What on earth can be the meaning of it all?” I asked. “Can his lordship have made a mistake?”
“No, I think not. We have been cleverly duped, that’s all.”
===Chinese===
“嗐，太知道了！”那人答道，“我右边住着一位冷面古板、循规蹈矩的寡妇，偏于低教会派（Low Church）；左边是新荷兰银行（Bank of New Holland）的出纳主任。两位都是极体面的社会中人，全然不像有作奸犯科习气的人。”
“那样的话，我们只好为唐突打扰告罪一声，并祝您早安。”
“请不必告罪。能帮上你们的忙，我倒是乐意的。早安。”
我们再次走下台阶，出到街上。穿过院门时，督察停住脚，察看右门柱上的一道印痕，又俯身拾起一样看似小石子的东西。办完这些，我们继续前行。
“这一切究竟是怎么回事？”我问，“会不会是大人记岔了？”
“不，我看不是。咱们是叫人巧妙地耍了，仅此而已。”

===Original===
“What makes you think so?”
“I didn’t think so until we passed through the gate on our way out. Now I’m certain of it. Come across the street.”
I followed him across the road to a small plain-looking house, with a neatly-curtained bow window and a brass plate on the front door. From the latter I discovered that the proprietress of the place was a dressmaker, but I was completely at a loss to understand why we were visiting her.
As soon as the door was opened the Inspector asked if Miss Tiffins were at home, and, on being told that she was, enquired if we might see her. The maid went away to find out, and presently returned and begged us to follow her. We did so down a small passage towards the door of the room which contained the bow window.
Miss Tiffins was a lady of uncertain age, with a prim, precise manner, and corkscrew curls. She seemed at a loss to understand our errand, but bade us be seated, and then asked in what way she could be of service to us.
===Chinese===
“您凭什么这样想？”
“没出院门的时候，我本还没这么想。这会儿我拿准了。随我到街对面来。”
我随他穿过马路，来到一座外表朴素的小宅前：凸窗悬着整洁的窗帘，正门上钉着一块铜牌。从铜牌上我得知，这宅子的女主人是位裁缝；可我们为何要来拜访她，我却全然捉摸不透。
门一开，督察便问蒂芬斯小姐（Miss Tiffins）在不在家，听说在，又问可否一见。女仆进去请示，不多时回来，请我们随她去。我们随她穿过一条小过道，朝那间带凸窗的房间的房门走去。
蒂芬斯小姐是位年纪难以捉摸的女士，举止拘谨刻板，鬓边垂着螺旋形的发卷。她似乎弄不清我们的来意，但仍请我们落座，随后问我们有何事需要她效劳。

===Original===
“In the first place, madam,” said the Inspector, “let me tell you that I am an officer of police. A serious crime has been perpetrated, and I have reason to believe that it may be in your power to give us a clue to the persons who committed it.”
“You frighten me, sir,” replied the lady. “I cannot at all see in what way I can help you. I lead a life of the greatest quietness. How, therefore, can I know anything of such people?”
“I do not wish to imply that you do know anything of them. I only want you to carry your memory back as far as yesterday, and to answer me the few simple questions I may ask you.”
“I will answer them to the best of my ability.”
“Well, in the first place, may I ask if you remember seeing a brougham drive up to that house opposite about midday yesterday?”
“No, I cannot say that I do,” the old lady replied after a moment’s consideration.
===Chinese===
“首先，夫人，”督察说，“请容我告知，我是名警官。眼下发生了一桩重案，我有理由相信，您或许能为我们提供作案者的线索。”
“您吓着我了，先生，”女士答道，“我全然想不出自己能帮上什么忙。我过的日子再清静不过，又怎么会认识那种人呢？”
“我并非暗示您认识他们。我只求您把记忆往回想，回到昨天，回答我几个简单的问题。”
“我定当尽力回答。”
“那么首先，请问您可记得，昨天正午时分，有一辆四轮轿式马车驶到对面那座宅子门前？”
“不，可说不上记得，”老太太沉吟片刻后答道。

===Original===
“Do you remember seeing a number of men leave the house during the afternoon?”
“No. If they came out I did not notice them.”
“Now, think for one moment, if you please, and tell me what vehicles, if any, you remember seeing stop there.”
“Let me try to remember. There was Judge’s baker’s cart, about three, the milk about five, and a furniture van about half-past six.”
“That’s just what I want to know. And have you any recollection whose furniture van it was?”
“Yes. I remember reading the name as it turned round. Goddard & James, George Street. I wondered if the tenant was going to move.”
===Chinese===
“您可记得，下午有一伙人从那宅子里出来？”
“不记得。他们若是出来过，我也没有留意。”
“那么劳驾，请您细细回想一下，告诉我们您可记得有什么车辆在那儿停过，若有的话。”
“让我想想。贾奇（Judge）家面包房的送货车是三点前后来的，送奶的车约莫五点，还有一辆家具搬运车，六点半光景。”
“这正是我要知道的。那辆家具车是谁家的，您可还记得？”
“记得。车子拐弯时我瞅见了上面的字号：戈达德与詹姆斯公司（Goddard & James），乔治街。我当时还纳闷，莫非那家房客要搬家。”

===Original===
The Inspector rose, and I followed his example.
“I am exceedingly obliged to you, Miss Tiffins. You have helped me materially.”
“I am glad of that,” she answered; “but I trust I shall not be wanted to give evidence in court. I really could not do it.”
“You need have no fear on that score,” the Inspector answered. “Good day.”
“Good day.”
When we had left the house the Inspector turned to me and said: “It was a great piece of luck finding a dressmaker opposite. Commend me to ladies of that profession for knowing what goes on in the street. Now we will visit Messrs. Goddard & James and see who hired the things. Meantime, Williams” (here he called the plainclothes constable to him), “you had better remain here and watch that house. If the man we saw comes out, follow him, and let me know where he goes.”
===Chinese===
督察起身，我随之而起。
“真是万分感激，蒂芬斯小姐。您帮了我大忙。”
“那就好了，”她答道，“只是我指望别传我上庭作证。我实在担当不起。”
“这一点您全不必担心，”督察答道，“再见。”
“再见。”
出了那座宅子，督察转身对我说：“对面恰巧是位裁缝，真是天大的运气。要说街上发生了什么，这一行的女士们最门儿清——这话我可要奉为至理。现在咱们去拜访戈达德与詹姆斯公司，看看东西是谁租的。威廉斯，”（说到这里，他把那名便衣巡警唤到跟前）“你还是留在这儿，盯住那座宅子。要是咱们见过的那个人出来，就盯住他，再把他去了哪儿告诉我。”

===Original===
“Very good, sir,” the constable replied, and we left him to his vigil.
Then, hailing a passing cab, we jumped into it and directed the driver to convey us to George Street. By this time it was getting on for midday, and we were both worn out. But I was in such a nervous state that I could not remain inactive. Phyllis had been in Nikola’s hands nearly fourteen hours, and so far we had not obtained one single definite piece of information as to her whereabouts.
Arriving at the shop of Messrs. Goddard & James, we went inside and asked to see the chief partner. An assistant immediately conveyed us to an office at the rear of the building, where we found an elderly gentleman writing at a desk. He looked up as we entered, and then, seeing the Inspector’s uniform, rose and asked our business.
“The day before yesterday,” began my companion, “you supplied a gentleman with a number of South-Sea weapons and curios on hire did you not?”
“I remember doing so﻿—yes,” was the old gentleman’s answer.
“What about it?”
===Chinese===
“是，先生，”巡警应道；我们便把他留在那儿守着。
随后我们拦下一辆过路的马车，跳上去，吩咐车夫送我们到乔治街。这时已近正午，我们俩都已精疲力竭。但我焦灼得根本坐不住。菲莉斯落到尼古拉手里已近十四个钟头，而到现在为止，关于她的下落，我们还没有得到一条确切的消息。
到了戈达德与詹姆斯公司的铺面，我们进店求见首席合伙人。一名伙计立刻领我们到店铺后部的一间办公室，里面一位上了年纪的先生正伏案书写。见我们进来，他抬起头，认出督察的制服，便起身问我们来意。
“前天，”我的同伴开口道，“贵号租给了一位先生一批南洋兵器和古玩，可有此事？”
“记得是有这么回事——是的，”老先生答道。
“那又怎样？”

===Original===
“Only I should be glad if you would favour me with a description of the person who called upon you about them﻿—or a glimpse of his letter, if he wrote.”
“He called and saw me personally.”
“Ah! That is good. Now would you be so kind as to describe him?”
“Well, in the first place, he was very tall and rather handsome he had, if I remember rightly, a long brown moustache, and was decidedly well dressed.”
“That doesn’t tell us very much, does it? Was he alone?”
“No. He had with him, when he came into the office, an individual whose face singularly enough remains fixed in my memory﻿—indeed I cannot get it out of my head.”
===Chinese===
“只不过，想请您赏个方便，把为这事登门的那位先生的模样说给我听——他若写过信，也容我看上一眼。”
“他是亲自登门见我的。”
“啊！那更好。现在可否劳驾，把他的模样说一说？”
“呃，首先，他个子很高，人也相当英俊——如果我没记错，留着两撇长长的棕色胡髭，衣着也着实讲究。”
“这没说出多少名堂，对吧？他当时独身一人？”
“不是。他进办公室时还带着一个人；说也奇怪，那个人的脸偏偏牢牢刻在我记忆里——说真的，怎么也甩不脱。”

===Original===
Instantly I became all excitement.
“What was this second person like?” asked the Inspector.
“Well, I can hardly tell you﻿—that is to say, I can hardly give you a good enough description of him to make you see him as I saw him. He was tall and yet very slim, had black hair, a sallow complexion, and the blackest eyes I ever saw in a man. He was clean-shaven and exquisitely dressed, and when he spoke, his teeth glittered like so many pearls. I never saw another man like him in my life.”
“Nikola for a thousand!” I cried, bringing my hand down with a thump upon the table.
“It looks as if we’re on the track at last,” said the Inspector. Then turning to Mr. Goddard again: “And may I ask now what excuse they made to you for wanting these things!”
===Chinese===
我霎时精神大振。
“第二个人什么模样？”督察问。
“唉，我很难说清——就是说，我很难把他的模样描述得让你们如亲眼见到一般。他个子高，人却又极清瘦，黑头发，面色蜡黄，还生着一双我在人身上见过的最黑的眼睛。他脸刮得干干净净，衣着精美绝伦，一开口，牙齿便如一排珍珠般闪光。我这辈子再没见过第二个这样的人。”
“拿一千来赌，也是尼古拉！”我喊道，一掌重重拍在桌上。
“看来咱们总算上道了，”督察说。接着又转向戈达德先生：“那么现在再请问，他们要租这些东西，可曾向您编过什么由头！”

===Original===
“They did not offer any; they simply paid a certain sum down for the hire of them, gave me their address, and then left.”
“And the address was?”
“83, Charlemagne Street. Our van took the things there and fetched them away last night.”
“Thank you. And now one or two other questions. What name did the hirer give?”
“Eastover.”
“And when they left your shop how did they go away?”
===Chinese===
“什么由头也没编；他们只是当场付清了一笔租金，留下地址，便走了。”
“地址是？”
“查理曼街 83 号。我们的货车把东西送了过去，昨天夜里又拉了回来。”
“谢谢。另外还有一两个问题。租东西的人报的什么名字？”
“伊斯特弗。”
“他们离开贵号时，是怎么走的？”

===Original===
“A cab was waiting at the door for them, and I walked out to it with them.”
“There were only two of them, you think?”
“No. There was a third person waiting for them in the cab, and it was that very circumstance which made me anxious to have my things brought back as soon as possible. If I had been able to, I should have even declined to let them go.”
“Why so?”
“Well, to tell you that would involve a story. But perhaps I had better tell you. It was in this way. About three years ago, through a distant relative, I got to know a man named Draper.”
“Draper!” I cried. “You don’t mean﻿—but there, I beg your pardon. Pray go on.”
===Chinese===
“门口有辆马车等着他们，我一直送他们到车前。”
“您觉得，他们只有两个人？”
“不止。车里还有第三个人等着他们，正是这一层，教我放心不下，巴不得东西尽早收回。若是我做得了主，我甚至不肯放它们出门。”
“这是为何？”
“唉，这话可就说来话长了。不过还是说了吧。事情是这样的：大约三年前，经由一位远亲，我认识了一个姓德雷珀的人。”
“德雷珀！”我喊道，“您该不是说——罢了，恕我失礼。请讲下去。”

===Original===
“As I say, I got to know this man Draper, who was a South-Sea trader. We met once or twice, and then grew more intimate. So friendly did we at last become, that I even went so far as to put some money into a scheme he proposed to me. It was a total failure. Draper proved a perfect fraud and a most unbusiness-like person, and all I got out of the transaction was the cases of curios and weapons which this man Eastover hired from me. It was because﻿—when I went out with my customers to their cab﻿—I saw this man Draper waiting for them that I became uneasy about my things. However, all’s well that ends well, and as they returned my goods and paid the hire I must not grumble.”
“And now tell me what you know of Draper’s present life,” the Inspector said.
“Ah! I’m afraid of that I can tell you but little. He has been twice declared bankrupt, and the last time there was some fuss made over his schooner, the *Merry Duchess*.”
“He possesses a schooner, then?”
“Oh, yes! A nice boat. She’s in harbour now, I fancy.”
===Chinese===
“正如我所说，我认识了这位德雷珀，他是个做南洋买卖的商人。我们会过一两次面，随后日渐亲密。到后来交情竟好到这地步：我甚至往他向我提议的一桩买卖里投了钱。结果一败涂地。德雷珀原来是个十足的骗子，又最没有生意头脑；那桩交易里我落到手的全部，就是这位伊斯特弗租走的那几箱古玩和兵器。正是因为——我送这两位主顾出门上车时——望见这个德雷珀在车里等着他们，我才为我的东西悬心。不过，结局好便一切都好；他们把货还了，租金也付了，我也就没什么好抱怨的了。”
“现在再说说德雷珀如今的境况，您知道多少？”督察说。
“唉！说到这个，只怕我能奉告的很少。他宣告过两回破产；上一回，他那艘纵帆船 *「快乐公爵夫人」号*（Merry Duchess）还闹出过一场风波。”
“这么说，他名下有一条纵帆船？”
“哦，有的！一条好船。我想这会儿就泊在港里。”

===Original===
“Thank you very much, Mr. Goddard. I am obliged to you for your assistance in this matter.”
“Don’t mention it. I hope that what I have told you may prove of service to you.”
“I’m sure it will. Good day.”
“Good day, gentlemen.”
He accompanied us to the door, and then bade us farewell.
===Chinese===
“多谢了，戈达德先生。这件事上蒙您相助，感激不尽。”
“不值一提。但愿我说的这些对你们有用。”
“一定有用。再见。”
“再见，二位先生。”
他送我们到门口，然后道别。

===Original===
“Now what are we to do?” I asked.
“Well, first, I am going back to the office to put a man on to find this schooner, and then I’m going to take an hour or two’s rest. By that time we shall know enough to be able to lay our hands on Dr. Nikola and his victim, I hope.”
“God grant we may!”
“Where are you going now?”
“Back to Potts Point,” I answered.
We thereupon bade each other farewell and set off in different directions.
===Chinese===
“现在咱们怎么办？”我问。
“唔，我先回署里派人去找那条纵帆船，然后歇上一两个钟头。到那时，我想咱们掌握的就够动手把尼古拉博士连同他手里的受害人一起擒获了。”
“但愿上帝保佑！”
“您这就去哪儿？”
“回波茨角，”我答道。
于是我们互相道别，分头而去。

===Original===
When I reached Mr. Wetherell’s house I learned from the butler that his master had fallen asleep in the library. Not wishing to disturb him, I enquired the whereabouts of my own bedroom, and on being conducted to it, laid myself down fully dressed upon the bed. So utterly worn out was I, that my head had no sooner touched the pillow than I was fast asleep. How long I lay there I do not know, but when I woke it was to find Mr. Wetherell standing beside me, holding a letter in his hand. He was white as a sheet, and trembling in every limb.
“Read this, Mr. Hatteras,” he cried. “For Heaven’s sake tell me what we are to do!”
I sat up on the side of the bed and read the letter he handed to me. It was written in what was evidently a disguised hand, on common notepaper, and ran as follows:﻿—

> “This is to inform you that your daughter is in very safe keeping. If you wish to find her you had better be quick about it. What’s more, you had better give up consulting the police, and suchlike, in the hope of getting hold of her. The only way you can get her will be to act as follows: At eight o’clock tonight charter a boat and pull down the harbour as far as Shark Point. When you get there, light your pipe three times, and someone in a boat near by will do the same. Be sure to bring with you the sum of one hundred thousand pounds in gold, and﻿—this is most important﻿—bring with you the little stick you got from China Pete, or do not come at all. Above all, do not bring more than one man. If you do not put in an appearance you will not hear of your daughter again.
> Yours obediently,
> “The Man Who Knows.”
===Chinese===
回到韦瑟雷尔先生府上，我从管家那里得知，主人在书房里睡着了。我不愿搅扰他，便问了自己卧室的所在，被领去之后，和衣倒在床上。我实在累得狠了，脑袋一沾枕头便沉沉睡去。躺了多久我不知道，醒来时却发现韦瑟雷尔先生站在床边，手里捏着一封信。他面白如纸，四肢发抖。
“读读这个，哈特拉斯先生，”他喊道，“看在老天分上，告诉我们该怎么办！”
我在床边坐直了身子，读了他递来的信。信写在普通的便笺上，字迹一望便知是刻意改换过的，全文如下：——

> “兹通知阁下：您的女儿此刻看管得十分稳妥。您若想寻回她，最好抓紧些。再者，您最好趁早断了求告警察之流的念想，指望靠那个找回她，是打错了算盘。要回令嫒，唯一的法子是照如下行事：今夜八点整，雇一条船，顺港而下，一直划到鲨鱼角（Shark Point）。到了那儿，把您的烟斗点着三回，附近船上自会有人照样点三回。务必随身携带十万英镑的金子，而且——这是最要紧的——务必带上您从中国皮特（China Pete）手里得到的那根小木棒，否则干脆别来。还有，千万至多带一个人来。您若不露面，令嫒的消息，您就再也听不到了。
> 您忠实的，
> “知情者”

## V Mystery / 第五章 谜团

===Original===
The following morning I was sitting in my room at the hotel idly scanning the *Standard*, and wondering in what way I should employ myself until the time arrived for me to board the yacht, when I heard a carriage roll up to the door.

On looking out I discovered a gorgeous landau, drawn by a pair of fine thoroughbreds, and resplendent with much gilded and crested harness, standing before the steps. A footman had already opened the door, and I was at the window just in time to see a tall, soldierly man alight from it. To my astonishment, two minutes later a waiter entered my room and announced “His Grace the Duke of Glenbarth.” It was the owner of the carriage and the father of my young friend, if by such a title I might designate the Marquis of Beckenham.

“Mr. Hatteras, I presume?” said he, advancing towards me and using that dignified tone that only an English gentleman can assume with anything approaching success.

“Yes, that is my name. I am honoured by your visit. Won’t you sit down?”

“Thank you.”

He paused for a moment, and then continued:
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我正坐在旅馆自己的房间里，懒懒地翻着《标准报》（*Standard*），盘算着在登船时刻到来之前这段日子该怎样打发，忽听得一辆马车辘辘驶到门前停下。

往外一望，只见台阶前停着一辆富丽堂皇的四轮敞篷马车（landau），由一对上好的良种马拉着，配着大片鎏金带纹章的马具，光彩夺目。一个男仆已经打开车门，我赶到窗前，恰好看清一个高个子、有军人气概的男人从车上下来。叫我惊讶的是，两分钟之后，一个侍者走进我的房间，通报说“格伦巴斯公爵（Duke of Glenbarth）大人到”。来客正是那辆马车的主人，也是我那位年轻朋友的父亲——倘若“朋友”这个称呼还配得上贝肯汉姆侯爵的话。

“想必是哈特拉斯先生吧？”他一面朝我走来，一面用一种唯有英国绅士才能学得略有小成的庄重腔调说道。

“正是，那是在下的名字。您肯光临，不胜荣幸。您请坐。”

“谢谢。”

他顿了片刻，接着说道：

===Original===
“Mr. Hatteras, I have to offer you an apology. I should have called upon you yesterday to express the gratitude I feel to you for having saved the life of my son, but I was unavoidably prevented.”

“I beg you will not mention it,” I said. “His lordship thanked me sufficiently himself. And after all, when you look at it, it was not very much to do. I would, however, venture one little suggestion. Is it wise to let him swim so far unaccompanied by a boat? The same thing might happen to him on another occasion, and no one be near enough to render him any assistance.”

“He will not attempt so much again. He has learned a lesson from this experience. And now, Mr. Hatteras, I trust you will forgive what I am about to say.
