My son has told me that you have just arrived in England from Australia. Is there any way I can be of service to you? If there is, and you will acquaint me with it, you will be conferring a great favour upon me.”

“I thank your Grace,” I replied﻿—I hope with some little touch of dignity﻿—“it is very kind of you, but I could not think of such a thing. But, stay, there is one service perhaps you could do me.”

“I am delighted to hear it, sir. And pray what may it be?”

“Your son’s tutor, Mr. Baxter! His face is strangely familiar to me. I have seen him somewhere before, but I cannot recall where. Could you tell me anything of his history?”
===Chinese===
“哈特拉斯先生，我得向您致歉。昨日我本该登门拜谢您救了我儿子性命的大恩，只因事出无奈，未能前来。”

“请您千万别放在心上，”我说，“大人他自己已经谢得够周到了。再说，细想起来，那实在算不得什么。不过，我斗胆提一句小小的忠告：让他游出那么远，又没有一条小船跟着，这妥当吗？下回再出同样的事，说不定附近就没有一个人能搭把手了。”

“他再不敢这样逞强了。这一遭已经给了他教训。那么，哈特拉斯先生，接下来说的话，还望您多多包涵。犬子告诉我，您刚从澳大利亚来到英国。有什么地方我能为您效劳吗？若有，只管让我知道，那可是赏了我的脸。”

“多谢大人，”我答道——但愿答得也带几分体面——“您实在太客气了，可这样的事我想也不敢想。不过——且慢，倒有一件小事，或许能劳您大驾。”

“听您这么说，真叫我高兴，先生。请问是何事？”

“令郎的家庭教师，巴克斯特先生！他这张脸我看着奇怪地眼熟。我从前在什么地方见过他，只是一时想不起在哪儿。您能给我讲讲他的来历吗？”

===Original===
“Very little, I fear, save that he seems a worthy and painstaking man, an excellent scholar, and very capable in his management of young men. I received excellent references with him, but of his past history I know very little. I believe, however, that he was a missionary in the South Seas for some time, and that he was afterwards for many years in India. I’m sorry I cannot tell you more about him since you are interested in him.”

“I’ve met him somewhere, I’m certain. His face haunts me. But to return to your son﻿—I hope he is none the worse for his adventure?”

“Not at all, thank you. Owing to the system I have adopted in his education, the lad is seldom ailing.”

“Pardon my introducing the subject. But do you think it is quite wise to keep a youth so ignorant of the world? I am perhaps rather presumptuous, but I cannot help feeling that such a fine young fellow would be all the better for a few companions.”

“You hit me on rather a tender spot, Mr. Hatteras. But, as you have been frank with me, I will be frank with you. I am one of those strange beings who govern their lives by theories. I was brought up by my father, I must tell you, in a fashion totally different from that I am employing with my son. I feel now that I was allowed a dangerous amount of license. And what was the result? I mixed with everyone, was pampered and flattered far beyond what was good for me, derived a false notion of my own importance, and when I came to man’s estate was, to all intents and purposes, quite unprepared and unfitted to undertake the duties and responsibilities of my position.
===Chinese===
“恐怕我知道得很少，只晓得他似乎是个正派而勤勉的人，学问极佳，管教年轻人颇为得法。他来时带着上好的荐书，至于他过去的经历，我知道得极少。不过我记得，他早先在南洋做过一阵传教士，后来又在印度待了许多年。您既对他上心，我却讲不出更多的东西，实在抱歉。”

“我准在哪儿见过他，这一点我有把握。那张脸老在我眼前晃。还是说说令郎吧——但愿他经了这一场冒险，没落下什么不舒服？”

“一点儿也没有，谢谢您。多亏我在他的教育上采用的那套法子，这孩子难得生病。”

“恕我多嘴提这个话头。可您觉得，让一个后生对世事这般一无所知，当真妥当吗？我这么说未免放肆，可我总忍不住想，这样一位好青年，多几个同伴只会更有益处。”

“您可戳到了我的痛处，哈特拉斯先生。不过，既然您对我直言，我也对您直言。我便是那种把自己的日子交给种种理论来打理的怪人。容我告诉您，家父教养我的法子，同我如今用在犬子身上的截然不同。如今想来，我当年被纵容到了危险的地步。结果如何？我什么人都混迹，受的娇宠和奉承远远过了头，对自己的分量生出了虚妄的想法；及至成年，无论从哪方面说，都全然没有准备好，也全然不配承担我这个身份的职分与责任。

===Original===
“Fortunately I had the wit to see where the fault lay, and there and then I resolved that if ever I were blessed with a son, I would conduct his education on far different lines. My boy has not met a dozen strangers in his life. His education has been my tenderest care. His position, his duties towards his fellow-men, the responsibilities of his rank, have always been kept rigorously before him. He has been brought up to understand that to be a Duke is not to be a titled nonentity or a pampered roué, but to be one whom Providence has blessed with an opportunity of benefiting and watching over the welfare of those less fortunate than himself in the world’s good gifts.

“He has no exaggerated idea of his own importance; a humbler lad, I feel justified in saying, you would nowhere find. He has been educated thoroughly, and he has all the best traditions of his race kept continually before his eyes. But you must not imagine, Mr. Hatteras, that because he has not mixed with the world he is ignorant of its temptations. He may not have come into personal contact with them, but he has been warned against their insidious influences, and I shall trust to his personal pride and good instincts to help him to withstand them when he has to encounter them himself. Now, what do you think of my plan for making a nobleman?”

“A very good one, with such a youth as your son, I should think, your Grace; but I would like to make one more suggestion, if you would allow me?”

“And that is?”

“That you should let him travel before he settles down. Choose some fit person to accompany him. Let him have introductions to good people abroad, and let him use them; then he will derive different impressions from different countries, view men and women from different standpoints, and enter gradually into the great world and station which he is some day to adorn.”
===Chinese===
“所幸我还算有脑子，看出了毛病出在哪里，当下便拿定主意：若上天赐我一子，我定要按截然不同的路子来施教。我这孩子长这么大，生人统共没见过一打。他的教育是我最贴心尽力的功课。他的身份、他对世人的义务、他那个等级的责任，始终一丝不苟地摆在他眼前。我教养他，是要他明白：做公爵并非做一个空有头衔的庸人，也不是做一个养尊处优的浪荡子，而是做一个蒙上天眷顾、得有机会造福并看护那些在世间福分上不及自己的人们的人。”

“他对自己的分量没有半点夸大的想法；我可以有把握地说，天底下再找不到比他更谦逊的后生了。他受的教育极周全，他那个族系最优秀的传统始终陈列在他眼前。可是哈特拉斯先生，您切莫以为他不曾涉足人世，便对人世的种种诱惑一无所知。他也许没同它们亲身打过交道，却早已有人告诫过他提防它们潜移默化的影响；待他日后亲身遭遇时，我自会指望他的自尊心和良善天性帮他顶住。怎么样，您觉得我这套造就贵族的法子如何？”

“用在大人令郎这样的青年身上，我看是套极好的法子；不过我还有一条建议，可否容我再提一提？”

“请讲？”

“在他安顿下来之前，您该让他出门游历。挑一个合适的人陪同前往。让他带着引荐书信去见国外的好人家，并让他善用这些书信；这样他便能从不同国度获得不同的印象，从不同角度观察男男女女，渐渐走进那片他终有一日要为之生辉的宏大世界和地位中去。”

===Original===
“I had thought of that myself, and his tutor has lately spoken to me a good deal upon the subject. I must own it is an idea that commends itself strongly to me. I will think it over. And now, sir, I must wish you good day. You will not let me thank you, as I should have wished, for the service you have rendered my house, but, believe me, I am none the less grateful. By the way, your name is not a common one. May I ask if you have any relatives in this county?”

“Only one at present, I fancy﻿—my father’s brother, Sir William Hatteras, of Murdlestone, in the New Forest.”

“Ah! I never met him. I knew his brother James very well in my younger days. But he got into sad trouble, poor fellow, and was obliged to fly the country.”

“You are speaking of my father. And you knew him?”

“Knew him? indeed, I did. And a better fellow never stepped; but, like most of us in those days, too wild﻿—much too wild! And so you are James’s son? Well, well! This is indeed a strange coincidence. But dear me, I am forgetting; I must beg your pardon for speaking so candidly of your father.”
===Chinese===
“这个我自己也想过，他的家庭教师近来还就此同我谈过许多。我得承认，这主意深得我心。容我再斟酌斟酌。好了，先生，我这就向您告辞了。您救了我一家的大恩，您又不肯让我照原本想望的那样向您道谢，可是请相信，我的感激分毫不减。对了，您这个姓氏并不多见。可否问一句，您在本郡还有亲属吗？”

“眼下我想只有一位——家父的兄弟，新森林（New Forest）里默德尔斯通（Murdlestone）的威廉·哈特拉斯爵士（Sir William Hatteras）。”

“啊！我从没见过他。他兄弟詹姆斯（James）我年轻时倒相熟得很。可惜他闯了大祸，可怜的人，只得逃离了英国。”

“您说的正是家父。这么说您认识他？”

“认识？岂止认识。天底下再走不出比他更好的汉子了；只是，跟我们那时候的大多数人一样，太野了——实在太野了！这么说，您就是詹姆斯的儿子？哎呀呀！这可真是天缘凑巧了。瞧我，光顾着说话；令尊的事我讲得这样直白，务请您恕罪。”

===Original===
“No offence, I’m sure.”

“And pray tell me where my old friend is now?”

“Dead, your Grace! He was drowned at sea.”

The worthy old gentleman seemed really distressed at this news. He shook his head, and I heard him murmur:

“Poor Jim! Poor Jim!”

Then, turning to me again, he took my hand.
===Chinese===
“哪里的话，绝不见怪。”

“那么请告诉我，我这位老朋友如今在哪里？”

“他已故世了，大人！他在海上遇难淹死了。”

这位忠厚的老绅士听了这个消息，似乎着实难过。他摇了摇头，我听见他低声念叨：

“可怜的吉姆！可怜的吉姆！”

随后他重新转向我，握住我的手。

===Original===
“This makes our bond a doubly strong one. You must let me see more of you! How long do you propose remaining in England?”

“Not very much longer, I fear. I am already beginning to hunger for the South again.”

“Well, you must not go before you have paid us a visit. Remember we shall always be pleased to see you. You know our house, I think on the cliff. Good day, sir, good day.”

So saying, the old gentleman accompanied me downstairs to his carriage, and, shaking me warmly by the hand, departed.

Again I had cause to ponder on the strangeness of the fate that had led me to Hampshire﻿—first to the village where my father was born, and then to Bournemouth, where by saving this young man’s life I had made a firm friend of a man who again had known my father. By such small coincidences are the currents of our lives diverted.
===Chinese===
“这么一来，你我这份交情又深了一层。您务必让我多多见您！您打算在英国盘桓多久？”

“只怕不会太久了。我又开始惦念起南方来了。”

“那么，您总得先到舍下做客一趟再走。记住，我们随时欢迎您来。我们家您是知道的，就在悬崖上，我想。再见，先生，再见。”

说罢，老绅士陪我下楼走到他的马车旁，热烈地同我握了握手，登车而去。

命运把我引到汉普郡（Hampshire）来的这桩离奇事，又给了我一层值得思索的道理——它先把我引到父亲出生的那座村子，又引到伯恩茅斯（Bournemouth）；在伯恩茅斯，我因救下这少年的性命，结下了一位知己，而这位知己又恰好认识我的父亲。我们一生的水流，便是被这般细小的巧合拨转的。

===Original===
That same afternoon, while tacking slowly down the bay, I met the Marquis. He was pulling himself in a small skiff, and when he saw me he made haste to come alongside and hitch on. At first I wondered whether it would not be against his father’s wishes that he should enter into conversation with such a worldly person as myself. But he evidently saw what was passing in my mind, and banished all doubts by saying:

“I have been on the lookout for you, Mr. Hatteras. My father has given me permission to cultivate your acquaintance, if you will allow me.”

“I shall be very pleased,” I answered. “Won’t you come aboard and have a chat? I’m not going out of the bay this afternoon.”

He clambered over the side and seated himself in the well, clear of the boom, as nice-looking and pleasant a young fellow as any man could wish to set eyes on. “Well,” I thought to myself, “if all Peers were like this boy there’d be less talk of abolishing the House of Lords.”

“You can’t imagine how I’ve been thinking over all you told me the other day,” he began when we were fairly on our way. “I want you to tell me more about Australia and the life you lead out there, if you will.”

“I’ll tell you all I can with pleasure,” I answered. “But you ought to go and see the places and things for yourself. That’s better than any telling. I wish I could take you up and carry you off with me now; away down to where you can make out the green islands peeping out of the water to port and starboard, like bits of the Garden of Eden gone astray and floated out to sea. I’d like you to smell the breezes that come off from them towards evening, to hear the ‘trades’ whistling overhead, and the thunder of the surf upon the reef. Or at another time to get inside that selfsame reef and look down through the still, transparent water, at the rainbow-coloured fish dashing among the coral boulders, in and out of the most beautiful fairy grottoes the brain of man can conceive.”
===Chinese===
当天下午，我正抢风缓缓驶下海湾，遇见了侯爵。他独自划着一条小艇，一看见我，便急忙靠上来搭住船舷。起初我还犯嘀咕，他父亲是否愿意他同我这样一个尘俗之辈攀谈。可他显然看穿了我的心思，一句话便打消了我的疑虑：

“我一直在留神等您呢，哈特拉斯先生。家父已经准许我同您结交，只要您肯赏脸。”

“我是求之不得，”我答道，“上船来聊聊可好？今天下午我不出海湾。”

他从船舷翻进来，在帆桁之下的舱阱里坐定，真是个任谁看了都愿意多瞧几眼的好脾性青年。“唉，”我心想，“倘若贵族议员个个像这孩子，嚷嚷着要废除上议院的声音也该少些了。”

等船真正走顺了，他开口道：“您真想象不到，前儿您讲的那些话，我翻来覆去想了多久。我想请您再给我讲讲澳大利亚，讲讲您在那边过的生活，不知您愿不愿意。”

“我乐意把我知道的都讲给您听，”我答道，“不过您实在该亲自去看看那些地方、那些东西，那比谁讲都强。但愿我这会儿就能把您拎起来，随身带走；一路带到那样的地方——朝左舷右舷望去，都看得见一座座绿岛从水面探出头来，像伊甸园（Garden of Eden）走失的碎片漂进了大海。我要让您闻一闻傍晚时分从岛上吹来的微风，听一听头顶呼啸的‘信风’（trades），听一听浪头砸在礁石上的雷鸣。再不然，换个时辰，钻进那同一道礁盘之内，透过纹丝不动的澄澈海水往下看，看彩虹色的鱼群在珊瑚巨砾间穿梭，游进游出于人的头脑所能想见的最美的那些仙窟。”

===Original===
“Oh, it must be lovely! And to think that I may live my life and never see these wonders. Please go on; what else can you tell me?”

“What more do you want to hear? There is the pick of every sort of life for you out there. Would you know what real excitement is? Then I shall take you to a new gold rush. To begin with you must imagine yourself setting off for the field, with your trusty mate marching step by step beside you, pick and shovel on your shoulders, and both resolved to make your fortunes in the twinkling of an eye. When you get there, there’s the digger crowd, composed of every nationality. There’s the warden and his staff, the police officers, the shanty keepers, the blacks, and dogs.

“There’s the tented valley stretching away to right and left of you, with the constant roar of sluice boxes and cradles, the creak of windlasses, and the perpetual noise of human voices. There’s the excitement of pegging out your claim and sinking your first shaft, wondering all the time whether it will turn up trumps or nothing. There’s the honest, manly labour from dawn to dusk. And then, when daylight fails, and the lamps begin to sparkle over the field, songs drift up the hillside from the drinking shanties in the valley, and you and your mate weigh up your day’s returns, and, having done so, turn into your blankets to dream of the monster nugget you intend to find upon the morrow. Isn’t that real life for you?”

He did not answer, but there was a sparkle in his eyes which told me I was understood.
===Chinese===
“啊，那一定美极了！可想想看，我这一生一世，也许永远见不着这些奇观。求您接着讲；还有什么可讲的？”

“您还想听什么？那边什么样的日子任您挑。您想知道什么才是真正的刺激吗？那我就带您去赶一场新起的淘金热。先得请您想象自己动身奔赴金矿：忠实的伙伴与您并肩而行，一步不落，鹤嘴锄和铁锹扛在肩上，两人都打定主意转眼间就发大财。到了地方，迎面是各国人等凑成的掘金人潮，有矿区监督官和他手下的属员，有警官，有酒寮掌柜，有黑人，还有狗。

“搭满帐篷的山谷在您左右两翼铺展开去，流矿槽和淘金摇篮的轰响不绝于耳，绞车吱嘎，人声鼎沸。钉桩圈定矿址、打下头一口竖井，那份心悬也够滋味——时时刻刻掂着这一注到底是满堂红还是一场空。从天亮到天黑，都是堂堂正正的男子汉劳作。待到天光收尽，灯火在矿区上次第闪亮，谷中酒寮里的歌声顺着山坡飘上来，您和伙伴掂量当天的收成，掂量完了，钻进毯子，去梦那块打算明天就挖出来的大金块。这对您难道不算真正的生活吗？”

他没有作声，可他眼睛里的光彩告诉我，他听懂了。

===Original===
“Then if you want other sorts of enterprise, there is Thursday Island, where I hail from, with its extraordinary people. Let us suppose ourselves wandering down the Front at nightfall, past the Kanaka billiard saloons and the Chinese stores, into, say, the Hotel of All Nations. Who is that handsome, dark, mysterious fellow, smoking a cigarette and idly flirting with the pretty bar girl? You don’t know him, but I do! There’s indeed a history for you. You didn’t notice, perhaps, that rakish schooner that came to anchor in the bay early in the forenoon. What lines she had! Well, that was his craft. Tomorrow she’ll be gone, it is whispered, to try for pearl in prohibited Dutch waters. Can’t you imagine her slinking round the islands, watching for the patrolling gunboat, and ready, directly she has passed, to slip into the bay, skim it of its shell, and put to sea again. Sometimes they’re chased﻿—and then?”

“What then?”

“Well, a clean pair of heels or trouble with the authorities, and possibly a year in a Dutch prison before you’re brought to trial! Or would you do a pearling trip in less exciting but more honest fashion? Would you ship aboard a rugger with five good companions, and go a-cruising down the New Guinea coast, working hard all day long, and lying out on deck at night, smoking and listening to the lip-lap of the water against the counter, or spinning yarns of all the world?”

“What else?”

“Why, what more do you want? Do you hanker after a cruise aboard a stinking beche-de-mer boat inside the Barrier Reef, or a run with the sandalwood cutters or tortoiseshell gatherers to New Guinea; or do you want to go ashore again and try an overlanding trip half across the continent, riding behind your cattle all day long, and standing your watch at night under dripping boughs, your teeth chattering in your head, waiting for the bulls to break, while every moment you expect to hear the Bunyip calling in that lonely water-hole beyond the fringe of Mulga scrub?”
===Chinese===
“要是您还想要点别的营生，那儿还有星期四岛——我的老家——有它那群非同寻常的人物。咱们不妨设想，黄昏时分沿着滨海正街（the Front）漫步，走过卡纳卡人（Kanaka）的台球厅和华人铺子，比方说，一头跨进万国旅馆（Hotel of All Nations）。那个漂亮、黝黑、神秘兮兮的家伙是谁，叼着香烟，正有一下没一下地逗着漂亮的酒吧姑娘？您不认得他，我可认得！这里头真有一段故事呢。您大概没留意，上半天早些时候开进湾里抛锚的那艘外形轻狂的纵帆船——多标致的船型！喏，那就是他的船。有风声说，明天它就要开拔，到荷兰人禁采的海域里去碰珍珠。您难道想象不出它贴着群岛潜行的样子？盯住巡逻炮舰的动向，只等它一过，便立刻溜进海湾，把湾里的贝壳刮捞个干净，再重新出海。有时它们会被追捕——然后呢？”

“然后呢？”

“嘿，要么撒开腿跑个干净，要么同官府纠缠不清，弄不好还得先在荷兰人的监狱里蹲上一年，才轮到提审！再不，您想不想换一种刺激少些、却更本分的法子采珠？到一艘 rugger（采珠小帆船）上入伙，同五位好伙伴一道，沿新几内亚海岸一路巡游南下，白日整日苦干，夜里躺在甲板上，抽着烟，听海水拍打船尾的啪嗒啪嗒，或者讲些天南海北的闲话？”

“还有呢？”

“怎么，您还想要什么？您是眼馋乘一条臭烘烘的海参（bêche-de-mer）船在大堡礁里头跑一趟呢，还是想随檀香木采伐船、玳瑁采集船往新几内亚去跑一程？再不然，您干脆重新上岸，试一试赶着牛群横跨半个大陆的长途贩运——白天整日跟在牛群后面颠簸，夜里在滴水的枝桠下守夜，牙齿冻得在脑袋里咯咯打架，一面提防公牛炸群，一面时时刻刻竖着耳朵，生怕听见茂尔加灌木（Mulga）丛外那一眼孤零零的水塘里，本耶普（Bunyip，澳洲传说中的水怪）叫出声来？”

===Original===
“You make me almost mad with longing.”

“And yet, somehow, it doesn’t seem so fine when you’re at it. It’s when you come to look back upon it all from a distance of twelve thousand miles that you feel its real charm. Then it calls to you to return in every rustle of the leaves ashore, in the blue of the sky above, in the ripple of the waves upon the beach. And it eats into your heart, so that you begin to think you will never be happy till you’re back in the old tumultuous devil-may-care existence again.”

“What a life you’ve led! And how much more to be envied it seems than the dull monotony of our existence here in sleepy old England.”

“Don’t you believe it. If you wanted to change I could tell you of dozens of men, living exactly the sort of life I’ve described, who would only too willingly oblige you. No, no! Believe me, you’ve got chances of doing things we would never dream of. Do them, then, and let the other go. But all the same, I think you ought to see more of the world I’ve told you of before you settle down. In fact, I hinted as much to your father only yesterday.”

“He said that you had spoken of it to him. Oh, how I wish he would let me go!”

“Somehow, d’you know, I think he will.”
===Chinese===
“您说得我心里痒痒的，几乎要发疯了。”

“可是不知怎么的，真到了其中的时候，又显不出这么美了。非得隔着一万两千英里回头眺望，你才尝得出它真正的魅力。于是它在岸上每一阵叶响里，在头顶那一片蓝天里，在浪头拍岸的涟漪里，声声唤你回去。它一点点啃进你的心，叫你渐渐觉得，不回到从前那种沸反盈天、无法无天的老日子里去，你到死也快活不起来。”

“您过的是怎样一种生活啊！比起我们在这昏昏欲睡的老英格兰里的沉闷单调，它可真叫人羡慕。”

“您可千万别信这个。您若真想交换，我能给您点出几十个人来，过的恰恰就是我讲的这种日子，他们巴不得成全您呢。不，不！相信我，您得到的机会，能做我们做梦都不敢想的事情。那就去做吧，别的由它去。不过话虽如此，我觉得您在安顿下来之前，总该把我讲给您的这个世界多看看。其实就在昨天，我还向令尊暗示过这个意思。”

“他说您提过这层意思。啊，我真盼着他肯放我去！”

“不知怎么的，您知道吗，我倒觉得他会的。”

===Original===
I put the cutter over on another tack, and we went crashing back through the blue water towards the pier. The strains of the band came faintly off to us. I had enjoyed my sail, for I had taken a great fancy to this bright young fellow sitting by my side. I felt I should like to have finished the education his father had so gallantly begun. There was something irresistibly attractive about him, so modest, so unassuming, and yet so straightforward and gentlemanly.

Dropping him opposite the bathing machines, I went on to my own anchorage on the other side of the pier. Then I pulled myself ashore and went up to the town. I had forgotten to write an important letter that morning, and as it was essential that the business should be attended to at once, to repair my carelessness, I crossed the public gardens and went through the gardens to the post office to send a telegram.

I must tell you here that since my meeting with Mr. Baxter, the young Marquis’s tutor, I had been thinking a great deal about him, and the more I thought the more certain I became that we had met before. To tell the truth, a great distrust of the man was upon me. It was one of those peculiar antipathies that no one can explain. I did not like his face, and I felt sure that he did not boast any too much love for me.

As my thoughts were still occupied with him, my astonishment may be imagined, on arriving at the building, at meeting him face to face upon the steps. He seemed much put out at seeing me, and hummed and hawed over his “Good afternoon” for all the world as if I had caught him in the middle of some guilty action.
===Chinese===
我把小快艇转到另一舷抢风，我们劈开碧蓝的海水，轰轰地朝栈桥驶回。乐队的乐曲隐隐约约向我们飘来。这一趟出海我畅快极了，因为我当真喜欢上了坐在我身边的这个爽朗的少年。我简直觉得，恨不得由我来接手完成他父亲如此勇毅地开了头的教育。他身上有一股难以抗拒的吸引力，那样谦逊，那样不摆架子，却又那样率直、那样有绅士风度。

我在更衣车对面把他放下，继续驶往栈桥另一侧我自己的锚地。随后我划桨上岸，进了镇子。那天早晨我忘写一封要紧的信，而此事必须立刻料理，为弥补我的疏忽，我穿过公共花园，一路穿过园子，到邮局去发一封电报。

这里我得告诉您，自从同小侯爵的家庭教师巴克斯特先生照面之后，我心里一直放他不下，越想越确信我们从前见过。说实在的，我对这人怀着深深的不信任。那是任谁也说不清道不明的一种特殊的厌恶。我不喜欢他的脸，而且我敢肯定，他对我也没有多少好感可供夸口。

正因为满脑子还是他，所以一到邮局门口，竟同他迎面撞个正着，我那份惊诧可想而知。他见了我也显得大为狼狈，一句“下午好”支支吾吾了半天才出口，那模样活像我撞破了他正干的什么亏心勾当。

===Original===
Returning his salutation, I entered the building and looked about me for a desk at which to write my wire. There was only one vacant, and I noticed that the pencil suspended on the string was still swinging to and fro as it had been dropped. Now Baxter had only just left the building, so there could be no possible doubt that it was he who had last used the stand. I pulled the form towards me and prepared to write. But as I did so I noticed that the previous writer had pressed so hard upon his pencil that he had left the exact impression of his message plainly visible upon the pad. It ran as follows:

> Letter received. You omitted Reverend. The train is laid, but a new element of danger has arisen.

It was addressed to “Nikola, Green Sailor Hotel, East India Dock Road, London,” and was signed “Nineveh.”

The message was so curious that I looked at it again, and the longer I looked the more certain I became that Baxter was the sender. Partly because its wording interested me, and partly for another reason which will become apparent later on, I inked the message over, tore it from the pad, and placed it carefully in my pocketbook. One thing at least was certain, and that was, if Baxter were the sender, there was something underhand going on. If he were not, well, then there could be no possible harm in my keeping the form as a little souvenir of a rather curious experience.
===Chinese===
我回了他的问候，走进局里，四下张望找一张空桌写电文。只有一张桌子空着，我注意到悬在绳上的铅笔还在来回晃荡，显然是刚被人放下的。巴克斯特前脚才离开这屋子，所以毫无疑问，最后用过这笔架的正是他。我把电报单拉到面前，正要动笔，却发觉前一位写字的人下笔太重，把他的电文原原本本地印在了拍纸簿上，看得一清二楚。那电文是：

> 信已收到。你漏了“牧师”（Reverend）的称呼。引线已经铺好，但一个新的危险因素出现了。

电文的收报人是“尼古拉（Nikola），伦敦东印度码头路‘绿衣水手’酒馆”，落款是“尼尼微”（Nineveh）。

这份电文实在古怪，我又端详了一遍，越看越认定发报人就是巴克斯特。一来因为那措辞引起了我的兴味，二来也因为另一个缘由——这个缘由后文自会分明——我便把那印痕用墨水描实，从拍纸簿上撕下来，仔细收进记事本。至少有一点是确定的：倘若发报人真是巴克斯特，那就有什么见不得人的勾当正在进行。倘若不是，那么我把这张电报单留下，当作一段颇为离奇的经历的小小纪念，也无伤大雅。

===Original===
I wrote my own message, and having paid for it left the office. But I was not destined to have the society of my own thoughts for long. Hardly had I reached the Invalids’ Walk before I felt my arm touched. To my supreme astonishment I found myself again confronted by Mr. Baxter. He was now perfectly calm and greeted me with extraordinary civility.

“Mr. Hatteras, I believe,” he said. “I think I had the pleasure of meeting you on the sands a few days ago. What a beautiful day it is, isn’t it? Are you proceeding this way? Yes? Then perhaps I may be permitted the honour of walking a short distance with you.”

“With pleasure,” I replied. “I am going up the cliff to my hotel, and I shall be glad of your company. I think we met in the telegraph office just now.”

“In the post office, I think. I had occasion to go in there to register a letter.”

His speech struck me as remarkable. My observation was so trivial that it hardly needed an answer, and yet not only did he vouchsafe me one, but he corrected my statement and volunteered a further one on his own account. What reason could he have for wanting to make me understand that he had gone in there to post a letter? What would it have mattered to me if he had been there, as I suggested, to send a telegram?

“Mr. Baxter,” I thought to myself, “I’ve got a sort of conviction that you’re not the man you pretend to be, and what’s more I’d like to bet a shilling to a halfpenny that, if the truth were only known, you’re our mysterious friend Nineveh.”
===Chinese===
我写好自己的电文，付了钱，便出了邮局。可我注定清静不了多久。刚走到疗养者步道（Invalids' Walk），我就觉得胳膊被人碰了一下。叫我万分错愕的是，站在我面前的又是巴克斯特先生。这会儿他已泰然自若，同我见礼时客气得非同寻常。

“想必是哈特拉斯先生吧，”他说，“前几天在海滩上，我有幸见过您一面。今天天气多好，是不是？您是往这边走？是吗？那么，可否容我有此荣幸，陪您走上一小段？”

“乐意之至，”我答道，“我正要上崖顶回旅馆，有您做伴再好不过。我记得我们方才在电报局里碰见过。”

“是在邮局里吧，我想。我进去有点事，去寄一封挂号信。”

他这话叫我暗暗称奇。我那句闲谈本无关痛痒，几乎用不着回答，可他非但赏了一个答话，还纠正我的话头，又自动补了一句。他究竟为什么要让我明白，他进去是寄挂号信的？倘若他真如我所说，是去发电报的，那与我又有何干？

“巴克斯特先生，”我心里暗想，“我多少有了点成见：您绝不是您装出来的那个人。不但如此，我愿拿一先令对半便士打赌——只要真相大白，您就是我们那位神秘的朋友‘尼尼微’。”

===Original===
We walked for some distance in silence. Presently my companion began to talk again﻿—this time, however, in a new strain and perhaps with a little more caution.

“You have been a great traveller, I understand, Mr. Hatteras.”

“A fairly great one, Mr. Baxter. You also, I am told, have seen something of the world.”

“A little﻿—very little.”

“The South Seas, I believe. D’you know Papeete?”

“I have been there.”
===Chinese===
我们默默走了一程。过了一会儿，我这位同伴又开了口——只是这回换了调子，或许还多了几分小心。

“听说您游历甚广，哈特拉斯先生。”

“还算去得不少，巴克斯特先生。听说您也见过些世面。”

“略见一二——少得很。”

“在南洋一带，我想。您认得帕皮提吗？”

“去过。”

===Original===
“D’you know New Guinea at all?”

“No. I was never near it. I am better acquainted with the Far East﻿—China, Japan, etc.”

Suddenly something, I shall never be able to tell what, prompted me to say:

“And the Andamans?”

The effect on my companion was as sudden as it was extraordinary. For a moment he staggered on the path like a drunken man; his face grew ashen pale, and he had to give utterance to a hoarse choking sound before be could get out a word. Then he said:

“No﻿—no﻿—you are quite mistaken, I assure you. I never knew the Andamans.”
===Chinese===
“您对新几内亚熟不熟？”

“不熟。我从没到过那一带。我更熟悉的是远东——中国、日本等地。”

忽然间，有个什么东西——我永远说不上来是什么——促使我说了一句：

“那么安达曼群岛（the Andamans）呢？”

这句话对同伴的刺激，来得又突然又非同小可。有一瞬他在小径上踉跄了一下，像个醉汉；脸色霎时灰白，喉咙里先挤出一声嘶哑的哽咽，才吐得出字来。然后他说：

“不——不——您全弄错了，我向您保证。我从来不认得什么安达曼群岛。”

===Original===
Now, on the Andamans, as all the world knows, are located the Indian penal establishments, and noting his behaviour, I became more and more convinced in my own mind that there was some mystery about Mr. Baxter that had yet to be explained. I had still a trump card to play.

“I’m afraid you are not very well, Mr. Baxter,” I said at length “Perhaps the heat is too much for you, or we are walking too fast? This is my hotel. Won’t you come inside and take a glass of wine or something to revive you?”

He nodded his head eagerly. Large drops of perspiration stood on his forehead, and I saw that he was quite unstrung.

“I am not well﻿—not at all well.”

As soon as we reached the smoking-room I rang for two brandies and sodas. When they arrived he drank his off almost at a gulp, and in a few seconds was pretty well himself again.
===Chinese===
众所周知，印度的流放监狱就设在安达曼群岛上；再看他这副举止，我心里越来越确信，巴克斯特先生身上有一桩尚待揭破的隐情。我手里还有一张王牌可打。

“恐怕您身子很不舒服吧，巴克斯特先生，”我终于开口道，“也许热劲儿太大，或者是我们走得太急？敝旅馆到了。进去喝杯葡萄酒或别的什么，提提神可好？”

他忙不迭地点头。他额头上挂着大颗大颗的汗珠，我看得出他方寸已乱。

“我是不舒服——很不舒服。”

一到吸烟室，我便按铃要了两杯掺苏打水的白兰地。酒一送来，他几乎一口闷了下去，不几秒钟便大体恢复了常态。

===Original===
“Thank you for your kindness, Mr. Hatteras,” he said. “I think we must have walked up the hill a little too fast for my strength. Now, I must be going back to the town. I find I have forgotten something.” Almost by instinct I guessed his errand. He was going to dispatch another telegram. Resolved to try the effect of one parting shot, I said:

“Perhaps you do not happen to be going near the telegraph office again? If you are, should I be taxing your kindness too much if I asked you to leave a message there for me? I find I have forgotten one.”

He bowed and simply said:

“With much pleasure.”

He pronounced it “pleasure,” and as he said it he licked his lips in his usual self-satisfied fashion. I wondered how he would conduct himself when he saw the message I was going to write.

Taking a form from a table near where I sat, I wrote the following:
===Chinese===
“多谢您的好意，哈特拉斯先生，”他说，“想必是我们上坡走得太急，超出了我的体力。好了，我得回城里去了。我发觉自己忘了一件事。”我几乎是本能地猜到了他的差事：他要去另发一封电报。我存心临别再放一枪试试效果，便说：

“您这趟该不会正好又要路过电报局吧？若是路过，可否劳您大驾，替我在那儿留一封电文？我发觉自己也忘了一封。”

他躬了躬身，只说了一句：

“乐意效劳。”

他说“效劳”两个字时，照例又用他那副自鸣得意的样子舔了舔嘴唇。我不禁寻思，待他看到我即将写下的电文，又会是怎样一副做派。

我从身旁的桌子上取过一张电报单，写下如下电文：

===Original===
> John Nicholson, Langham Hotel, London. 

 The train is laid, but a new danger has arisen. Hatteras.

Blotting it carefully, I gave it into his hands, at the same time asking him to read it, lest my writing should not be decipherable and any question might be asked concerning it. As he read I watched his face intently. Never shall I forget the expression that swept over it. I had scored a complete victory. The shaft went home. But only for an instant. With wonderful alacrity he recovered himself and, shaking me feebly by the hand, bade me goodbye, promising to see that my message was properly delivered.
===Chinese===
> 约翰·尼科尔森（John Nicholson），兰厄姆旅馆（Langham Hotel），伦敦。

 引线已经铺好，但一个新的危险已经出现。哈特拉斯。

我仔细吸干墨迹，把它交到他手里，同时请他过目一遍，免得我的字迹认不出来，日后生出什么疑问。他读的时候，我目不转睛地盯着他的脸。那一刻他脸上掠过的表情，我到死也忘不了。我赢了个彻彻底底。这一箭正中靶心。可惜只有一瞬。他以惊人的敏捷回过神来，有气无力地同我握了握手，道了再见，还一口答应说，我的电文必定妥为送到。

===Original===
When he had gone I laid myself back in my chair for a good think. The situation was a peculiar one in every way. If he were up to some devilry I had probably warned him. If not, why had he betrayed himself so openly?

Half an hour later an answer to my first telegram arrived, and, such is the working of Fate, it necessitated my immediate return to London. I had been thinking of going for some days past, but had put it off. Now it was decided for me.

As I did not know whether I should return to Bournemouth again, I determined to call upon the Marquis to bid him goodbye. Accordingly, donning my hat, I set off for the house.

Now if Burke may be believed, the Duke of Glenbarth possesses houses in half the counties of the kingdom; but I am told his seaside residence takes precedence of them all in his affections. Standing well out on the cliffs, it commands a lovely view of the bay﻿—looks toward the Purbeck Hills on the right, and the Isle of Wight and Hengistbury Head on the left. The house itself, as far as I could see, left nothing to be desired, and the grounds had been beautified in the highest form of landscape gardening.

I found my friend and his father in a summerhouse upon the lawn. Both appeared unaffectedly glad to see me, and equally sorry to hear that I had come to bid them goodbye. Mr. Baxter was not visible, and it was with no little surprise I learned that he, too, was contemplating a trip to the metropolis.
===Chinese===
他走后，我仰在椅子里好好思量了一番。这局面从哪一头看都透着古怪。倘若他在搞什么鬼名堂，我多半已经打草惊蛇。倘若不是，他又为何那样公开地自露马脚？

半小时后，我头一封电报的回电到了，而命运便是这样作弄人：它逼得我即刻返回伦敦。前几天我就动过回去的念头，只是一直拖着。如今，这桩事已不由我做主了。

因为拿不准还会不会回伯恩茅斯来，我决意登门拜望侯爵，向他道别。于是，我戴上帽子，朝那座宅子去了。

倘若伯克（Burke，指《伯克贵族名录》）可信，格伦巴斯公爵在王国半数的郡里都置有宅邸；不过听说，这座海滨住所在他心里的分量压过了它们全部。它高高立在悬崖上，将海湾的秀丽景致尽收眼底——右边望得见珀贝克丘陵（Purbeck Hills），左边望得见怀特岛（Isle of Wight）和亨吉斯特伯里角（Hengistbury Head）。宅子本身据我看无可挑剔，园囿更是照着最高明的造园法修饰过的。

我在草坪上的一座凉亭里找到了我的朋友和他父亲。两人见了我都是发自真心的高兴，听说我是来辞行的，又同样地怅然。巴克斯特先生不见人影，而听说他也正打算往首都去一趟，我不由得颇感意外。

===Original===
“I hope, if ever you visit Bournemouth again, you will come and see us,” said the Duke as I rose to leave.

“Thank you,” said I, “and I hope if ever your son visits Australia you will permit me to be of some service to him.”

“You are very kind. I will bear your offer in mind.”

Shaking hands with them both, I bade them goodbye, and went out through the gate.

But I was not to escape without an interview with my clerical friend after all. As I left the grounds and turned into the public road I saw a man emerge from a little wicket gate some fifty yards or so further down the hedge. From the way he made his appearance, it was obvious he had been waiting for me to leave the house.

It was, certainly enough, my old friend Baxter. As I came up with him he said, with the same sanctimonious grin that usually encircled his mouth playing round it now:
===Chinese===
“我希望，您日后若再到伯恩茅斯来，一定来看我们，”我起身告辞时，公爵说道。

“多谢，”我说，“我也希望，令郎日后若到澳大利亚去，您肯赏脸让我为他尽一份力。”

“您太客气了。您这番美意，我会记在心上。”

我同他们父子二人一一握别，走出大门。

可我到底没能躲开同我那位教士朋友的一番面谈。我刚出了园子拐上大路，便看见一个男人从树篱更下头约莫五十码外的一扇小边门里踱了出来。瞧他那露面的架势，明摆着是专候我离开这宅子的。

果不其然，正是我的老朋友巴克斯特。我赶上他时，他开口了，平日总挂在他嘴边的那副假仁假义的讪笑，此刻又在那儿打转：

===Original===
“A nice evening for a stroll, Mr. Hatteras.”

“A very nice evening, as you say, Mr. Baxter.”

“May I intrude myself upon your privacy for five minutes?”

“With pleasure. What is your business?”

“Of small concern to you, sir, but of immense importance to me. Mr. Hatteras, I have it in my mind that you do not like me.”

“I hope I have not given you cause to think so. Pray what can have put such a notion into your head?”
===Chinese===
“今晚散散步，天气倒不错，哈特拉斯先生。”

“确是不错的晚上，诚如您所言，巴克斯特先生。”

“可否容我打扰您五分钟清静？”

“请便。您有何贵干？”

“于您无足轻重，先生，于我却事关重大。哈特拉斯先生，我心里总觉着，您不喜欢我。”

“我希望我没有给您这种想法的由头。请问，是什么叫您生出了这般念头？”

===Original===
I half hoped that he would make some allusion to the telegram he had dispatched for me that morning, but he was far too cunning for that. He looked me over and over out of his small ferrety eyes before he replied:

“I cannot tell you why I think so, Mr. Hatteras, but instinct generally makes us aware when we are not quite all we might be to other people. Forgive me for speaking in this way to you, but you must surely see how much it means to me to be on good terms with friends of my employer’s family.”

“You are surely not afraid lest I should prejudice the Duke against you?”

“Not afraid, Mr. Hatteras! I have too much faith in your sense of justice to believe that you would willingly deprive me of my means of livelihood﻿—for of course that is what it would mean in plain English.”

“Then you need have no fear. I have just said goodbye to them. I am going away tomorrow, and it is very improbable that I shall ever see either of them again.”

“You are leaving for Australia?”
===Chinese===
我半心半意地指望他会提一提那天早晨替我发的那封电报，可他狡猾得很，偏不沾这个边。他用他那双雪貂似的小眼睛把我上上下下打量了个够，才答道：

“我说不清为何有此想法，哈特拉斯先生，可当我们在旁人眼里未免有亏欠时，本能总会让我们察觉。恕我这样冒昧对您说话，但您一定看得出，同我东家一家的朋友们和睦相处，于我是何等要紧。”

“您该不是怕我在公爵面前说您的坏话吧？”

“不是怕，哈特拉斯先生！我对您的公正心信得过，不信您会存心断了我的生计——说白了，事情就是那么回事。”

“那您就大可放心。我方才已同他们道过别。我明天就要走了，此生再见到他们二位中任何一位，怕是渺茫得很。”

“您这就回澳大利亚去了？”

===Original===
“Very shortly, I think.”

“I am much obliged to you for the generous way you have treated me. I shall never forget your kindness.”

“Pray don’t mention it. Is that all you have to say to me? Then good evening!”

“Good evening, Mr. Hatteras.” He turned back by another gate into the garden, and I continued my way along the cliff, reflecting on the curious interview I had just passed through. If the truth must be known, I was quite at a loss to understand what he meant by it! Why had he asked that question about Australia? Was it only chance that had led him to put it, or was it done designedly, and for some reason connected with that mysterious “Train” mentioned in his telegram?

I was to find out later, and only too thoroughly!
===Chinese===
“我想是快了。”

“承蒙您这样宽厚待我，我感激不尽。您的大恩大德，我永世不忘。”

“请千万别客气。您要对我说的就这些了？那么，晚安！”

“晚安，哈特拉斯先生。”他从另一道门折回园子里去了，我则继续沿着崖顶的路走，回味着方才这场古怪的会面。说句实话，我实在摸不透他这番举动是何用意！他为什么偏偏问起澳大利亚？那只是随口碰巧，还是有意为之，同他电报里提到的那条神秘的“引线”有着什么干系？

这个，我后来便知道了——而且知道得再透彻不过！

## II On the Trail / 第二章 循迹追踪

===Original===
As soon as Wetherell was able to speak again he said as feebly as an old man of ninety, “Take me home, Mr. Hatteras, take me home, and let us think out together there what is best to be done to rescue my poor child.” The Governor rose to his feet and gave him his arm.
“I think you’re right, Mr. Wetherell,” he said. “It is of course just probable that you will find your daughter at her home when you arrive. God grant she may be! But in case she is not I will communicate all I know to the Police Commissioner on his arrival, and send him and his officers on to you. We must lose no time if we wish to catch these scoundrels.” Then turning to me, he continued: “Mr. Hatteras, it is owing to your promptness that we are able to take such early steps. I shall depend upon your further assistance in this matter.”
“You may do so with perfect confidence, my lord,” I answered. “If you knew all you would understand that I am more anxious perhaps than anyone to discover the whereabouts of the young lady and my unfortunate friend.”
If his Excellency thought anything he did not give utterance to it, and Mr. Wetherell’s carriage being at the door we went out to it without another word. As we stepped into it Mr. Wetherell cried to the coachman:
“Home, and as fast as you can go.”
Next moment we were being whirled down the drive at a pace which at any other time I should have thought dangerous. Throughout the journey we sat almost silent, wrapped in our anxieties and forebodings; hoping almost against hope that when we arrived at Potts Point we should find Phyllis awaiting us there. At last we turned into the grounds, and on reaching the house I sprang out and rang the bell, then I went down to help my companion to alight. The butler opened the door and descended the steps to take the rugs. Wetherell stopped him almost angrily, crying:
===Chinese===
韦瑟雷尔刚能开口说话，便像个九十岁的老人一样有气无力地说道：“带我回家吧，哈特拉斯先生，带我回家，让咱们回去一起想想，怎样才能救出我那可怜的孩子。”总督站起身来，把胳膊递过去搀他。
“我想您说得对，韦瑟雷尔先生，”他说，“当然，您到家时，说不定恰好能在家里找到令嫒。但愿上帝保佑如此！万一她不在，等警察总监（Police Commissioner）一到，我会把我知道的一切都通报给他，再派他带着手下的警官到您那儿去。要逮住这伙恶棍，咱们一刻也不能耽搁。”接着他转向我，继续说道：“哈特拉斯先生，全亏您当机立断，我们才能这么早采取行动。这件事往后我还要多多仰仗您出力。”
“您尽管放心托付，大人，”我答道，“您若知晓全部内情，便会明白，要查出这位小姐和我那位不幸的朋友的下落，我也许比谁都心急。”
总督阁下纵然心里另有想法，也没有说出口来。韦瑟雷尔先生的马车已停在门前，我们一言不发地出去上车。跨进车厢时，韦瑟雷尔先生朝车夫喊道：
“回家，能赶多快赶多快。”
转眼间，马车已顺着车道飞驰而下，那速度若在平常，我定要喊一声危险。一路上我们几乎默坐无言，各自沉浸在焦虑与不祥的预感里，心里只存着一线近乎无望的希望——但愿赶到波茨角（Potts Point）时，菲莉斯正在家里等着我们。终于，马车拐进宅院，一到屋前，我便跳下车去按铃，随后又回身搀我的同伴下车。管家开了门，走下台阶来收车毯，韦瑟雷尔几乎是怒气冲冲地拦住他，喊道：

===Original===
“Where is your mistress? Has she come home?”
The expression of surprise on the man’s face told me, before he had time to utter a word, that our hopes were not to be realized.
“Miss Phyllis, sir?” the man said. “Why, she’s at the ball at Government ’Ouse.”
Wetherell turned from him with a deep sigh, and taking my arm went heavily up the steps into the hall.
“Come to my study, Mr. Hatteras,” he said, “and let me confer with you. For God’s sake don’t desert me in my hour of need!”
“You need have no fear of that,” I answered. “If it is bad for you, think what it is for me.” And then we went upstairs together.
===Chinese===
“你家主母在哪儿？她回来了没有？”
还不等那人开口，他脸上的惊愕已经告诉我：我们的指望落空了。
“菲莉斯小姐，老爷？”那人说，“嗐，她这会儿不还在总督府（Government ’Ouse）的舞会上嘛。”
韦瑟雷尔深深叹了口气，转过身去，挽起我的胳膊，迈着沉重的步子走上台阶，进了门厅。
“到我书房来，哈特拉斯先生，”他说，“让我跟你商量商量。看在上帝分上，千万别在这个节骨眼上撇下我！”
“这您尽管放心，”我答道，“您难受，可想想我又是什么滋味吧。”说罢我们便一同上了楼。

===Original===
Reaching his study, Mr. Wetherell led the way in and sat down. On a side table I noticed a decanter of whisky and some glasses. Without asking permission I went across to them and poured out a stiff nobbler for him.
“Drink this,” I said; “it will pull you together a little; remember you will want all your strength for the work that lies before us.”
Like a child he did as he was ordered, and then sank back into his chair. I went across to the hearthrug and stood before him.
“Now,” I said, “we must think this out from the very beginning, and to do that properly we must consider every detail. Have you any objection to answering my questions?”
“Ask any questions you like,” he replied, “and I will answer them.”
===Chinese===
到了书房，韦瑟雷尔先生领我进去坐下。我瞥见一张侧桌上摆着威士忌细颈瓶和几只酒杯，也不及请示，便走过去给他斟了满满一杯烈酒（nobbler）。
“把这个喝了，”我说，“它能让您稍稍振作些；记住，眼前的担子不轻，您得攒足全部精神才成。”
他像个孩子似的依言喝了，随即瘫回椅子里。我踱到炉前地毯上，在他跟前站定。
“现在，”我说，“咱们必须从头把这件事捋一遍；要捋得透，就得把每个细节都过一遍。您介意回答我几个问题吗？”
“想问什么只管问，”他答道，“我一定照实回答。”

===Original===
“In the first place, then, how soon after his arrival in the colony did your daughter get to know this sham Beckenham?”
“Three days,” he answered.
“At a dance, dinner party, picnic, or what?”
“At none of these things. The young man, it appears, had seen my daughter in the street, and having been struck with her beauty asked one of the aides-de-camp at Government House, with whom we are on intimate terms, to bring him to call. At the time, I remember, I thought it a particularly friendly action on his part.”
“I don’t doubt it,” I answered. “Well that, I think, should tell us one thing.”
===Chinese===
“那么第一件：那个假贝肯汉姆到了殖民地之后多久，令嫒就认识了他？”
“三天。”他答道。
“是在舞会上、晚宴上、野餐会上，还是别的什么场合？”
“都不是。据说，那年轻人在街上见过我女儿，为她的容貌所动，便请总督府里一位与我们交情深厚的副官带他登门拜访。记得我当时还想，他这份殷勤真是难得。”
“这我不怀疑，”我答道，“唔，我看这件事本身就该让咱们明白一点。”

===Original===
“And what is that?”
“That his instructions were to get to know your daughter without delay.”
“But what could his reason have been, do you think?”
“Ah, that I cannot tell you just yet. Now you must pardon what I am going to say: do you think he was serious in his intentions regarding Phyllis﻿—I mean your daughter?”
“Perfectly, as far as I could tell. His desire, he said, was, if she would have him, to be allowed to marry her on his twenty-first birthday, which would be next week, and in proof of permission he showed me a cablegram from his father.”
“A forgery, I don’t doubt. Well, then, the only construction I can put upon it is that the arrival of the real Beckenham in Sydney must have frightened him, thus compelling the gang to resort to other means of obtaining possession of her at once. Now our next business must be to find out how that dastardly act was accomplished. May I ring the bell and have up the coachman who drove your daughter to the ball?”
===Chinese===
“明白哪一点？”
“他那背后的指使，就是叫他毫不耽搁地结识令嫒。”
“可你想，他们这样处心积虑，图的是什么呢？”
“唉，这个我眼下还说不准。接下来这话您得恕我直言：您认为他对菲莉斯——我是说，对令嫒——是真心求娶吗？”
“据我判断，完全当真。他说，只要她肯嫁，他盼着能在自己二十一岁生日那天成婚——就是下星期——为证明家里点了头，还给我看了他父亲拍来的电报。”
“八成是伪造的。那么，依我看，唯一的解释是：真贝肯汉姆到了悉尼，准是吓着了他，逼得这一伙人不得不立刻改用别的手段，马上把她弄到手。眼下咱们要办的，就是查清那桩卑鄙勾当究竟是怎么得逞的。我可以摇铃，把今晚送令嫒去舞会的车夫叫上来问话吗？”

===Original===
“By all means. Please act in every way in this matter as if this house were your own.”
I rang the bell, and when the butler appeared to answer it Mr. Wetherell instructed him to find the man I wanted and send him up. The servant left the room again, and for five minutes we awaited his reappearance in silence. When he did come back he said,
“Thompson has not come home yet, sir.”
“Not come home yet! Why, it’s nearly eleven o’clock! Send him in directly he arrives. Hark! What bell is that?”
“Front door, sir.”
“Go down and answer it then, and if it should be the Commissioner of Police show him up here at once.”
===Chinese===
“尽管问。这件事上请您全权做主，只当这宅子是您自家的。”
我摇了铃，管家应声而来，韦瑟雷尔先生吩咐他去把我要找的人寻来送上楼。仆人又退了出去，足足有五分钟，我们默然等着他回话。他回来时说道，
“汤普森（Thompson）还没回来，老爷。”
“还没回来！哎呀，都快十一点了！他一到就马上叫他上来。听！这是哪儿的铃？”
“大门，老爷。”
“那就快下去应门，要是来的是警察总监，立刻请他到这儿来。”

===Original===
As it turned out it was not the Commissioner of Police, but an Inspector.
“Good evening,” said Mr. Wetherell. “You have come from Government House, I presume?”
“Exactly so, sir,” replied the Inspector. “His Excellency gave us some particulars and then sent us on to you.”
“You know the nature of the case?”
“His Excellency informed us himself.”
“And what steps have you taken?”
===Chinese===
来的结果不是警察总监，而是一位警察督察（Inspector）。
“晚上好，”韦瑟雷尔先生说，“您是从总督府来的吧？”
“正是，先生，”督察答道，“总督阁下向我们交待了几句详情，就打发我们到您这儿来了。”
“案子的来龙去脉，你们都清楚了？”
“总督阁下亲自交待的。”
“那你们采取了什么步骤？”

===Original===
“Well, sir, to begin with, we have given orders for a thorough search throughout the city and suburbs for the tutor and the sham nobleman, at the same time more men are out looking for the real Lord Beckenham. We are also trying to find your coachman, who was supposed to have driven Miss Wetherell away from Government House, and also the carriage, which is certain to be found before very long.”
He had hardly finished speaking before there was another loud ring at the bell, and presently the butler entered the room once more. Crossing to Mr. Wetherell, he said:
“Two policemen are at the front door, and they have brought Thompson home, sir.”
“Ah! We are likely to have a little light thrown upon the matter now. Let them bring him up here instantly.”
“He’s not in a very nice state, sir.”
“Never mind that. Let them bring him up here, instantly!” Again the butler departed, and a few moments later heavy footsteps ascended the stairs and approached the study door. Then two stalwart policemen entered the room supporting between them a miserable figure in coachman’s livery. His hat and coat were gone and his breeches were stained with mud, while a large bruise totally obscured his left eye. His master surveyed him with unmitigated disgust.
===Chinese===
“是这样，先生：第一，我们已经下令，在全城和四郊彻查那位家庭教师和那个假贵族的下落，同时另派了更多人手去找真贝肯汉姆勋爵。我们也在找您的车夫——据信是他把韦瑟雷尔小姐从总督府载走的——还有那辆马车，要不了多久准能找到。”
他话音未落，铃又响了一长声，不多时管家再次进来，走到韦瑟雷尔先生跟前说：
“老爷，门口有两个警察，他们把汤普森送回来了。”
“啊！这下子事情该能见点眉目了。马上带他上来。”
“只是他那样子不太像样，老爷。”
“管他像不像样，马上带他上来，立刻！”管家又去了，片刻之后，沉重的脚步声上了楼，朝书房门口而来。随即两名壮健的警察走进屋来，中间架着一个穿车夫号衣的可怜人。他帽子外套都没了，马裤上糊满泥污，左眼乌青肿起一大块，把整只眼睛都遮没了。主人把他上下打量，满脸掩饰不住的嫌恶。

===Original===
“Stand him over there opposite me,” said Mr. Wetherell, pointing to the side of the room furthest from the door.
The policemen did as they were ordered, while the man looked more dead than alive.
“Now, Thompson,” said Wetherell, looking sternly at him, “what have you got to say for yourself?”
But the man only groaned. Seeing that in his present state he could say nothing, I went across to the table and mixed him a glass of grog. When I gave it to him he drank it eagerly. It seemed to sharpen his wits, for he answered instantly:
“It wasn’t my fault, sir. If I’d only ha’ known what their game was I’d have been killed afore I’d have let them do anything to hurt the young lady. But they was too cunnin’ for me, sir.”
===Chinese===
“让他站到那边我对面去，”韦瑟雷尔先生指着屋里离门最远的那一侧说。
警察依言照办，那人却已是半死不活。
“喂，汤普森，”韦瑟雷尔正色瞪着他说，“你还有什么可说的？”
那人却只是哼哼。我看他这副样子一时说不出话来，便走到桌边给他调了一杯格罗格酒（grog，掺水的烈酒）。他接过去急急喝了，脑子似乎立刻灵光起来，当即答道：
“这不赖我，老爷。我要是早知道他们打的什么主意，就是搭上这条命，也不能叫他们碰小姐一根毫毛哇。可他们实在精得厉害，我斗不过，老爷。”

===Original===
“Be more explicit, sir!” said Wetherell sternly. “Don’t stand there whining, but tell your story straightforwardly and at once.”
The poor wretch pulled himself together and did his best.
“It was in this way, sir,” he began. “Last week I was introduced by a friend of mine to as nice a spoken man as ever I saw. He was from England, he said, and having a little money thought he’d like to try his ’and at a bit o’ racing in Australia, like. He was on the lookout for a smart man, he said, who’d be able to put him up to a wrinkle or two, and maybe train for him later on. He went on to say that he’d ’eard a lot about me, and thought I was just the man for his money. Well, we got more and more friendly till the other night, Monday, when he said as how he’d settled on a farm a bit out in the country, and was going to sign the agreement, as he called it, for to rent it next day. He was goin’ to start a stud farm and trainin’ establishment combined, and would I take the billet of manager at three ’undred a year? Anyway, as he said, ‘Don’t be in a ’urry to decide; take your time and think it over. Meet me at the Canary Bird ’Otel on Thursday night (that’s tonight, sir) and give me your decision.’ Well, sir, I drove Miss Wetherell to Government ’Ouse, sir, according to orders, and then, comin’ ’ome, went round by the Canary Bird to give ’im my answer, thinkin’ no ’arm could ever come of it. When I drove up he was standin’ at the door smoking his cigar, an’ bein’ an affable sort of fellow, invited me inside to take a drink. ‘I don’t like to leave the box,’ I said. ‘Oh, never mind your horse,’ says he. ‘ ’Ere’s a man as will stand by it for five minutes.’ He gave a respectable lookin’ chap, alongside the lamppost, a sixpence, and he ’eld the ’orse, so in I went. When we got inside I was for goin’ to the bar, but ’e says, ‘No. This is an important business matter, and we don’t want to be over’eard.’ With that he leads the way into a private room at the end of the passage and shuts the door. ‘What’s yours?’ says he. ‘A nobbler o’ rum,’ says I. Then he orders a nobbler of rum for me and a nobbler of whisky for ’imself. And when it was brought we sat talkin’ of the place he’d thought o’ takin’ an’ the ’orses he was goin’ to buy, an’ then ’e says, ‘ ’Ullo! Somebody listenin’ at the door. I ’eard a step. Jump up and look.’ I got up and ran to the door, but there was nobody there, so I sat down again and we went on talking. Then he says, takin’ up his glass: ‘ ’Ere’s to your ’ealth, Mr. Thompson, and success to the farm.’ We both drank it en’ went on talkin’ till I felt that sleepy I didn’t know what to do. Then I dropped off, an’ after that I don’t remember nothin’ of what ‘appened till I woke up in the Domain, without my hat and coat, and found a policeman shakin’ me by the shoulder.”
“The whole thing is as plain as daylight,” cried Wetherell bitterly. “It is a thoroughly organized conspiracy, having me for its victim. Oh, my girlie! My poor little girlie! What has my obstinacy brought you to!”
Seeing the old man in this state very nearly broke me down, but I mastered myself with an effort and addressed a question to the unfortunate coachman:
===Chinese===
“说清楚点儿！”韦瑟雷尔厉声道，“别站在那儿哼哼唧唧，痛痛快快，把事情原原本本讲出来。”
那可怜虫强打精神，尽其所能地讲了。
“事情是这么开始的，老爷，”他开了腔，“上礼拜，一位朋友把我引荐给一位先生，那么和声和气说话的人，我平生头一回见识。他说他是打英国来的，手头攒了几个钱，想上澳大利亚来小试一把赛马的营生。他说他正要物色一个精明人，能点拨他一两招，往后兴许还替他驯马。他还说，我的名声他早有耳闻，觉得他的钱交到我手里正合适。得，我们就这么越走越近，直到前几天晚上——礼拜一——他说他已在乡下相中了一座农场，第二天就要去签那所谓的‘租约’。他打算办一座种马场外带驯马场，问我可愿屈就经理一职，一年三百镑。末了他说：‘不忙回话；慢慢琢磨，想周全了，礼拜四晚上——就是今晚，老爷——到“金丝雀鸟”旅馆（Canary Bird ’Otel）来给我个准信儿。’就这样，老爷，我奉命送韦瑟雷尔小姐去了总督府，回程路上就顺脚拐到金丝雀鸟那儿给他回话，心里想这能出什么乱子。我赶到时，他正站在门口抽雪茄，为人和气，便请我进去喝上一杯。我说：‘我不乐意撂下赶车的座儿。’他说：‘嗐，马怕什么，这儿有个人，替你照看五分钟。’他给了路灯杆子边上一个体面模样的汉子六个便士，那人就替我牵住了马，我这才进去。进了门我本想去酒柜那儿，可他说：‘不，这是桩正经买卖，不能让人听了去。’说着便领我进了过道尽头的一间雅座，随手带上了门。他问：‘你喝什么？’我说：‘来一盅朗姆。’他就叫了一盅朗姆给我，自己叫了一盅威士忌。酒送来了，我们就坐下，聊他相中的那处地方、他要买的马，聊着聊着他忽然说：‘咦！门口有人偷听，我听见脚步声了。快起来去瞧瞧。’我起身奔到门边，外头连个人影也没有，只好又坐下接着聊。后来他端起酒盅说：‘敬你一杯，汤普森先生，祝农场兴旺。’我们俩一饮而尽，接着又聊，聊着聊着我困得睁不开眼。后来我就睡着了，打那以后的事一点儿也记不得了，等醒过来，人已经在多曼公园（the Domain）的草地上，帽子外套都不翼而飞，一个警察正抓着我的肩膀摇呢。”
“整件事明明白白，跟白昼一样，”韦瑟雷尔悲愤地喊道，“这是一场布置周密的阴谋，冲着我来的。唉，我的乖女儿！我可怜的小乖乖！都是我这一意孤行，把你害到了这步田地！”
老人这副模样，几乎把我的心肠揉碎，但我强自镇定，向那倒霉的车夫发问：

===Original===
“Pull yourself together, Thompson, and try and tell me as correctly as you can what this friend of yours was like.”
I fully expected to hear him give an exact description of the man who had followed us from Melbourne, but I was mistaken.
“I don’t know, sir,” said Thompson, “as I could rightly tell you, my mind being still a bit dizzy-like. He was tall, but not by any manner of means big made; he had very small ’ands an’ feet, a sort o’ what they call death’s-’ead complexion; ’is ’air was black as soot, an’ so was ’is eyes, an’ they sparkled like two diamonds in ’is ’ead.”
“Do you remember noticing if he had a curious gold ring on his little finger, like a snake?”
“He had, sir, with two eyes made of some black stone. That’s just as true as you’re born.”
“Then it was Nikola,” I cried in an outburst of astonishment, “and he followed us to Australia after all!”
===Chinese===
“振作点儿，汤普森，尽力把你那位朋友的相貌，给我说得仔细些。”
我满心以为，他会把那个从墨尔本（Melbourne）一路跟踪我们的人的模样一五一十说出来，谁料我猜错了。
“说不好，老爷，”汤普森道，“脑子这会儿还晕乎着呢。他个子高，可怎么也算不上壮实；一双手和脚都小得很；脸色就是人家说的那种死人头脸色；头发黑得跟煤烟似的，眼睛也那么黑，在他脑袋上亮得像两颗钻石。”
“你记不记得，他小指上有没有戴一枚奇怪的蛇形金戒指？”
“戴着，老爷，蛇眼睛是两粒黑石头镶的。千真万确，错不了。”
“那就是尼古拉！”我惊得脱口喊道，“他到底还是一路追到澳大利亚来了！”

===Original===
Wetherell gave a deep sigh that was more like a groan than anything else; then he became suddenly a new man.
“Mr. Inspector,” he cried to the police officer, “that man, or traces of him, must be found before daylight. I know him, and he is as slippery as an eel; if you lose a minute he’ll be through your fingers.”
“One moment first,” I cried. “Tell me this, Thompson: when you drove up to the Canary Bird Hotel where did you say this man was standing?”
“In the verandah, sir.”
“Had he his hat on?”
“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
韦瑟雷尔长叹一声，那声音与其说是叹息，不如说是呻吟；随后他忽然像换了一个人。
“督察先生，”他冲那位警官喊道，“天亮之前，务必寻到那个人，或是他的踪迹。我了解他，他滑得跟泥鳅一样；耽误一分钟，他就从你们指缝里溜了。”
“先慢一步，”我说，“先告诉我这个，汤普森：你赶车到金丝雀鸟旅馆门口时，那人站在哪儿？”
“在廊子里，老爷。”
“头上戴没戴帽子？”
“戴着，老爷。”

===Original===
“And then you went towards the bar, but it was crowded, so he took you to a private room?”
“Yes, sir.”
“And once there he began giving you the details of this farm he proposed starting. Did he work out any figures on paper?”
“Yes, sir.”
“On what?”
===Chinese===
“后来你们要上酒柜去，可那儿人多，他就把你带进了雅座？”
“是，老爷。”
“进去之后，他就开始跟你细算他打算开办的那座农场的种种账目。他在纸上列过什么数字没有？”
“列了，老爷。”
“写在什么上头？”

===Original===
“On a letter or envelope; I’m not certain which.”
“Which of course he took from his pocket?”
“Yes, sir.”
“Very good,” I said. Then turning to the police officer, “Now, Mr. Inspector, shall we be off to the Canary Bird?”
“If you wish it, sir. In the meantime I’ll send instructions back by these men to the different stations. Before breakfast time we must have the man who held the horse in our hands.”
“You don’t know him, I suppose?” I asked Thompson.
===Chinese===
“一封信上，还是信封上，我记不清了。”
“那当然是他从口袋里掏出来的？”
“是，老爷。”
“很好，”我说，随即转向那位警官，“那么，督察先生，咱们这就上金丝雀鸟旅馆走一趟？”
“听您的，先生。趁这工夫，我让这两人把指令捎回各个警局。早饭之前，务必把那个替他牵马的人抓到手里。”
“那个人你不认得吧？”我问汤普森。

===Original===
“No, sir; but I’ve seen him before,” he answered.
“He’s a Sydney fellow then?”
“Oh yes, sir.”
“Then there should be no difficulty in catching him. Now let us be going.”
Mr. Wetherell rose to accompany us, but hard though it was to stop him I eventually succeeded in dissuading him from such a course.
“But you will let me know directly you discover anything, won’t you, Mr. Hatteras?” he cried as we were about to leave the room. “Think what my anxiety will be.”
===Chinese===
“不认得，老爷，不过从前见过。”他答道。
“那就是悉尼本地人了？”
“可不，老爷。”
“那抓他该不难。走吧。”
韦瑟雷尔先生起身要一同去，我拦得虽为难，终究还是劝住了他。
“可一有消息，你马上打发人告诉我，行不行，哈特拉斯先生？”我们正要出屋时他喊道，“想想我这颗心要怎么熬吧。”

===Original===
I gave my promise and then, accompanied by the Inspector, left the house. Hailing a passing cab we jumped into it and told the driver to proceed as fast as he could to the hotel in question. Just as we started a clock in the neighbourhood struck twelve. Phyllis had been in Nikola’s hands three hours.
Pulling up opposite the Canary Bird (the place where the coachman had been drugged), we jumped out and bade the cabman wait. The hotel was in complete darkness, and it was not until we had pealed the bell twice that we succeeded in producing any sign of life. Then the landlord, half dressed, carrying a candle in his hand, came downstairs and called out to know who was there and what we wanted. My companion immediately said “Police,” and in answer to that magic word the door was unbarred.
“Good evening, Mr. Bartrell,” said the Inspector politely. “May we come in for a moment on business?”
“Certainly, Mr. Inspector,” said the landlord, who evidently knew my companion. “But isn’t this rather a late hour for a call. I hope there is nothing the matter?”
“Nothing much,” returned the Inspector; “only we want to make a few enquiries about a man who was here tonight, and for whom we are looking.”
“If that is so I’m afraid I must call my barman. I was not in the bar this evening. If you’ll excuse me I’ll go and bring him down. In the meantime make yourselves comfortable.”
===Chinese===
我应承下来，便同督察一起出了宅子。拦下一辆过路的马车，我们跳上去，吩咐车夫尽快赶到那家旅馆。正要动身，附近一座钟敲了十二下。菲莉斯落到尼古拉手里，已经三个钟头了。
马车在金丝雀鸟旅馆（就是车夫被灌药的地方）对面停住，我们跳下车，吩咐车夫等着。旅馆里一片漆黑，直拉了两遍铃，才弄出一点活人的动静。店主半穿不挂，手里举着一支蜡烛下了楼，大声问是谁、要干什么。我那同伴应声说了句“警察”，这个字眼仿佛咒语一般，门闩应声而开。
“晚上好，巴特雷尔先生（Bartrell），”督察客气地说，“有桩公事，能进去耽搁片刻吗？”
“当然可以，督察先生，”店主说，他显然认得我这位同伴，“只是这个时辰登门，未免太晚了吧。但愿没出什么事？”
“没什么大事，”督察答道，“不过是想打听一个今晚来过这儿的人，我们正在找他。”
“要是这样，恐怕我得把酒柜伙计叫来。今晚我没在柜上待着。失陪片刻，我去把他带下来。二位随意请坐。”

===Original===
He left us to kick our heels in the hall while he went upstairs again. In about ten minutes, and just as my all-consuming impatience was well-nigh getting the better of me, he returned, bringing with him the sleepy barman.
“These gentlemen want some information about a man who was here tonight,” the landlord said by way of introduction. “Perhaps you can give it?”
“What was he like, sir?” asked the barman of the Inspector.
The latter, however, turned to me.
“Tall, slim, with a sallow complexion,” I said, “black hair and very dark restless eyes. He came in here with the Hon. Sylvester Wetherell’s coachman.”
The man seemed to recollect him at once.
===Chinese===
他把我们撂在门厅里干等，自己又上楼去了。约莫十分钟后，就在我心头的焦灼几乎要按捺不住时，他回来了，身后跟着睡眼惺忪的酒柜伙计。
“这两位先生想打听一个今晚来过店里的人，”店主权作引见地说，“兴许你说得上？”
“那人什么样儿，先生？”伙计问督察。
督察却把话头转给了我。
“高个子，瘦削，面色蜡黄，”我说，“黑头发，一双黑眼睛滴溜溜转个不停。他是跟西尔维斯特·韦瑟雷尔（Sylvester Wetherell）先生家的车夫一道进来的。”
那人似乎立刻想起来了。

===Original===
“I remember him,” he said. “They sat in No. 5 down the passage there, and the man you mention ordered a nobbler of rum for his friend and a whisky for himself.”
“That’s the fellow we want,” said the Inspector. “Now tell me this, have you ever seen him in here before?”
“Never once,” said the barman, “and that’s a solemn fact, because if I had I couldn’t have forgotten it. His figurehead wouldn’t let you do that. No, sir, tonight was the first night he’s ever been in the Canary Bird.”
“Did anyone else visit them while they were in the room together?”
“Not as I know of. But stay, I’m not so certain. Yes; I remember seeing a tall, good-looking chap come down the passage and go in there. But it was some time, half an hour maybe, after I took in the drinks.”
“Did you see him come out again?”
===Chinese===
“记得，”他说，“他们坐在那边过道里的 5 号房，就是您说的那位，给他的朋友要了一盅朗姆，给自己要了一份威士忌。”
“就是我们要找的人没错，”督察说，“再问你一句，从前见过他上这儿来吗？”
“一回也没见过，”伙计说，“这话千真万确，真见过就忘不了——就凭他那副长相，你想忘也忘不掉。不，先生，今晚是他头一回来金丝雀鸟。”
“他们在那屋里坐着的时候，有别人去找过他们吗？”
“据我所知没有。慢着，这我不敢打包票。对，想起来了，我见过一个高高的、体面漂亮的小伙子顺着过道走进那间屋去。不过那已是我把酒送进去好一会儿之后的事了，兴许有半个钟头。”
“看见他出来没有？”

===Original===
“No. But I know the coachman got very drunk, and had to be carried out to the carriage.”
“How do you know that?”
“Because I saw the other two doing it.”
The Inspector turned to me.
“Not very satisfactory, is it?”
“No,” I answered. “But do you mind letting us look into No. 5﻿—the room they occupied?”
===Chinese===
“没看见。可我知道那车夫烂醉如泥，是让人抬出去送上车的。”
“这你怎么知道的？”
“因为抬人的就是另外那两个，我亲眼瞧见的。”
督察转向我。
“这口供不太顶事，是吧？”
“是啊，”我答道，“不过能不能让我们看看 5 号房——他们待过的那间？”

===Original===
“Not at all,” said the landlord. “Will you come with me?”
So saying he led the way down the passage to a little room on the right-hand side, the door of which he threw open with a theatrical flourish. It was in pitch darkness, but a few seconds later the gas was lit and we could see all that it contained. A small table stood in the centre of the room and round the walls were ranged two or three wooden chairs. A small window was at the further end and a fireplace opposite the door. On the table was a half-smoked cigar and a torn copy of the Evening Mercury. But that was not what I wanted, so I went down on my hands and knees and looked about upon the floor.
Presently I descried a small ball of paper near the grate. Picking it up I seated myself at the table and turned to the barman, who was watching my movements attentively.
“Was this room used by any other people after the party we are looking for left?”
“No, sir. There was nobody in either of these two bottom rooms.”
“You are quite certain of that?”
===Chinese===
“没问题，”店主说，“随我来吧。”
说罢他领路沿过道走到右手边一间小屋前，煞有介事地一挥手把门推开。屋里漆黑一团，片刻之后煤气灯点亮，屋里的陈设尽收眼底：屋子当中摆一张小桌，靠墙一字排开两三把木椅；远端开着一扇小窗，门对面是个壁炉。桌上搁着半支雪茄和一份撕破的《晚间信使报》（Evening Mercury）。可我要找的不是这些，便四肢着地趴下去，在地板上四处察看。
不多时，我瞧见炉格旁有一小团纸。我拾起来，在桌边坐下，转向那正目不转睛瞧着我动作的伙计。
“咱们要找的那伙人走了以后，这间屋还有别的客人用过吗？”
“没有，先生。这两间靠里的屋谁也没进去过。”
“这话确凿？”

===Original===
“Perfectly certain.”
I took up the ball of paper, unrolled it and spread it out upon the table. To my disgust it was only the back half of an envelope, and though it had a few figures dotted about upon it, was of no possible use to us.
“Nothing there?” asked the Inspector.
“Nothing at all,” I answered bitterly, “save a few incomprehensible figures.”
“Well, in that case, we’d better be getting up to the station and see if they’ve discovered anything yet.”
“Come along, then,” I answered. “We must be quick though, for we’ve lost a lot of precious time, and every minute counts.”
===Chinese===
“确凿无疑。”
我拿起那团纸，摊开来铺在桌上。叫我泄气的是，那只是半截信封的背面，虽说上面散落着几个数字，对我们却毫无用处。
“没有收获？”督察问。
“什么都没有，”我懊恼地答道，“只有几个看不明白的数字。”
“既然如此，咱们最好上警局去瞧瞧，看那边查出什么没有。”
“那就走吧，”我答道，“不过得抓紧，宝贵的时间已经耗费太多，如今是一分一秒都耽搁不起。”

===Original===
I took up the *Evening Mercury* and followed him out to the cab, after having sincerely thanked the hotel proprietor and the barman for their courtesy. The Inspector gave the driver his orders and we set off. As we went we discussed our next movements, and while we were doing so I idly glanced at the paper I held in my hand. There was a lamp in the cab, and the light showed me on the bottom right-hand corner a round blue india-rubber stamp mark, “W. E. Maxwell, stationer and newsagent, 23, Ipswell Street, Woolahra.”
“Stop the cab!” I almost shouted. “Tell the man to drive us back to the Canary Bird as fast as he can go.”
The order was given, the cab faced round, and in less than a minute we were on our way back.
“What’s up now?” asked the astonished Inspector.
“Only that I believe I’ve got a clue,” I cried.
I did not explain any further, and in five minutes we had brought the landlord downstairs again.
===Chinese===
我拿起那份*《晚间信使报》*，向店主和酒柜伙计诚恳道了谢，跟着他出门上车。督察吩咐了车夫去向，我们便动身了。一路上我们商量着下一步怎么走，商量间我随手翻看手里那份报纸。车里点着一盏灯，灯光照出报纸右下角一枚圆形的蓝色橡皮图章：“W·E·马克斯韦尔（W. E. Maxwell），书报文具商，伍拉拉（Woolahra）伊普斯韦尔街（Ipswell Street）23 号。”
“停车！”我几乎喊出声来，“叫车夫掉头，多快赶回金丝雀鸟旅馆去！”
命令传下去，马车掉过头，不到一分钟，我们已在回程路上。
“又怎么了？”督察愕然问。
“没什么，只怕我摸着线索了。”我喊道。
我没有多作解释，五分钟后，我们又一次把店主请下了楼。

===Original===
“I’m sorry to trouble you in this fashion,” I cried, “but life and death depend on it. I want you to let me see No. 5 again.”
He conducted us to the room, and once more the gas was lit. The small strip of envelope lay upon the table just as I had thrown it down. I seated myself and again looked closely at it. Then I sprang to my feet.
“I thought so!” I cried excitedly, pointing to the paper; “I told you I had a clue. Now, Mr. Inspector, who wrote those figures?”
“The man you call Nikola, I suppose.”
“That’s right. Now who would have bought this newspaper? You must remember that Thompson only left his box to come in here.”
“Nikola, I suppose.”
===Chinese===
“这般三番两次搅扰，实在过意不去，”我说，“可这实在是人命关天。请您让我再看一眼 5 号房。”
他把我们领进那间屋，煤气灯又点了起来。那半截信封还摊在桌上，正是我撂下时的样子。我坐下，又凑近细看，随即一跃而起。
“果然不出所料！”我激动地指着那张纸喊道，“我说过我摸着线索了。督察先生，您说，那些数字是谁写的？”
“想必就是你称作尼古拉的那个人。”
“正是。那么这份报纸又会是谁买的？您别忘了，汤普森离开赶车的座位，不过是进这店里来一趟。”
“想必也是尼古拉。”

===Original===
“Very good. Then according to your own showing Nikola owned this piece of envelope and this *Evening Mercury*. If that is certain, look here!”
He came round and looked over my shoulder. I pointed to what was evidently part of the gummed edge of the top of the envelope. On it were these three important words, “﻿—swell Street, Woolahra.”
“Well,” he said, “what about it?”
“Why, look here!” I said, as I opened the Evening Mercury and pointed to the stamp-mark at the bottom. “The man who bought this newspaper at Mr. Maxwell’s shop also bought this envelope there. The letters ‘swell’ before ‘street’ constitute the last half of Ipswell, the name of the street. If that man be Nikola, as we suspect, the person who served him is certain to remember him, and it is just within the bounds of possibility he may know his address.”
“That’s so,” said the Inspector, who was struck with the force of my argument. “I know Mr. Maxwell’s shop, and our best plan will be to go on there as fast as we can.”
Again thanking the landlord for his civility, we returned to our cab and once more set off, this time for Mr. Maxwell’s shop in Ipswell Street. By the time we reached it, it was nearly three o’clock, and gradually growing light.
===Chinese===
“很好。那么照您自己这套推论，这半截信封和这份*《晚间信使报》*，就都是尼古拉的物件。倘若这一点坐实了，您瞧这个！”
他绕到我跟前，从我肩头上望下来。我指着信封上端封口胶边残存的那一小条。上面留着三个关键的字样：“—swell 街，伍拉拉”。
“唔，”他说，“这又怎样？”
“您瞧这儿！”我说着摊开《晚间信使报》，指给他看报尾的图章。“在马克斯韦尔先生铺子里买这份报纸的人，顺手也在那儿买了这个信封。‘街’字前头那几个字母 swell，正是 Ipswell——那条街名——的后半截。倘若那人真如我们所料是尼古拉，经手卖货的伙计必定记得他，而且没准儿还能知道他的住处，这希望虽小，却也并非全无可能。”
“有道理，”督察说，我这番推论显然说动了他，“马克斯韦尔先生的铺子我知道，咱们眼下最要紧的，就是尽快赶到那儿去。”
又向店主道了一回谢，我们回到车上再次出发，这一回是去伊普斯韦尔街马克斯韦尔先生的铺子。赶到那儿时，已近凌晨三点，天色渐渐发白了。

===Original===
As the cab drew up alongside the curb the Inspector jumped out and rang the bell at the side door. It was opened after awhile by a shock-headed youth, about eighteen years of age, who stared at us in sleepy astonishment.
“Does Mr. Maxwell live at the shop?” asked the Inspector.
“No, sir.”
“Where then?”
“Ponson Street﻿—third house on the left-hand side.”
“Thank you.”
===Chinese===
马车挨着路缘石停稳，督察跳下车，去拉侧门的铃。过了一会儿，门开了，探出一个蓬头乱发的小伙子，年约十八岁，睡意惺忪地瞪着我们。
“马克斯韦尔先生就住在铺子里吗？”督察问。
“不住，先生。”
“那住在哪儿？”
“庞森街（Ponson Street）——左手边第三栋。”
“多谢。”

===Original===
Once more we jumped into the cab and rattled off. It seemed to me, so anxious and terrified was I for my darling’s safety, that we were fated never to get the information we wanted; the whole thing was like some nightmare, in which, try how I would to move, every step was clogged.
A few minutes’ drive brought us to Ponson Street, and we drew up at the third house on the left-hand side. It was a pretty little villa, with a nice front garden and a creeper-covered verandah. We rang the bell and waited. Presently we heard someone coming down the passage, and a moment later the door was unlocked.
“Who is there?” cried a voice from within.
“Police,” said my companion as before.
The door was immediately opened, and a very small sandy-complexioned man, dressed in a flaring suit of striped pyjamas, stood before us.
“Is anything wrong, gentlemen?” he asked nervously.
===Chinese===
我们又一次跳上马车，辘辘驶去。我的心上人吉凶未卜，我又是焦急又是惊惶，只觉得我们命中注定休想问出想要的消息；整件事就像一场噩梦，任我怎么奔命，每迈一步都像陷在泥里。
几分钟的车程便到了庞森街，我们在左手边第三栋房子前停住。那是一座秀气的小宅子，前院花草宜人，一条藤蔓爬满的廊子。我们按了铃等着。不多时，听见有人穿过过道走来，紧接着门锁一响。
“谁呀？”屋里一个声音问。
“警察。”我那同伴照旧答道。
门应声而开，一个个头很矮、面色沙黄的汉子站在我们面前，一身扎眼的条纹睡衣裤。
“出了什么事吗，二位先生？”他不安地问。

===Original===
“Nothing to affect you, Mr. Maxwell,” my companion replied. “We only want a little important information, if you can give it us. We are anxious to discover a man’s whereabouts before daylight, and we have been led to believe that you are the only person who can give us the necessary clue.”
“Good gracious! I never heard of such a thing. But I shall be happy to serve you if I can,” the little man answered, leading the way into his dining-room and opening the shutters with an air of importance his appearance rather belied. “What is it?”
“Well, it’s this,” I replied, producing the piece of envelope and the Evening Mercury. “You see these letters on the top of this paper, don’t you?” He nodded, his attention at once secured by seeing his own name. “Well, that envelope was evidently purchased in your shop. So was this newspaper.”
“How can you tell that?”
“In the case of the envelope, by these letters; in that of the paper, by your rubber stamp on the bottom.”
“Ah! Well, now, and in what way can I help you?”
===Chinese===
“跟您无关，马克斯韦尔先生，”我同伴答道，“只想跟您讨一点要紧的消息，不知您可愿赐告。我们急着要在天亮之前查明一个人的下落，据我们一路追下来的线索，能给出关键门径的，唯有先生您一人。”
“老天爷！这种事我可从没听说过。不过只要帮得上，乐意效劳。”小个子答道，把我们领进饭厅，又郑重其事地推开百叶窗——那份煞有介事的架势，跟他的模样颇不相称。“什么事？”
“是这样，”我答道，掏出那半截信封和那份《晚间信使报》，“您瞧见这纸头上端的这几个字母了吧？”他一眼看见自家的名号，注意力顿时被勾住了，点了点头。“唔，这个信封，分明是在您铺子里买的。这份报纸也是。”
“这您怎么断定的？”
“信封嘛，凭这几个字母；报纸嘛，凭报尾您那枚橡皮图章。”
“哦！那么，我能帮上什么忙呢？”

===Original===
“We want to know the address of the man who bought them.”
“That will surely be difficult. Can you give me any idea of what he was like?”
“Tall, slightly foreign in appearance, distinctly handsome, sallow complexion, very dark eyes, black hair, small hands and feet.”
As my description progressed the little man’s face brightened. Then he cried with evident triumph﻿—
“I know the man; he came into the shop yesterday afternoon.”
“And his address is?”
===Chinese===
“我们想知道，买这两样东西的人住在哪儿。”
“那可难办了。能说说他长什么模样吗？”
“高个子，相貌略带外国气，眉目分明称得上英俊，面色蜡黄，眼睛极黑，黑头发，手脚都小。”
我这一路描摹下去，小个子脸上的神色一路亮起来。随即他得意洋洋地喊道——
“这人我认得，昨天下午来过铺子里。”
“他住哪儿？”

===Original===
His face fell again. His information was not quite as helpful as he had expected it would be.
“There I can’t help you, I’m sorry to say. He bought a packet of paper and envelopes and the *Evening Mercury* and then left the shop. I was so struck by his appearance that I went to the door and watched him cross the road.”
“And in which direction did he go?”
“Over to Podgers’ chemist shop across the way. That was the last I saw of him.”
“I’m obliged to you, Mr. Maxwell,” I said, shaking him by the hand. “But I’m sorry you can’t tell us something more definite about him.” Then turning to the Inspector: “I suppose we had better go off and find Podgers. But if we have to spend much more time in rushing about like this we shall be certain to lose them altogether.”
“Let us be off to Podgers, then, as fast as we can go.”
===Chinese===
他脸上又挂了下来。他掌握的情况，并不像他自己指望的那样顶用。
“这我可帮不上忙了，抱歉得很。他买了一沓信纸信封，还有那份*《晚间信使报》*，就出了铺子。他那副容貌实在惹眼，我不由得走到门口，望着他穿过马路。”
“往哪边去了？”
“到马路对过的波杰斯（Podgers）药房去了。那是我最后一次看见他。”
“多谢您了，马克斯韦尔先生，”我握着他的手说，“只可惜您说不出更确实的消息。”说罢转向督察：“我看咱们还是赶紧去找波杰斯吧。只是照这么东奔西跑地耗下去，只怕终究要把他们跟丢了。”
“那就快去波杰斯那儿，能多快赶多快。”

===Original===
Bidding Mr. Maxwell goodbye, we set off again, and in ten minutes had arrived at the shop and had Mr. Podgers downstairs. We explained our errand as briefly as possible, and gave a minute description of the man we wanted.
“I remember him perfectly,” said the sedate Podgers. “He came into my shop last night and purchased a bottle of chloroform.”
“You made him sign the poison book, of course?”
“Naturally I did, Mr. Inspector. Would you like to see his signature?”
“Very much,” we both answered at once, and the book was accordingly produced.
Podgers ran his finger down the list.
===Chinese===
同马克斯韦尔先生道了别，我们再次动身，十分钟后便到了那家药房，把波杰斯先生请下了楼。我们把来意尽量简短地说明，又把要找的那人的相貌细细描述了一遍。
“我记得清清楚楚，”一向沉稳的波杰斯说，“昨晚他来过我的铺子，买了一瓶氯仿（chloroform）。”
“那您自然让他签了毒药登记簿吧？”
“自然签了，督察先生。要不要看看他的签名？”
“正要看看。”我们不约而同地说，登记簿随即取了来。
波杰斯的手指顺着名单往下捋。

===Original===
“Brown, Williams, Davis﻿—ah! here it is. ‘Chloroform: J. Venneage, 22, Calliope Street, Woolahra.’ ”
“Venneage!” I cried. “Why, that’s not his name!”
“Very likely not,” replied Podgers; “but it’s the name he gave me.”
“Never mind, we’ll try 22, Calliope Street on the chance,” said the Inspector. “Come along, Mr. Hatteras.”
Again we drove off, this time at increased pace. In less than fifteen minutes we had turned into the street we wanted, and pulled up about a hundred yards from the junction. It was a small thoroughfare, with a long line of second-class villa residences on either side. A policeman was sauntering along on the opposite side of the way, and the Inspector called him over. He saluted respectfully, and waited to be addressed.
“What do you know of number 22?” asked the Inspector briefly. The constable considered for a few moments, and then said:
===Chinese===
“布朗、威廉斯、戴维斯——啊！在这儿。‘氯仿一瓶：J·维尼奇（J. Venneage），伍拉拉卡利俄佩街（Calliope Street）22 号。’”
“维尼奇！”我叫出声来，“嗐，那哪是他的名字！”
“多半不是，”波杰斯答道，“可他留给我的是这个名字。”
“不要紧，卡利俄佩街 22 号，碰碰运气也去一趟，”督察说，“走吧，哈特拉斯先生。”
我们又驱车上路，这一回催得更急。不到一刻钟，马车已拐进要找的那条街，在离街口约一百码的地方停住。这是条小街，两侧一线排开长长的二等小宅子。街那头有个警察在溜达，督察把他唤了过去。他规规矩矩敬了个礼，候着问话。
“22 号那宅子，你知道些什么？”督察开门见山地问。那巡警寻思了片刻，答道：

===Original===
“Well, to tell you the truth, sir, I didn’t know until yesterday that it was occupied.”
“Have you seen anybody about there?”
“I saw three men go in just as I came on the beat tonight.”
“What were they like?”
“Well, I don’t know that I looked much at them. They were all pretty big, and they seemed to be laughing and enjoying themselves.”
“Did they! Well, we must go in there and have a look at them. You had better come with us.”
===Chinese===
“呃，说实话，先生，直到昨天我还不知道那房子里住了人。”
“在那儿附近见过什么人吗？”
“今晚我刚上岗巡逻那阵子，亲眼见三个人进了那院门。”
“什么样儿的三个人？”
“呃，我也没怎么细瞧。个个都挺壮实，一路说说笑笑，挺得意的样子。”
“是吗！好，咱们得进去瞧瞧。你跟我们一起去。”

===Original===
We walked on down the street till we arrived at No. 22. Then opening the gate we went up the steps to the hall door. It was quite light enough by this time to enable us to see everything distinctly. The Inspector gave the bell a good pull and the peal reechoed inside the house. But not a sound of any living being came from within in answer. Again the bell was pulled, and once more we waited patiently, but with the same result.
“Either there’s nobody at home or they refuse to hear,” said the Inspector. “Constable, you remain where you are and collar the first man you see. Mr. Hatteras, we will go round to the back and try to effect an entrance from there.”
We left the front door, and finding a path reached the yard. The house was only a small one, with a little verandah at the rear on to which the back door opened. On either side of the door were two fair-sized windows, and by some good fortune it chanced that the catch of one of these was broken.
Lifting the sash up the Inspector jumped into the room, and its soon as he was through I followed him. Then we looked about us. The room, however, was destitute of furniture or occupants.
“I don’t hear anybody about,” my companion said, opening the door that led into the hall. Just at that moment I heard a sound, and touching his arm signed to him to listen. We both did so, and sure enough there came again the faint muttering of a human voice. In the half-dark of the hall it sounded most uncanny.
“Somebody in one of the front rooms,” said the Inspector. “I’ll slip along and open the front door, bring in the man from outside, and then we’ll burst into the room and take our chance of capturing them.”
===Chinese===
我们沿街走过去，到了 22 号门前。推开院门，拾级而上，到了正门外。这时天光已亮，看什么都清清楚楚。督察使劲拉了拉门铃，铃声在屋里空空地回荡，屋里却没有一点活人的声息应答。铃又拉了一回，我们再度耐心等候，结果照旧。
“要么是没人在家，要么是存心不应，”督察说，“巡警，你就守在这儿，见人就揪住别放。哈特拉斯先生，咱们绕到后头，想法子从后面进去。”
我们离开正门，寻着一条小径进了后院。房子不大，后头带一方小廊子，后门便开在廊上。门两侧各有两扇不小的窗子，说来也巧，其中一扇的窗闩偏偏是坏的。
督察托起窗框，翻身跳进屋里，他前脚刚落地，我也跟着钻了进去。我们四下张望。这屋子里家具什物一无所有，人也一个没有。
“没听见有什么动静。”我同伴说着，推开通向门厅的门。恰在此时，我听见一点声响，忙碰碰他的手臂，示意他细听。我们都屏息听去，果然，又传来一阵人声的模糊咕哝。在半明半暗的门厅里，那声音听得人毛骨悚然。
“前屋里有人，”督察说，“我溜过去开正门，把外头那位巡警带进来，然后咱们冲进屋去，逮着谁算谁。”

===Original===
He did as he proposed, and when the constable had joined us we moved towards the room on the left.
Again the mutterings came from the inside, and the Inspector turned the handle of the door. It was locked, however.
“Let me burst it in,” I whispered.
He nodded, and I accordingly put my shoulder against it, and bringing my strength to bear sent it flying in.
Then we rushed into the room, to find it, at first glance, empty. Just at that moment, however, the muttering began again, and we looked towards the darkest corner; somebody was there, lying on the ground. I rushed across and knelt down to look. It was Beckenham; his mouth gagged and his hands and feet bound. The noise we had heard was that made by him trying to call us to his assistance.
In less time than it takes to tell I had cut his bonds and helped him to sit up. Then I explained to the Inspector who he was.
===Chinese===
他依计而行，等巡警进来会合，我们便朝左手那间屋逼过去。
屋里的咕哝声又起，督察去拧门把手。门却锁着。
“我来撞开它。”我低声说。
他点点头，我便把肩膀顶上门板，运足力气，轰然把门撞了开去。
我们冲进屋去，乍一看，屋里空无一人。可就在此时，那咕哝声又响起来，我们朝最暗的角落望去——有人躺在地上。我抢过去跪下细看，竟是贝肯汉姆！嘴里塞着东西，手脚都捆着。我们先前听见的那声响，正是他拼命想唤我们过去救他的动静。
说时迟那时快，我已割断绳索，扶他坐起，又向督察说明了他的身份。

===Original===
“Thank God you’re found!” I cried. “But what does it all mean? How long have you been like this? and where is Nikola?”
“I don’t know how long I’ve been here,” he answered, “and I don’t know where Nikola is.”
“But you must know something about him!” I cried. “For Heaven’s sake tell me all you can! I’m in awful trouble, and your story may give me the means of saving a life that is dearer to me than my own.”
“Get me something to drink first, then,” he replied; “I’m nearly dying of thirst; after that I’ll tell you all I can.”
Fortunately I had had the foresight to put a flask of whisky into my pocket, and I now took it out and gave him a stiff nobbler. It revived him somewhat, and he prepared to begin his tale. But the Inspector interrupted:
“Before you commence, my lord, I must send word to the Commissioner that you have been found.”
===Chinese===
“感谢上帝，总算找着您了！”我喊道，“可这到底是怎么回事？您被弄成这样有多久了？尼古拉又人在哪儿？”
“我不知道自己在这儿待了多久，”他答道，“尼古拉去了哪儿，我也不知道。”
“可您总该知道点他的事吧！”我喊道，“看在老天分上，把知道的都说出来！我如今焦头烂额，您这一番经历，兴许就能给我救下一条性命——那性命在我心里，比我自己的还重。”
“那先给我弄点喝的，”他答道，“我渴得快不行了；喝完，我把知道的一切都告诉你们。”
幸而我早有先见之明，往口袋里揣了一小瓶威士忌，这时掏出来，给他倒了一大盅。他喝下去，精神稍稍恢复，正要开口讲述，却被督察拦住：
“您开讲之前，大人，我得先给总监送个信儿，就说您已经寻获了。”

===Original===
He wrote a message on a piece of paper and dispatched the constable with it. Having done so he turned to Beckenham and said:
“Now, my lord, pray let us hear your story.”
Beckenham forthwith commenced.
===Chinese===
他在一张纸上写了封信，打发巡警送去。办完这事，他转向贝肯汉姆说：
“好了，大人，请给我们讲讲您的经历吧。”
贝肯汉姆随即讲了起来。

## VIII Our Imprisonment and Attempt at Escape / 第八章 身陷囹圄与脱身之计

===Original===
For what length of time I lay unconscious after hearing Beckenham’s cry, and feeling the cord tighten round my throat, as narrated in the preceding chapter, I have not the remotest idea; I only know that when my senses returned to me again I found myself in complete darkness. The cord was gone from my neck, it is true, but something was still encircling it in a highly unpleasant fashion. On putting my hand up to it, to my intense astonishment, I discovered it to be a collar of iron, padlocked at the side, and communicating with a wall at the back by means of a stout chain fixed in a ring, which again was attached to a swivel.

This ominous discovery set me hunting about to find out where I was, and for a clue as to what these things might mean. That I was in a room was evident from the fact that, by putting my hands behind me, I could touch two walls forming a corner. But in what part of the town such a room might be was beyond my telling. One thing was evident, however, the walls were of brick, unplastered and quite innocent of paper.

As not a ray of light relieved the darkness I put my hand into my ticket pocket, where I was accustomed to carry matches, and finding that my captors had not deprived me of them, lit one and looked about me. It was a dismal scene that little gleam illumined. The room in which I was confined was a small one, being only about ten feet long by eight wide, while, if I had been able to stand upright, I might have raised my hand to within two or three inches of the ceiling. In the furthest left-hand corner was a door, while in the wall on the right, but hopelessly beyond my reach, was a low window almost completely boarded up. I had no opportunity of seeing more, for by the time I had realised these facts the match had burnt down to my fingers. I blew it out and hastened to light another.

Just as I did so a low moan reached my ear. It came from the further end of the room. Again I held the match aloft; this time to discover a huddled-up figure in the corner opposite the door. One glance at it told me that it was none other than my young friend the Marquis of Beckenham. He was evidently still unconscious, for though I called him twice by name, he did not answer, but continued in the same position, moaning softly as before. I had only time for a hurried glance at him before my last match burned down to my fingers, and had to be extinguished. With the departure of the light a return of faintness seized me, and I fell back into my corner, if not quite insensible, certainly unconscious of the immediate awkwardness of our position.

It was daylight when my power of thinking returned to me, and long shafts of sunshine were percolating into us through the chinks in the boards upon the window. To my dismay the room looked even smaller and dingier than when I had examined it by the light of my match some hours before. The young Marquis lay unconscious in his corner just as I had last seen him, but with the widening light I discovered that his curious posture was due more to extraneous circumstances than to his own weakness, for I could see that he was fastened to the wall by a similar collar to my own.

I took out my watch, which had not been taken from me as I might have expected, and examined the dial. It wanted five minutes of six o’clock. So putting it back into my pocket, I set myself for the second time to try and discover where we were. By reason of my position and the chain that bound me, this could only be done by listening, so I shut my eyes and put all my being into my ears. For some moments no sound rewarded my attention. Then a cock in a neighbouring yard on my right crowed lustily, a dog on my left barked, and a moment later I heard the faint sound of someone coming along the street. The pedestrian, whoever he might be, was approaching from the right hand, and, what was still more important, my trained ear informed me that he was lame of one leg, and walked with crutches. Closer and closer he came. But to my surprise he did not pass the window; indeed, I noticed that when he came level with it the sound was completely lost to me. This told me two things: one, that the window, which, as I have already said, was boarded up, did not look into the main thoroughfare; the other, that the street itself ran along on the far side of the very wall to which my chain was attached.
===Chinese===
听了贝肯汉姆的喊声、又觉绳子在喉间收紧之后——正如前一章所述——我昏迷了多久，自己毫无概念；我只知道再度恢复知觉时，正处于一片漆黑之中。脖子上的绳子倒是不见了，可仍有什么东西以一种极不舒坦的方式箍在那里。我抬手一摸，不禁大吃一惊：那竟是一只铁领圈，侧面用挂锁锁着，后面连着一条粗链，链子固定在一只环上，那只环又接在一个转环上。

这一凶多吉少的发现促使我四下摸索，想弄清自己身在何处，也想找出这些东西究竟意味着什么的线索。我在一间屋子里，这显而易见，因为只要把双手伸向身后，便能触到两面成直角的墙。可这样的屋子究竟坐落在城中哪一处，我便无从说起了。不过有一点很明显：墙是砖砌的，未抹灰泥，也全然没有糊过墙纸的痕迹。
