由于没有一丝光亮冲破黑暗，我把手伸进票袋——我习惯在那里放火柴——发觉掳我的人没有把它们搜走，便划燃一根，环顾四周。那一星微光所照亮的，是一派凄惨景象。囚禁我的这间屋子很小，长宽不过约十英尺乘八英尺；倘若我能站直身子，一举手便离天花板只有两三英寸了。最左边的角落里有一扇门，右墙上则有一扇几乎被木板完全钉死的矮窗，可惜够不着。我来不及看得更多，因为等我把这些弄清，火柴已烧到指头。我吹熄它，赶紧又点上一根。

就在这时，一声低低的呻吟传进我耳中，是从屋子那头传来的。我又一次把火柴举高，这回发现门对面的角落里蜷缩着一团人影。一眼便认出，那不是别人，正是我的年轻朋友贝肯汉姆侯爵。他显然仍昏迷不醒，因为我连唤两声他的名字，他都没有应答，依旧保持着原来的姿势，轻声呻吟如故。我只来得及匆匆瞥他一眼，最后一根火柴便烧到了指头，只得熄灭。光亮一去，一阵晕眩又向我袭来，我颓然倒回自己的角落里，即便没有完全失去知觉，也全然没有意识到我们眼下的处境有多么不妙。

待我恢复思考能力时，天已大亮，长长的阳光透过窗板上木条的缝隙渗进来，落在我们身上。令我沮丧的是，这屋子比我几小时前借火柴光查看时显得更小、更阴暗了。年轻的侯爵仍照我上次见到的模样昏迷在角落里，但随着光线渐亮，我发现他那古怪的姿势与其说是自身虚弱所致，不如说是外力造成——我看得出来，他也被一只同我一样的领圈拴在墙上。

我掏出怀表——它并没有如我料想的那样被拿走——看了看表盘，差五分六点。我把表放回口袋，第二次试着弄清我们的所在。由于我的位置和拴着我的锁链，这只能靠耳朵来完成，于是我便闭上双眼，把全副心神都灌注到听觉上。起初好一阵子，什么声音也没有。随后，右边邻家院里一只公鸡高声啼叫，左边一条狗吠了起来，片刻之后，我又听见有人沿街走来的轻微声响。那行人不管是谁，是从右手边过来的，而更要紧的是，我这双训练有素的耳朵告诉我，他一条腿是瘸的，拄着双拐走路。他越走越近。可令我诧异的是，他并没有从窗前经过；而且我注意到，他一走到与窗户齐平的地方，声音便完全听不见了。这告诉我两件事：其一，如我先前所说，那扇钉了木板的窗并不临大街；其二，街道本身正沿着我锁链所系那面墙的另一侧延伸。

===Original===
As I arrived at the knowledge of this fact, Beckenham opened his eyes; he sat up as well as his chain would permit, and gazed about him in a dazed fashion. Then his right hand went up to the iron collar enclosing his neck, and when he had realized what it meant he appeared even more mystified than before. He seemed to doze again for a minute or so, then his eyes opened, and as they did so they fell upon me, and his perplexity found relief in words.

“Mr. Hatteras,” he said, in a voice like that of a man talking in his sleep, “where are we and what on earth does this chain mean?”

“You ask me something that I want to know myself,” I answered. “I cannot tell you where we are, except that we are in Port Said. But if you want to know what I think it means, well, I think it means treachery. How do you feel now?”

“Very sick indeed, and my head aches horribly. But I can’t understand it at all. What do you mean by saying that it is treachery?”

This was the one question of all others I had been dreading, for I could not help feeling that when all was said and done I was bitterly to blame. However, unpleasant or not, the explanation had to be got through, and without delay.

“Lord Beckenham,” I began, sitting upright and clasping my hands round my knees, “this is a pretty bad business for me. I haven’t the reputation of being a coward, but I’ll own I feel pretty rocky and mean when I see you sitting there on the floor with that iron collar round your neck and that chain holding you to the wall, and know that it’s, in a measure, all my stupid, blundering folly that has brought it about.”
===Chinese===
就在我弄明白这一点的当儿，贝肯汉姆睁开了眼睛；他趁锁链容许勉强坐起身，茫然地环顾四周。随后他右手摸上箍住脖子的铁领圈，等弄明白那是怎么回事，他显得比先前更加困惑了。他似乎又打了一分钟左右的盹，然后睁开眼，目光落在我身上，满腹疑团终于化作言语倾吐出来。

“哈特拉斯先生，”他用一种恍如梦呓的声音说道，“我们这是在哪儿？这条链子到底是什么意思？”

“你问我的，正是我自己也想知道的事，”我答道，“我们身在何处，我无法告诉你，只知道这里是塞得港（Port Said）。可你要问我的看法——嗯，我看这意味着背叛。你现在感觉如何？”

“难受得很，头疼得厉害。可我完全弄不明白。你说这是背叛，是什么意思？”

这正是所有问题中我最怕听到的一个，因为我不由得觉得，归根结底，这一切都怪我不好。然而，不管多么难堪，事情总得解释清楚，而且刻不容缓。

“贝肯汉姆勋爵，”我坐直身子，双手抱膝，开口道，“这事对我而言可真是糟透了。我并非以懦夫闻名，可我承认，看见你脖子上箍着铁领圈、被链子拴在墙边坐在地上，又知道这在相当程度上全是我愚蠢莽撞的昏招招来的，我心里实在又发虚又惭愧。”

===Original===
“Oh, don’t say that, Mr. Hatteras!” was the young man’s generous reply. “For whatever or whoever may be to blame for it, I’m sure you’re not.”

“That’s because you don’t know everything, my lord. Wait till you have heard what I have to tell you before you give me such complete absolution.”

“I’m not going to blame you whatever you may tell me; but please go on!”

There and then I set to work and told him all that had happened to me since my arrival in London; informed him of my meeting with Nikola, of Wetherell’s hasty departure for Australia, of my distrust for Baxter, described the telegram incident and Baxter’s curious behaviour afterwards, narrated my subsequent meeting with the two men in the Green Sailor Hotel, described my journey to Plymouth, and finished with the catastrophe that had happened to me there.

“Now you see,” I said in conclusion, “why I regard myself as being so much to blame.”

“Excuse me,” he answered, “but I cannot say that I see it in the same light at all.”
===Chinese===
“噢，别这么说，哈特拉斯先生！”年轻人宽宏大量地答道，“不管这事该怪什么、怪谁，反正肯定不该怪你。”

“那是因为你还不知道全部缘由，大人。等你听完我要说的话，再这般全然赦免我不迟。”

“不管你要说什么，我都不会怪你；不过请快讲吧！”

当下我便一五一十地对他讲了我到伦敦以后发生的一切：告诉他我如何遇见尼古拉博士，韦瑟雷尔如何匆匆启程去了澳大利亚，我如何对巴克斯特心存疑忌；又描述了那封电报的风波以及巴克斯特事后古怪的举动，叙述了我随后在“绿衣水手”酒馆里与那两人的会面，讲了我去普利茅斯（Plymouth）的旅程，最后以我在那儿遭的祸事作结。

“现在你明白了吧，”我最后说道，“为什么我觉得自己难辞其咎。”

“恕我直言，”他答道，“我可完全不能这样看。”

===Original===
“I’m afraid I must be more explicit then. In the first place you must understand that, without a shadow of a doubt, Baxter was chosen for your tutor by Nikola, whose agent he undoubtedly is, for a specific purpose. Now what do you think that purpose was? You don’t know? To induce your father to let you travel, to be sure. You ask why they should want you to travel? We’ll come to that directly. Their plan is succeeding admirably, when I come upon the scene and, like the great blundering idiot I am, must needs set to work unconsciously to assist them in their nefarious designs. Your father eventually consents, and it is arranged that you shall set off for Australia at once. Then it is discovered that I am going to leave in the same boat. This does not suit Nikola’s plans at all, so he determines to prevent my sailing with you. By a happy chance he is unsuccessful, and I follow and join the boat in Naples. Good gracious! I see something else now.”

“What is that?”

“Simply this. I could not help thinking at the time that your bout of seasickness between Naples and this infernal place was extraordinary. Well, if I’m not very much mistaken, you were physicked, and it was Baxter’s doing.”

“But why?”

“Ah! That’s yet to be discovered. But you may bet your bottom dollar it was some part of their devilish conspiracy. I’m as certain of that as that we are here now. Now here’s another point. Do you remember my running out of the Casino last night? Well, that was because I saw Nikola standing in the roadway watching us.”

“Are you certain? How could he have got here? And what could his reasons be for watching us?”
===Chinese===
“那么恐怕我得说得更明白些。首先你必须明白，毫无疑问，巴克斯特正是尼古拉博士——他无疑是尼古拉的代理人——为了特定目的挑选来做你家庭教师的。你猜那目的是什么？猜不着？就是要诱使你父亲同意让你出外旅行。你要问他们为什么想让你旅行？这个我们马上就谈。他们的计划进展得一帆风顺，偏偏我插了进来，像我这等头号笨蛋，竟不知不觉地帮着他们推行那邪恶的图谋。你父亲最终点了头，安排你即刻动身去澳大利亚。这时他们发现我打算乘同一条船走。这完全不合尼古拉博士的计划，于是他决意阻止我与你们同船。侥幸他没有得逞，我便随后在那不勒斯（Naples）赶上了船。天哪！我现在又看出一层问题。”

“什么问题？”

“很简单。我当时就忍不住觉得，你从那不勒斯到这个鬼地方之间那场晕船晕得蹊跷。嗯，要是我没有大错特错，你是被人下了药，而那是巴克斯特干的好事。”

“可这是为什么？”

“啊！那还有待查明。但你尽管把最后一枚铜板都押上去赌——那是他们恶魔阴谋的一环。这一点，我可以拿我们眼下坐在这儿打赌。再说另一件事。你还记得昨晚我冲出赌场的事吗？那是因为我看见尼古拉博士站在马路上监视我们。”

“你确定吗？他怎么会到这儿来？他监视我们又能有什么缘由？”

===Original===
“Why, can’t you see? To find out how his plot is succeeding, to be sure.”

“And that brings us back to our original question﻿—what is that plot?”

“That’s rather more difficult to answer! But if you ask my candid opinion I should say nothing more nor less than to make you prisoner and blackmail your father for a ransom.”

For some few minutes neither of us spoke. The outlook seemed too hopeless for words, and the Marquis was still too weak to keep up an animated conversation for any length of time. He sat leaning his head on his hand. But presently he looked up again.

“My poor father!” he said. “What a state he will be in!”

“And what worries me more,” I answered, “is how he will regret ever having listened to my advice. What a dolt I was not to have told him of my suspicions.”
===Chinese===
“嗨，你还看不出来吗？当然是想看看他的阴谋进展如何。”

“这就又回到我们最初的问题上了——那阴谋究竟是什么？”

“这个可就难答多了！不过你若问我的坦率看法，我说他们的图谋不外乎把你掳为人质，再向你父亲勒索赎金。”

有那么几分钟，我们俩谁都没有说话。前景似已绝望得无以言表，侯爵也仍太过虚弱，撑不了多久的活跃谈话。他坐着，以手支头。但不一会儿，他又抬起眼来。

“我可怜的父亲！”他说，“他不知要急成什么样子！”

“而更叫我忧心的，”我答道，“是他会如何懊悔当初听了我的主意。我竟没有把自己的疑心告诉他，真是蠢透了。”


===Original===
“You must not blame yourself for that. I am sure my father would hold you as innocent as I do. Now let us consider our position. In the first place, where are we, do you think? In the second, is there any possible chance of escape?”

“To the first my answer is, ‘don’t know;’ to the second, ‘can’t say.’ I have discovered one thing, however, and that is that the street does not lie outside that window, but runs along on the other side of this wall behind me. The window, I suspect, looks out on to some sort of a courtyard. But unfortunately that information is not much use to us, as we can neither of us move away from where we are placed.”

“Is there no other way?”

“Not one, as far as I can tell. Can you see anything on your side?”

“Nothing at all, unless we could get at the door. But what’s that sticking out of the wall near your feet?”

To get a better view of it I stooped as much as I was able.
===Chinese===
“这一点你可不能自责。我敢说，我父亲会和我一样认为你是无辜的。现在让我们合计一下处境吧。首先，你想我们身在何处？其次，究竟有没有逃脱的可能？”

“对第一个问题，我的回答是‘不知道’；对第二个，是‘说不上’。不过我倒弄明白了一件事：街道不在那扇窗外面，而是沿着我背后这面墙的另一侧延伸。我猜那扇窗朝着某种院子。可惜这消息对我们没多大用处，因为我们谁也挪不开各自待的地方。”

“就没有别的法子了吗？”

“就我看，一个也没有。你那边能看见什么吗？”

“什么也没有，除非咱们够得着那扇门。咦，你脚边从墙里伸出来的那个是什么？”

为了看清它，我竭力俯下身去。

===Original===
“It looks like a pipe.”

The end of a pipe it certainly was, and sticking out into the room but where it led to, and why it had been cut off in this peculiar fashion, were two questions I could no more answer than I could fly.

“Does it run out into the street, do you think?” was Beckenham’s immediate query. “If so, you might manage to call through it to some passerby, and ask him to obtain assistance for us!”

“A splendid notion if I could get my mouth anywhere within a foot of it, but as this chain will not permit me to do that, it might as well be a hundred miles off. It’s as much as I can do to touch it with my fingers.”

“Do you think if you had a stick you could push a piece of paper through? We might write a message on it.”

“Possibly, but there’s another drawback to that. I haven’t the necessary piece of stick.”
===Chinese===
“看着像根管子。”

那确实是根管子的端口，直伸进屋子里来；可它通向哪里，又为什么以这般古怪的方式被截断，这两个问题我全然无法作答——正如我不会飞一样。

“你看它是不是通到街上？”贝肯汉姆立刻问道，“要是那样，你也许能凑着它朝过路人呼喊，请他给我们弄些救援来！”

“好主意——只要我的嘴能凑到离它一英尺之内；可这条链子偏不容我做到，那它便与远在一百英里外无异。我顶多用指头碰得着它。”

“你想，要是有一根小棍，能不能把一张纸条从里面捅过去？咱们可以在上面写封求救信。”

“也许成，可这法子另有一桩难处：我没有那么一根棍子。”

===Original===
“Here is a stiff piece of straw; try that.”

He harpooned a piece of straw, about eight inches long, across the room towards me, and, when I had received it, I thrust it carefully into the pipe. A disappointment, however, was in store for us.

“It’s no use,” I reported sorrowfully, as I threw the straw away. “It’s an elbow halfway down, and that would prevent any message from being pushed through.”

“Then we must try to discover some other plan. Don’t lose heart!”

“Hush! I hear somebody coming.”

True enough a heavy footfall was approaching down the passage. It stopped at the door of the room in which we were confined, and a key was inserted in the lock. Next moment the door swung open and a tall man entered the room. A ray of sunlight, penetrating between the boards that covered the window, fell upon him, and showed us that his hair was white and that his face was deeply pitted with smallpox marks. Now, where had I met or heard of a man with those two peculiarities before? Ah! I remembered!
===Chinese===
“这有一根挺硬的麦秆，试试它。”

他把一根约八英寸长的麦秆像掷鱼叉似的从屋子那头朝我掷过来，我接住后，小心地把它捅进管子。然而等着我们的是一场失望。

“不顶用，”我把麦秆一扔，沮丧地报告道，“管子半道上有个弯头，什么信也休想捅过去。”

“那咱们就得另想法子。别灰心！”

“嘘！我听见有人来了。”

果然，一阵沉重的脚步声顺着过道走近，在我们囚室的门口停下，一把钥匙插进了锁孔。紧接着门豁然打开，一个高个子男人走进屋来。一缕阳光从遮窗的木板缝间透进来，落在他身上，照出他一头白发和一张密布天花瘢痕的脸。咦，我从前在哪儿见过、或是听说过兼具这两个特征的人？啊！我想起来了！

===Original===
He stood for a moment in the doorway looking about him, and then strolled into the centre of the room.

“Good morning, gentlemen,” he said, with an airy condescension that stung like an insult; “I trust you have no fault to find with the lodging our poor hospitality is able to afford you.”

“Mr. Prendergast,” I answered, determined to try him with the name of the man mentioned by my sweetheart in her letter. “What does this mean? Why have we been made prisoners like this? I demand to be released at once. You will have to answer to our consul for this detention.”

For a brief space he appeared to be dumbfounded by my knowledge of his name. But he soon recovered himself and leaned his back against the wall, looking us both carefully over before he answered.

“I shall be only too pleased,” he said sneeringly, “but if you’ll allow me to say so, I don’t think we need trouble about explanations yet awhile.”

“Pray, what do you mean by that?”
===Chinese===
他在门口站了片刻，环顾四周，然后踱到房间中央。

“早上好，二位先生，”他说道，那副轻飘飘的居高临下腔调，刺得人如同受了侮辱一般，“想必你们对我们这份寒酸的招待所能提供的宿处，并无不满吧。”

“普伦德加斯特先生，”我答道，决意用我恋人来信中提到的那个名字试探他，“这是什么意思？我们为什么被这样囚禁起来？我要求立刻释放。为这番扣押，你将得向我们的领事交代。”

一瞬间，他似乎因我道出他的名字而愣住了。但他很快定下神来，背靠着墙，把我们俩仔细打量了一番，才开口回答。

“我乐意之至，”他讥诮地说，“不过恕我直言，眼下的解释嘛，咱们还不必多此一举。”

“请问，这话是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Exactly what I say; as you are likely to be our guests for some considerable time to come, there will be no need for explanation.”

“You mean to keep us prisoners, then, do you? Very well, Mr. Prendergast, be assured of this, when I do get loose I’ll make you feel the weight of my arm.”

“I think it’s very probable there will be a fight if ever we do meet,” he answered, coolly taking a cigarette from his pocket and lighting it. “And it’s my impression you’d be a man worth fighting, Mr. Hatteras.”

“If you think my father will let me remain here very long you’re much mistaken,” said Beckenham. “And as for the ransom you expect him to pay, I don’t somehow fancy you’ll get a halfpenny.”

At the mention of the word “ransom” I noticed that a new and queer expression came into our captor’s face. He did not reply, however, except to utter his usual irritating laugh. Having done so he went to the door and called something in Arabic. In answer a gigantic negro made his appearance, bearing in his hands a tray on which were set two basins of food and two large mugs of water. These were placed before us, and Prendergast bade us, if we were hungry, fall to.

“You must not imagine that we wish to starve you,” he said. “Food will be served to you twice a day. And if you want it, you can even be supplied with spirits and tobacco. Now, before I go, one word of advice: Don’t indulge in any idea of escape. Communication with the outside world is absolutely impossible, and you will find that those collars and chains will stand a good strain before they will give way. If you behave yourselves you will be well looked after; but if you attempt any larks you will be confined in different rooms, and there will be a radical change in our behaviour towards you.”
===Chinese===
“就是我说的这个意思；既然二位往后相当长一段时间里都要做我们的客人，解释就大可不必了。”

“这么说，你们是打算一直囚禁我们了？好啊，普伦德加斯特先生，你记住，等我一旦脱身，定叫你尝尝我这只手臂的分量。”

“我看，倘若真有那么一天我们交上手，十有八九是一场恶斗，”他答道，不动声色地从口袋里掏出一支香烟点上，“而且依我的印象，你是条值得一斗的汉子，哈特拉斯先生。”

“你们若以为我父亲会容我在这儿久留，那就大错特错了，”贝肯汉姆说，“至于你们巴望他支付的赎金，我看你们连半便士也捞不着。”

一听到“赎金”二字，我注意到掳我们的人脸上掠过一种新的、古怪的神情。不过他没有答话，只是照例发出那种惹人冒火的笑。笑罢，他走到门口，用阿拉伯语喊了一句。应声而来的是一个身材魁伟的黑人，双手捧着一只托盘，上面摆着两盆吃食和两大杯水。托盘放到我们面前，普伦德加斯特便招呼我们，若是饿了就动手吃。

“你们切莫以为我们想让你们挨饿，”他说，“吃食每天供应两回。若是想要，烈酒和烟草也可以供给。好了，临走之前奉劝一句：休要动逃跑的念头。与外面世界通信是绝对办不到的，而且你们会发现，这些领圈和链子不吃够大的力气是不会断的。你们要是安分守己，就会受到好好的照料；可要是想胡闹撒野，你们就会被分开关押，我们对你们的态度也将彻底改变。”


===Original===
So saying he left the room, taking the precaution to lock the door carefully behind him.

When we were once more alone, a long silence fell upon us. It would be idle for me to say that the generous behaviour of the young Marquis with regard to my share in this wretched business had set my mind at rest. But if it had not done that it had at least served to intensify another resolution. Come what might, I told myself, I would find a way of escape, and he should be returned to his father safe and sound, if it cost me my life to do it. But how were we to escape? We could not move from our places on account of the chains that secured us to the walls, and, though I put all my whole strength into it, I found I could not dislodge the staple a hundredth part of an inch from its holding-place.

The morning wore slowly on, midday came and went, the afternoon dragged its dismal length, and still there was no change in our position. Towards sundown the same gigantic negro entered the room again, bringing us our evening meal. When he left we were locked up for the night, with only the contemplation of our woes, and the companionship of the multitudes of mice that scampered about the floor, to enliven us.

The events of the next seven days are hardly worth chronicling, unless it is to state that every morning at daylight the same cock crew and the same dog barked, while at six o’clock the same cripple invariably made his way down the street behind me. At eight o’clock, almost to the minute, breakfast was served to us, and, just as punctually, the evening meal made its appearance as the sun was declining behind the opposite housetop. Not again did we see any sign of Mr. Prendergast, and though times out of number I tugged at my chain I was never a whit nearer loosening it than I had been on the first occasion. One after another plans of escape were proposed, discussed, and invariably rejected as impracticable. So another week passed and another, until we had been imprisoned in that loathsome place not less than twenty days. By the end of that time, as may be supposed, we were as desperate as men could well be. I must, however, admit here that anything like the patience and pluck of my companion under such trying circumstances I had never in my life met with before. Not once did he reproach me in the least degree for my share in the wretched business, but took everything just as it came, without unnecessary comment and certainly without complaint.

One fact had repeatedly struck me as significant, and that was the circumstance that every morning between six and half-past, as already narrated, the same cripple went down the street; and in connection with this, within the last few days of the time, a curious coincidence had revealed itself to me. From the tapping of his crutches on the stones I discovered that while one was shod with iron, the other was not. Now where and when had I noticed that peculiarity in a cripple before? That I had observed it somewhere I felt certain. For nearly half the day I turned this over and over in my mind, and then, in the middle of our evening meal, enlightenment came to me.

I remembered the man whose piteous tale had so much affected Beckenham on the day of our arrival, and the sound his crutches made upon the pavement as he left us. If my surmise proved correct, and we could only manage to communicate with him, here was a golden opportunity. But how were we to do this? We discussed it, and discussed it, times out of number, but in vain. That he must be stopped on his way down the street need not to be argued at all. In what way, however, could this be done? The window was out of the question, the door was not to be thought of; in that case the only communicating place would be the small pipe by my side. But as I have already pointed out, by reason of the elbow it would be clearly impossible to force a message through it. All day we devoted ourselves to attempts to solve what seemed a hopeless difficulty. Then like a flash another brilliant inspiration burst upon me.
===Chinese===
说罢他便出了屋，还小心地随手把门锁好，以防万一。

等再度只剩我们两人，一场长久的沉默笼罩下来。若说年轻的侯爵对我在这件倒霉事中的责任所表现出的宽宏大量已让我心安，那是假话。可它即便没能安我的心，至少也让另一个决心愈发坚定。我告诉自己，无论付出什么代价，我也要寻出一条脱身之路，哪怕赔上性命，也要让他安然无恙地回到他父亲身边。可是我们怎么逃呢？拴在墙上的锁链使我们寸步难离，而且尽管我使出全身力气，也休想把固定链子的卡钉从它咬合的地方撼出百分之一英寸。

上午缓缓挨过，正午来了又去，下午拖着它阴郁的长尾，我们的处境依旧毫无变化。日落时分，那个同样魁伟的黑人又进屋来，给我们送来晚饭。他走后，我们便被锁着过夜，陪伴我们的只有对自身苦楚的思量，和在地板上乱窜的成群老鼠。

此后七日的事情几乎不值一记，除非说一下：每天破晓，还是那只公鸡啼鸣、那条狗吠叫；到六点钟，那个瘸子必定准时从我背后的街上走过。八点钟，分秒不差，早餐送到；同样准时，当太阳落到对面屋顶后面时，晚饭也来了。我们再没见到普伦德加斯特先生的半点踪影；尽管我不计其数次地拽那锁链，却丝毫没能比头一回更松动它半分。逃跑的计划一个接一个地提出、讨论，又一律因行不通而被否决。就这样过了一周又一周，直到我们被囚在那可憎的地方足足不下二十天。到那时节，可想而知，我们已急得走投无路。不过我必须在此承认，我生平从未见过谁像我这位同伴这样，在如此煎熬的境地里还能有这般耐心和胆气。他一次也没有因这件祸事里我的责任而对我有半句责备，凡事来了便受着，不多说一句闲话，更绝无怨言。

有一个事实反复引起我的注意，觉得意味深长：如前所述，每天早晨六点到六点半之间，总有个同样的瘸子沿街走过；而与此相关，在被囚的最后几天里，一个古怪的巧合在我眼前豁然显现。从他双拐叩击石路的声音里，我发现一支拐包着铁头，另一支却没有。咦，我从前在什么时候、什么地方也留意过一个瘸子的这种特征来着？我确信在哪儿见过。这事在我心里翻来覆去折腾了将近半天，直到吃晚饭吃到一半，我豁然开朗了。

我想起了我们抵达那天那个以其凄惨身世深深打动贝肯汉姆的人，想起了他离去时双拐敲在人行道上的声响。倘若我的猜测不错，而我们又能设法与他取得联络，这便是天赐良机。可是怎么做到呢？我们讨论又讨论，不知讨论了多少回，全是枉然。必须趁他沿街走过时把他叫住，这自不待言。可究竟用什么法子呢？窗子无从谈起，门更不用想；如此一来，唯一可通的口子就只剩我手边那根小管子了。但正如我已指出的，因为那道弯头，要把信从里面硬捅过去分明不可能。整整一天，我们都扑在这个看似无望的难题上。忽然间，灵光一闪，又一个妙主意迸了出来。

===Original===
“By Jove, I have it!” I said, taking care to whisper lest anyone might be listening at the door. “We must manage by hook or crook to catch a mouse and let him carry our appeal for help to the outside world.”

“A magnificent idea! If we can catch one I do believe you’ve saved us!”

But to catch a mouse was easier said than done. Though the room was alive with them they were so nimble and so cunning, that, try how we would, we could not lay hold of one. But at length my efforts were rewarded, and after a little struggle I held my precious captive in my hand. By this time another idea had come to me. If we wanted to bring Nikola and his gang to justice, and to discover their reason for hatching this plot against us, it would not do to ask the public at large for help﻿—and I must own, in spite of our long imprisonment, I was weak enough to feel a curiosity as to their motive. No! It must be to the beggar who passed the house every morning that we must appeal.

“This letter concerns you more than me,” I said to my fellow prisoner. “Have you a lead pencil in your pocket?”

He had, and immediately threw it across to me. Then, taking a small piece of paper from my pocket, I set myself to compose the following in French and English, assisted by my companion:﻿—

> “If this should meet the eye of the individual to whom a young Englishman gave half a sovereign in charity three weeks ago, he is implored to assist one who assisted him, and who has been imprisoned ever since that day in the room with the blank wall facing the street and the boarded-up window on the right hand side. To do this he must obtain a small file and discover a way to convey it into the room by means of the small pipe leading through the blank wall into the street; perhaps if this could be dislodged it might be pushed in through the aperture thus made. On receipt of the file an English five-pound note will be conveyed to him in the same way as this letter, and another if secrecy is observed and those imprisoned in the house escape.”
===Chinese===
“老天为证，有了！”我说，特意压低嗓门，惟恐有人在门口偷听。“咱们得想方设法逮一只老鼠，让它把咱们的求援信捎到外面的世界去。”

“绝妙的主意！只要能逮住一只，我就相信你救了咱们！”

可是逮老鼠说来容易做来难。屋里老鼠多的是，可它们既灵巧又狡猾，任凭我们怎么折腾，一只也抓不着。但我的努力终得回报，一番小小的扑腾之后，我把这珍贵的俘虏攥在了手心。这时候我又生出一个念头。我们若想把尼古拉博士及其同党绳之以法，又要查明他们设此毒计坑害我们的缘由，那就不能把求援之手伸向不特定的大众——而且我必须承认，尽管被囚了这么久，我还是没出息地暗自好奇他们的动机何在。不成！咱们要求救的，必须是每天早晨从屋前经过的那个乞丐。

“这封信关乎你胜过关乎我，”我对我的难友说，“你口袋里有铅笔吗？”

他有，立刻把它掷了过来。于是我从口袋里掏出一小片纸，在同伴的协助下，用法文和英文拟就了下面这封信：——

> “若此信能入三周前一位英国青年曾施舍半索弗林善款的那个人的眼：恳请他援助一位曾援助过他的人——此人自那日起，便被囚于一间临街是光墙、右侧窗户钉着木板的屋子里。为此，他须弄到一把小锉刀，并设法经由那根穿过临街光墙通到街上的小管子把它送进屋内；倘若管子能被捅脱，或许便可从由此形成的孔洞塞入。收到锉刀后，一张英国五镑纸币将以此信相同的方式送到他手中；若能保守秘密、使屋中被囚之人得以逃脱，另有一张奉上。”

===Original===
This important epistle had hardly been concocted before the door was unlocked and our dusky servitor entered with the evening meal. He had long since abandoned his first habit of bringing us our food in separate receptacles, but conveyed it to us now in the saucepan in which it was cooked, dividing it thence into our basins. These latter, it may be interesting to state, had not been washed since our arrival.

All the time that our jailer was in the room I held my trembling prisoner in my hand, clinging to him as to the one thing which connected us with liberty. But the door had no sooner closed upon him than I had tilted out my food upon the floor and converted my basin into a trap.

It may be guessed how long that night seemed to us, and with what trembling eagerness we awaited the first signs of breaking day. Directly it was light I took off and unravelled one of my socks. The thread thus obtained I doubled, and having done this, secured one end of it to the note, which I had rolled into a small compass, attaching the other to my captive mouse’s hind leg. Then we set ourselves to wait for six o’clock. The hour came; and minute after minute went by before we heard in the distance the tapping of the crutches on the stones. Little by little the sound grew louder, and then fainter, and when I judged he was nearly at my back I stooped and thrust our curious messenger into the pipe. Then we sat down to await the result.

As the mouse, only too glad to escape, ran into the aperture, the thread, on which our very lives depended, swiftly followed, dragging its message after it. Minutes went by; half an hour; an hour; and then the remainder of the day; and still nothing came to tell us that our appeal had been successful.

That night I caught another mouse, wrote the letter again, and at six o’clock next morning once more dispatched it on its journey. Another day went by without reply. That night we caught another, and at six o’clock next morning sent it off; a third, and even a fourth, followed, but still without success. By this time the mice were almost impossible to catch, but our wits were sharpened by despair, and we managed to hit upon a method that eventually secured for us a plentiful supply. For the sixth time the letter was written and dispatched at the moment the footsteps were coming down the street. Once more the tiny animal crawled into the pipe, and once more the message disappeared upon its journey.
===Chinese===
这封要紧的信刚刚炮制完毕，门锁便一响，我们那位黑肤色的仆役端着晚饭进来了。他早已丢掉起初分器盛送的习惯，如今直接用煮饭的那口锅把吃食端来，再从锅里分到我们的盆子里。说来诸位也许有兴趣：那些盆子，自我们到达之日起就不曾洗过。

狱卒在屋里的一整段时间，我都把那只瑟瑟发抖的小俘虏攥在手心，紧紧抓住它，仿佛抓住我们与自由相连的唯一一线。可他前脚一出门，我便把吃食倒在地上，把盆子改制成一个陷阱。

可以想见那一夜对我们何其漫长，我们又怀着怎样战栗的急切等待破晓的头一丝迹象。天刚亮，我便脱下一只长袜拆开，把拆出的线合成双股，然后将一端系在那张卷得小小的纸条上，另一端拴在我那老鼠俘虏的后腿上。然后我们便坐等六点钟。时辰到了；一分钟又一分钟过去，我们才听见远处双拐叩石的嗒嗒声。那声音渐渐由远而近，又渐渐减弱，等我估摸他快到我背后时，便俯下身，把我们这位古怪的信使塞进了管子。然后我们坐下来静候结果。

那老鼠巴不得脱身，一头钻进孔里，那根系着我们性命的细线紧随其后，拖着信儿去了。几分钟过去了；半小时；一小时；接着又捱过一天的其余时光；始终没有任何动静告诉我们，求援已经成功。

当夜我又逮了一只老鼠，把信重写一遍，次日早晨六点再度打发它上路。又一天过去，音信全无。那夜我们又逮住一只，翌晨六点把它送走；第三只、甚至第四只接连上阵，却依然无功。到这时老鼠几乎逮不着了，但绝望磨利了我们的心智，我们到底想出一个法子，最终保证了充足的供应。信第六次写就，在那脚步声沿街而来的当口发出。小兽再一次钻进管子，信件再一次踏上旅途。

===Original===
Another day was spent in anxious waiting, but this time we were not destined to be disappointed. About eight o’clock that night, just as we were giving up hope, I detected a faint noise near my feet; it was for all the world as if someone were forcing a stick through a hole in a brick wall. I informed Beckenham of the fact in a whisper, and then put my head down to listen. Yes, there was the sound again. Oh, if only I had a match! But it was no use wishing for what was impossible, so I put my hand down to the pipe. It was moving! It turned in my hand, moved to and fro for a brief space and then disappeared from my grasp entirely; next moment it had left the room. A few seconds later something cold was thrust into my hand, and from its rough edge I knew it to be a file. I drew it out as if it were made of gold and thrust it into my pocket. A piece of string was attached to it, and the reason of this I was at first at some loss to account for. But a moment’s reflection told me that it was to assist in the fulfilment of our share of the bargain. So, taking a five-pound note from the secret pocket in which I carried my paper money, I tied the string to it, and it was instantly withdrawn.

A minute could not have elapsed before I was at work upon the staple of my collar, and in less than half an hour it was filed through and the iron was off my neck.

If I tried for a year I could not make you understand what a relief it was to me to stand upright. I stretched myself again and again, and then crossed the room on tiptoe in the dark to where the Marquis lay.

“You are free!” he whispered, clutching and shaking my hand. “Oh, thank God!”
===Chinese===
又是一天在焦灼的等待中度过，但这一次命运没有再让我们失望。当晚八点来钟，正当我们濒于放弃希望，我察觉脚边有一丝轻微的响动；那动静活像有人把一根木棍往砖墙的孔洞里硬捅。我低声把这事告诉贝肯汉姆，随即俯下头去细听。不错，又是那声音。唉，这时要有一根火柴多好！可指望不可能的事也是枉然，我只得把手伸向管子。它在动！它在我手中转动，来回晃了一小会儿，随即整个从我掌中消失；转眼间它已离开这间屋子。几秒钟后，一样冰凉的东西塞进我手里，凭那粗糙的边缘，我知道那是一把锉刀。我把它抽出来，仿佛它是黄金打就，一把揣进口袋。上面系着一根细绳，起初我颇有些不解其故。但转念一想便明白：这是为了帮我们兑现交易中我方的那一份。于是我从放纸币的暗袋里抽出一张五镑纸币，把它系上细绳，那头立刻便把它拽了出去。

不到一分钟，我便动手锉我领圈上的卡钉；不到半小时，它便被锉断，铁圈从我脖子上落了下来。

哪怕花上一年的工夫，我也无法让你体会重新站直身子对我来说是何等的解脱。我一遍又一遍地伸展四肢，然后在黑暗中蹑手蹑脚地穿过屋子，来到侯爵躺着的地方。

“你自由了！”他攥住我的手摇着，低声道，“哦，感谢上帝！”

===Original===
“Hush! Put down your head and let me get to work upon your collar before you say anything more.”

As I was able this time to get at my work standing up, it was not very long before Beckenham was as free as I was. He rose to his feet with a great sigh of relief, and we shook hands warmly in the dark.

“Now,” I said, leading him towards the door, “we will make our escape, and I pity the man who attempts to stop us.”
===Chinese===
“嘘！先低下头去，让我把你的领圈锉开，再说话不迟。”

这一回我是站着干活的，没过多久，贝肯汉姆便和我一样自由了。他长舒一口气，站起身来，我们在黑暗中热烈地握手。

“好了，”我一边把他领向门口，一边说，“咱们这就逃出去，谁想拦，谁就自求多福吧。”

## Part I / 第一部

===Original===
Part I
===Chinese===
Part I / 第一部

## Part II / 第二部

===Original===
Part II
===Chinese===
Part II / 第二部

## VII Port Said, and What Befell Us There / 第七章 塞得港及我们在那里的遭遇

===Original===
Fortunately for me my arrangements fitted in exactly, so that at one thirty p.m., on the seventh day after my fatal meeting with Dr. Nikola in the West of England express, I had crossed the continent, and stood looking out on the blue waters of Naples Bay. To my right was the hill of San Martino, behind me that of Capo di Monte, while in the distance, to the southward, rose the cloud-tipped summit of Vesuvius. The journey from London is generally considered, I believe, a long and wearisome one; it certainly proved so to me, for it must be remembered that my mind was impatient of every delay, while my bodily health was not as yet recovered from the severe strain that had been put upon it.
The first thing to be done on arrival at the terminus was to discover a quiet hotel; a place where I could rest and recoup during the heat of the day, and, what was perhaps more important, where I should run no risk of meeting with Dr. Nikola or his satellites. I had originally intended calling at the office of the steamship company in order to explain the reason of my not joining the boat in Plymouth, planning afterwards to cast about me, among the various hotels, for the Marquis of Beckenham and Mr. Baxter. But, on second thoughts, I saw the wisdom of abandoning both these courses. If you have followed the thread of my narrative, you will readily understand why.
Nor for the same reason did I feel inclined to board the steamer which I could see lying out in the harbour, until darkness had fallen. I ascertained, however, that she was due to sail at midnight, and that the mails were already being got aboard.
Almost exactly as eight o’clock was striking, I mounted the gangway, and strolled down the promenade deck to the first saloon entrance; then calling a steward to my assistance, I had my baggage conveyed to my cabin, where I set to work arranging my little knicknacks, and making myself comfortable for the five weeks’ voyage that lay before us. So far I had seen nothing of my friends, and, on making enquiries, I discovered that they had not yet come aboard. Indeed, they did not do so until the last boat had discharged its burden at the gangway. Then I met Lord Beckenham on the promenade deck, and unaffected was the young man’s delight at seeing me.
===Chinese===
亏得我的行期安排得恰到好处，在英格兰西部快车上同尼古拉博士（Dr. Nikola）那场要命的会面之后，第七天下午一点三十分，我已横穿大陆，站在那儿眺望那不勒斯湾（Naples Bay）的碧蓝海水了。我右边是圣马蒂诺山（San Martino），身后是卡波迪蒙特山（Capo di Monte），南面远处则耸起维苏威火山（Vesuvius）云雾罩顶的峰峦。我相信，从伦敦来的这趟旅程，人们一般都嫌它漫长劳顿；对我来说则更是如此，因为要知道，我心里对每一步耽搁都不耐烦，而身子也还没从那场沉重的折磨里恢复过来。
到了终点站，头一件事是找一家清静的旅馆——白天炎热时我能歇脚养神的地方，而且或许更要紧的是，一个绝无撞见尼古拉博士或他党羽之虞的地方。我本打算先去轮船公司办事处走一遭，说明我何以没在普利茅斯（Plymouth）上船，然后再到各家旅馆去寻贝肯汉姆侯爵和巴克斯特先生。但再一转念，我明白这两桩事都作罢才是上策。你若一直循着我这段叙述的脉络读下来，自然不难明白其中缘故。
也是为了同样的缘故，天黑之前，我不愿登上那艘我看得出泊在港外的轮船。不过我打听清楚了，她定于午夜启航，邮件已在装船。
差不多就在八点钟声敲响之际，我踏上舷梯，沿散步甲板信步走到头等餐厅入口；然后唤来一名侍者帮忙，把行李运进舱房。我随即在舱里动手摆置我的小零碎，为眼前这五个星期的航程安顿舒服。到这时我还没见到我那些同伴的影子；一打听，才知道他们尚未上船。的确，直到最后一班驳船把客货运到舷梯边，他们才露面。于是我在散步甲板上遇见了贝肯汉姆侯爵，这位年轻人见了我那股欢喜劲儿，全然不是装出来的。

===Original===
“Mr. Hatteras,” he cried, running forward to greet me with outstretched hand, “this was all that was wanting to make my happiness complete. I am glad to see you. I hope your cabin is near ours.”
“I’m on the port side just abaft the pantry,” I answered, shaking him by the hand. “But tell me about yourself. I expect you had a pleasant journey across the continent.”
“Delightful!” was his reply. “We stayed a day in Paris, and another in Rome, and since we have been here we have been rushing about seeing everything, like a regulation pair of British tourists.”
At this moment Mr. Baxter, who had been looking after the luggage, I suppose, made his appearance, and greeted me with more cordiality than I had expected him to show. To my intense surprise, however, he allowed no sign of astonishment to escape him at my having joined the boat after all. But a few minutes later, as we were approaching the companion steps, he said:﻿—
===Chinese===
“哈特拉斯先生，”他叫着，抢上前来伸出手迎我，“就差这一下，我的快乐便十全十美了。见到您真高兴。但愿您的舱房就在我们近旁。”
“我在左舷一侧，紧挨配膳室后面，”我握着他的手答道，“不过说说你自己吧。我想你们横穿大陆的一路总还愉快吧？”
“快活极了！”他答道，“我们在巴黎盘桓了一天，在罗马又盘桓一天，到这儿以后就一直四处奔走观光，活脱一对地道的英国游客。”
这时巴克斯特先生露面了——我猜他方才是在照管行李——他向我寒暄，那份热络出乎我的意料。然而令我大为诧异的是，我终究还是上了船，他竟没露半点惊讶的神色。可是几分钟后，我们走近升降口扶梯时，他说道——

===Original===
“I understood from his lordship, Mr. Hatteras, that you were to embark at Plymouth; was I mistaken, therefore, when I thought I saw you coming off with your luggage this evening?”
“No, you were not mistaken,” I answered, being able now to account for his lack of surprise. “I came across the continent like yourselves, and only joined the vessel a couple of hours ago.”
Here the Marquis chimed in, and diverted the conversation into another channel.
“Where is everybody?” he asked, when Mr. Baxter had left us and gone below. “There are a lot of names on the passenger list, and yet I see nobody about!”
===Chinese===
“哈特拉斯先生，我听大人说，您原定在普利茅斯上船；那么，今晚我恍惚瞧见您带着行李上船来，是我看错了不成？”
“不，你没有看错，”我答道——这下他何以不惊讶，我也能解释了。“我同你们一样是横穿大陆来的，不过两小时前才上船。”
这时侯爵插进话来，把谈话引上了另一条道。
“人都到哪儿去了？”等巴克斯特先生离开我们下舱之后，他问道。“旅客名单上一大串名字，可我四下里连个人影也瞧不见！”

===Original===
“They are all in bed,” I answered. “It is getting late, you see, and, if I am not mistaken, we shall be under way in a few minutes.”
“Then, I think, if you’ll excuse me for a few moments, I’ll go below to my cabin. I expect Mr. Baxter will be wondering where I am.”
When he had left me I turned to the bulwarks and stood looking across the water at the gleaming lights ashore. One by one the boats alongside pushed off, and from the sounds that came from for’ard, I gathered that the anchor was being got aboard. Five minutes later we had swung round to our course and were facing for the open sea. For the first mile or so my thoughts chased each other in rapid succession. You must remember that it was in Naples I had learnt that my darling loved me, and it was in Naples now that I was bidding goodbye to Europe and to all the strange events that had befallen me there. I leant upon the rail, looked at the fast receding country in our wake, at old Vesuvius, fire-capped, away to port, at the Great Bear swinging in the heavens to the nortard, and then thought of the Southern Cross which, before many weeks were passed, would be lifting its head above our bows to welcome me back to the sunny land and to the girl I loved so well. Somehow I felt glad that the trip to England was over, and that I was really on my way home at last.
===Chinese===
“全都睡了，”我答道，“你瞧，时候不早了，要是我没弄错，不出几分钟我们就要启航。”
“那么，请恕我失陪片刻，我该回舱房去了。我想巴克斯特先生要纳闷我上哪儿去了。”
他走后，我转身凭倚舷墙，隔水眺望岸上闪烁的灯火。船旁的驳船一艘接一艘撑开，从船头传来的声响里，我听出锚正被收上船来。五分钟后，船已掉转航向，船头朝外海驶去。起初一英里多的路程里，我的思绪纷至沓来，此起彼伏。你必定记得，正是在那不勒斯，我得知我的心上人爱着我；也正是在那不勒斯，我此刻正向欧洲、向我在此地遭遇的一切奇事作别。我倚着栏杆，望船尾后飞快退去的国土，望左舷远处顶着火冠的老维苏威火山，望北方天穹里缓缓运转的大熊座，又想起南十字座——再过不了几个星期，它便要在我们船头前抬起头来，迎我回到那片阳光灿烂的土地，回到我如此深爱着的姑娘身边。不知怎的，英国之行总算结束，我如今真的踏上归途，我心里只觉得欢喜。

===Original===
The steamer ploughed her almost silent course, and three-quarters of an hour later we were abreast of Capri. As I was looking at it, Lord Beckenham came down the deck and stood beside me. His first speech told me that he was still under the influence of his excitement; indeed, he spoke in rapturous terms of the enjoyment he expected to derive from his tour.
“But are you sure you will be a good sailor?” I asked.
“Oh, I have no fear of that,” he answered confidently. “As you know, I have been out in my boat in some pretty rough weather and never felt in the least ill, so I don’t think it is likely that I shall begin to be a bad sailor on a vessel the size of the *Saratoga*. By the way, when are we due to reach Port Said?”
“Next Thursday afternoon, I believe, if all goes well.”
===Chinese===
轮船几乎悄无声息地犁开航路，三刻钟后，我们已同卡普里岛（Capri）并舷而行。我正望着它，贝肯汉姆侯爵沿甲板走来，站到我身旁。他一开口我就听出，他还浸沉在兴奋里；的确，他眉飞色舞地历数这趟旅行预料中的种种乐处，语调狂喜不已。
“可你敢保自己经得起风浪、不晕船吗？”我问。
“噢，这我倒不怕，”他满有把握地答道，“您知道，我乘自己的小船出过海，遇过相当恶劣的天气，从没觉得半点不适，所以我想，在*「萨拉托加」号*（Saratoga）这样大小的船上，我不至于忽然晕起船来。对了，我们该什么时候到塞得港（Port Said）？”
“要是顺当，我想是下星期四下午。”

===Original===
“Will you let me go ashore with you if you go? I don’t want to bother you, but after all you have told me about the place, I should like to see it in your company.”
“I’ll take you with pleasure,” I answered, “provided Mr. Baxter gives his consent. I suppose we must regard him as skipper.”
“Oh, I don’t think we need fear his refusing. He is very good-natured, you know, and lets me have my own way a good deal.”
“Where is he now?”
===Chinese===
“要是您上岸，能带我一齐去吗？我并不想搅扰您，可您把那地方讲了那么多，我很想有您做伴亲眼看看。”
“带你去我是求之不得，”我答道，“只要巴克斯特先生肯点头。我想我们总得把他当作当家的才是。”
“噢，我想不必怕他不肯。您知道，他脾气好得很，多半都依着我。”
“他这会儿在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Down below, asleep. He has had a lot of running about today and thought he would turn in before we got under way. I think I had better be going now. Good night.”
“Good night,” I answered, and he left me again.
When I was alone I returned to my thoughts of Phyllis and the future, and as soon as my pipe was finished, went below to my bunk. My berth mate I had discovered earlier in the evening was a portly English merchant of the old school, who was visiting his agents in Australia; and, from the violence of his snores, I should judge had not much trouble on his mind. Fortunately mine was the lower bunk, and, when I had undressed, I turned into it to sleep like a top until roused by the bathroom steward at half-past seven next morning. After a good bathe I went back to my cabin and set to work to dress. My companion by this time was awake, but evidently not much inclined for conversation. His usual jovial face, it struck me, was not as rosy as when I had made his acquaintance the night before, and from certain signs I judged that his good spirits were more than half assumed.
All this time a smart sea was running, and, I must own, the *Saratoga* was rolling abominably.
===Chinese===
“在下面睡觉呢。他今天跑东跑西忙了一整天，想趁开船之前先躺下。我看我现在也该走了。晚安。”
“晚安，”我应了一声，他便又离开了我。
独自一人时，我又回到对菲莉斯和今后的遐想里；一斗烟抽完，便下舱上了床铺。当天傍晚早些时候我已弄清，与我同舱的是一位老派的胖胖的英国商人，此行是去澳大利亚巡视他的代理商；听他那鼾声之响，我断定他心里没什么大心事。所幸我睡的是下铺，脱了衣服躺下便睡得香甜，直到次日早晨七点半被浴室侍者唤醒。痛快洗了个澡，我回舱动手穿衣。这会儿同伴已经醒了，却显然不大想攀谈。我瞧着他那平日快活的红脸膛，比昨晚初识时差了几分血色，而且从种种迹象判断，他那份好兴致多半是硬装出来的。
这一路上风浪不小，我得承认，*「萨拉托加」号*摇晃得实在不像话。

===Original===
“A very good morning to you, my dear sir,” my cabin mate said, with an air of enjoyment his pallid face belied, as I entered the berth. “Pray how do you feel today?”
“In first-class form,” I replied, “and as hungry as a hunter.”
He laid himself back on his pillow with a remark that sounded very much like “Oh dear,” and thereafter I was suffered to shave and complete my toilet in silence. Having done so I put on my cap and went on deck.
===Chinese===
“祝您早安，亲爱的先生，”我进舱时，同舱的伙伴开口道，一副怡然自得的气派，他那张苍白的脸却拆穿了它。“请问您今天觉得怎样？”
“精神好得很，”我答道，“而且饿得像猎人一样。”
他仰回枕头上，咕哝了一句听来极像“唉哟”的话，此后便由得我闷声不响地刮脸、梳洗。收拾停当，我戴上帽子上了甲板。

===Original===
It was indeed a glorious morning; bright sunshine streamed upon the decks, the sea was a perfect blue, and so clear was the air that, miles distant though it was from us, the Italian coastline could be plainly discerned above the port bulwarks. By this time I had cross-examined the chief steward, and satisfied myself that Nikola was not aboard. His absence puzzled me considerably. Was it possible that I could have been mistaken in the whole affair, and that Baxter’s motives were honest after all? But in that case why had Nikola drugged me? And why had he warned me against sailing in the *Saratoga*? The better to think it out I set myself for a vigorous tramp round the hurricane deck, and was still revolving the matter in my mind, when, on turning the corner by the smoking-room entrance, I found myself face to face with Baxter himself. As soon as he saw me, he came smiling towards me, holding out his hand.
“Good morning, Mr. Hatteras,” he said briskly; “what a delightful morning it is, to be sure. You cannot tell how much I am enjoying it. The sea air seems to have made a new man of me already.”
“I am glad to hear it. And pray how is your charge?” I asked, more puzzled than ever by this display of affability.
“Not at all well, I am sorry to say.”
===Chinese===
这的确是个壮丽的早晨；灿烂的阳光泻在甲板上，海水蓝得纯粹，空气又那般澄澈，意大利的海岸线虽然离我们还有数英里之遥，从左舷舷墙上方仍能望得清清楚楚。这时我已盘问过事务长，确信尼古拉不在船上。他不在，叫我大惑不解。难道说这整桩事从头起就是我看岔了，巴克斯特的动机终究是清白的？可若是那样，尼古拉为什么用药麻翻我？又为什么警告我别乘*「萨拉托加」号*？为把这事想个透彻，我沿遮阳甲板快步兜起圈子来；心里还在翻来覆去地盘算，一转弯到了吸烟室入口，却同巴克斯特本人打了个照面。他一见我，便含笑迎上来，伸出了手。
“早安，哈特拉斯先生，”他脆快地说，“今儿早晨可真够美的。您猜不出我有多受用。这海上的空气，好像已经叫我脱胎换骨了。”
“听你这么说我很高兴。那么，您照管的那位少爷怎样了？”我问——他这一番亲热，更叫我摸不着头脑了。
“很遗憾，他一点儿也不舒服。”

===Original===
“Not well? You don’t surely mean to say that he is seasick?”
“I’m sorry to say I do. He was perfectly well until he got out of his bunk half an hour ago. Then a sudden, but violent, fit of nausea seized him, and drove him back to bed again.”
“I am very sorry to hear it, I hope he will be better soon. He would have been one of the last men I should have expected to be bowled over. Are you coming for a turn round?”
“I shall feel honoured,” he answered, and thereupon we set off, step for step, for a constitutional round the deck. By the time we had finished it was nine o’clock, and the saloon gong had sounded for breakfast.
===Chinese===
“不舒服？您总不会是说，他晕了船吧？”
“很遗憾，正是如此。半个钟头前他从床铺里起来时还是好好的。随后一阵突然而又猛烈的恶心袭来，把他赶回床上去了。”
“听您这么说真遗憾，望他快些好起来。要论谁最不容易病倒，他本该排在最末一批的。您来兜一圈吗？”
“那真是三生有幸，”他答道。于是我们步调一致地出发，沿甲板作保健散步。一圈走完已是九点，餐厅的锣响过，唤人用早餐了。

===Original===
The meal over, I repaired to the Marquis’s cabin, and having knocked, was bidden enter. I found my lord in bed, retching violently; his complexion was the colour of zinc, his hands were cold and clammy; and after every spasm his face streamed with perspiration.
“I am indeed sorry to see you like this,” I said, bending over him. “How do you feel now?”
“Very bad indeed!” he answered, with a groan. “I cannot understand it at all. Before I got out of bed this morning I felt as well as possible. Then Mr. Baxter was kind enough to bring me a cup of coffee, and within five minutes of drinking it, I was obliged to go back to bed feeling hopelessly sick and miserable.”
“Well, you must try and get round as soon as you can, and come on deck; there’s a splendid breeze blowing, and you’ll find that will clear the sickness out of you before you know where you are.”
===Chinese===
吃罢饭，我走到侯爵的舱房，敲了敲门，里面唤我进去。只见大人躺在床上，干呕得厉害；面色白得像锌，双手冰冷黏湿；每阵痉挛过后，满脸都是涔涔的汗。
“见你这副样子我真难过，”我俯下身去说，“现在觉得怎么样？”
“糟透了！”他哼哼着答道，“我简直莫名其妙。今天早晨下床之前，我还觉得再好没有了。后来巴克斯特先生好心给我端来一杯咖啡，喝下去不出五分钟，我就恶心难受得没了指望，只好又躺回床上。”
“唉，你得赶紧养好了到甲板上去；外头正刮着好风，等你回过神来，那恶心早给吹跑了。”

===Original===
But his only reply was another awful fit of sickness, that made as if it would tear his chest asunder. While he was under the influence of it, his tutor entered, and set about ministering to him with a care and fatherly tenderness that even deceived me. I can see things more plainly now, on looking back at them, than I could then, but I must own that Baxter’s behaviour towards the boy that morning was of a kind that would have hoodwinked the very Master of All Lies himself. I could easily understand now how this man had come to have such an influence over the kindly-natured Duke of Glenbarth, who, when all was said and done, could have had but small experience of men of Baxter’s type.
Seeing that, instead of helping, I was only in the way, I expressed a hope that the patient would soon be himself again, and returned to the deck.
Luncheon came, and still Lord Beckenham was unable to leave his berth. In the evening he was no better. The following morning he was, if anything, stronger; but towards midday, just as he was thinking of getting up, his nausea returned upon him, and he was obliged to postpone the attempt. On Wednesday there was no improvement, and, indeed, it was not until Thursday afternoon, when the low-lying coast of Port Said was showing above the sea-line, that he felt in any way fit to leave his bunk. In all my experience of seasickness I had never known a more extraordinary case.
===Chinese===
可他唯一的回答，是又一阵要命的呕吐，仿佛要把他的胸膛撕成两半。他正难受的当口，他的家庭教师走了进来，悉心看护，那份慈父般的温存连我也骗过了。如今事过境迁，我回望当时，比那时看得分明；但我必须承认，那天早晨巴克斯特对待这孩子的那副样子，只怕连“万谎之主”（the Master of All Lies，指魔鬼）本人也要被他蒙蔽过去。至此我很容易明白，此人何以能对好性子的格伦巴斯公爵有那么大的影响——说到底，公爵对巴克斯特这一流的人物，见识得实在太少。
眼见我非但帮不上忙，反倒碍手碍脚，我便说了句愿病人早日康复，回甲板去了。
午餐时分到了，贝肯汉姆侯爵还是起不了床。到了晚上，也不见好。第二天早晨，他总算好些了；可是临近中午，正想起身，恶心又卷土重来，只好作罢。星期三毫无起色；说真的，直到星期四下午，塞得港低平的海岸线浮出海面时，他才觉得勉强能够下床。就我见识过的晕船病例来说，从没见过比这更离奇的。

===Original===
It was almost dark before we dropped our anchor off the town, and as soon as we were at a standstill I went below to my friend’s cabin. He was sitting on the locker fully dressed.
“Port Said,” I announced. “Now, how do you feel about going ashore? Personally, I don’t think you had better try it.”
“Oh! but I want to go. I have been looking forward to it so much. I am much stronger than I was, believe me, and Mr. Baxter doesn’t think it could possibly hurt me.”
“If you don’t tire yourself too much,” that gentleman put in.
===Chinese===
我们在城外抛锚时，天差不多黑透了。船一停稳，我就下舱到我那位朋友的房里去。他衣裳穿得齐齐整整，坐在箱柜上。
“塞得港到了，”我通报道。“那么，你觉得上岸去怎么样？要依我说，你最好别去。”
“噢！可我要去。我一直巴望着这一天呢。我比先前强壮多了，相信我，巴克斯特先生也觉得这决计伤不着我。”
“只要别累着自己，”那位先生插言道。

===Original===
“Very well, then,” I said. “In that case I’m your man. There are plenty of boats alongside, so we’ll have no difficulty about getting there. Won’t you come, too, Mr. Baxter?”
“I think not, thank you,” he answered. “Port Said is not a place of which I am very fond, and as we shall not have much time here, I am anxious to utilize our stay in writing His Grace a letter detailing our progress so far.”
“In that case I think we had better be going,” I said, turning to his lordship.
===Chinese===
“那好，”我说，“这事就交给我了。船边驳船多的是，过去不难。您不一起来吗，巴克斯特先生？”
“我想就不去了，谢谢您，”他答道，“塞得港这地方我不大喜欢，何况我们在这里耽搁不了多久，我想趁这点工夫给公爵大人写封信，把我们到此为止的行程详细禀报一番。”
“既然如此，我看我们最好这就动身，”我转身对大人说。

===Original===
We made our way on deck, and, after a little chaffering, secured a boat, in which we were pulled ashore. Having arrived there, we were immediately beset by the usual crowd of beggars and donkey boys, but withstanding their importunities, we turned into the Rue de Commerce and made our way inland. To my companion the crowded streets, the diversity of nationalities and costume, and the strange variety of shops and wares, were matters of absorbing interest. This will be the better understood when it is remembered that, poor though Port Said is in orientalism, it was nevertheless the first Eastern port he had encountered. We had both a few purchases to make, and this business satisfactorily accomplished, we hired a guide and started off to see the sights.
Passing out of the Rue de Commerce, our attention was attracted by a lame young beggar who, leaning on his crutches, blocked our way while he recited his dismal catalogue of woes. Our guide bade him be off, and indeed I was not sorry to be rid of him, but I could see, by glancing at his face, that my companion had taken his case more seriously. In fact we had not proceeded more than twenty yards before he asked me to wait a moment for him, and taking to his heels ran back to the spot where we had left him. When he rejoined us I said:﻿—
“You don’t mean to say that you gave that rascal money?”
“Only half a sovereign,” he answered. “Perhaps you didn’t hear the pitiful story he told us? His father is dead, and now, if it were not for his begging, his mother and five young sisters would all be starving.” I asked our guide if he knew the man, and whether his tale were true.
===Chinese===
我们上了甲板，讲了一阵价钱，雇定一条小船，由人摇着送我们上岸。一到岸上，照例就有成群的乞丐和赶驴的少年围拢上来；我们顶住了他们的纠缠，拐进商业街（Rue de Commerce，法语），朝城内走去。在我这位同伴眼里，拥挤的街道、民族与服饰的五花八门、店铺与货物的奇异多样，都是引人入胜的物事。只要想到——塞得港虽有东方风味，却贫乏得很——这是他遇到的第一座东方口岸，便不难体会他这份兴致。我们两人都有几样东西要买；这桩买卖称心地办完后，便雇了一名向导，动身看风景去了。
走出商业街时，一个跛脚的年轻乞丐惹起了我们的注意；他拄着双拐，挡住去路，絮絮叨叨地数说他那套凄惨的苦情。向导喝他走开；说实话，我也巴不得甩脱他，可我瞟了一眼同伴的脸，看出他把这事当了真。果然，我们前行不到二十码，他就请我稍候片刻，拔腿跑回我们撇下那乞丐的地方去了。等他重新赶上我们，我说道——
“你总不会是说，你给了那无赖钱吧？”
“不过半枚索弗林（sovereign，一英镑金币）罢了，”他答道，“您大概没听见他讲的那段可怜身世吧？他父亲死了，如今若不是靠他讨钱，他母亲和五个年幼的妹妹全得挨饿。”我问向导认不认识那人，他讲的是不是实情。

===Original===
“No, monsieur,” he replied promptly, “it is all one big lie. His father is in the jail, and, if she had her rights, his mother would be there too.” Not another word was said on the subject, but I could see that the boy’s generous heart had been hurt. How little he guessed the effect that outburst of generosity was to have upon us later on.
At our guide’s suggestion, we passed from the commercial, through the European quarter, to a large mosque situated in Arab Town. It was a long walk, but we were promised that we should see something there that would amply compensate us for any trouble we might be put to to reach it. This turned out to be the case, but hardly in the fashion he had predicted.
The mosque was certainly a fine building, and at the time of our visit was thronged with worshippers. They knelt in two long lines, reaching from end to end, their feet were bare, and their heads turned towards the east. By our guide’s instructions we removed our boots at the entrance, but fortunately, seeing what was to transpire later, took the precaution of carrying them into the building with us. From the main hall we passed into a smaller one, where a number of Egyptian standards, relics of the war of ’82, were unrolled for our inspection. While we were examining them, our guide, who had for a moment left us, returned with a scared face to inform us that there were a number of English tourists in the mosque who had refused to take their boots off, and were evidently bent on making trouble. As he spoke the ominous hum of angry voices drifted in to us, increasing in volume as we listened. Our guide pricked up his ears and looked anxiously at the door.
“There will be trouble directly,” he said solemnly, “if those young men do not behave themselves. If messieurs will be guided by me, they will be going. I can show them a back way out.”
===Chinese===
“不，先生（monsieur，法语），”他应声答道，“那全是一整套天大的谎话。他父亲在坐牢，他母亲要是得了她应得的报应，这会儿也早在里面了。”这个话题再没人提起，可我看得出，这孩子的一片热心肠被刺伤了。他哪里想得到，这一番慷慨的发作，日后会给我们招来怎样的后果。
依着向导的主意，我们从商业区穿过欧洲人聚居区，来到阿拉伯城区（Arab Town）一座大清真寺。路走得不短，但他许诺说，那里有的东西瞧，任凭我们多费周折，也必叫我们补偿有余。这话后来果然应验，只是全然不是他预言的那种样子。
这座清真寺的确是座宏伟的建筑，我们到访时正值信徒济济一堂。他们跪成两列长排，从这头直贯到那头，人人赤着脚，头朝东方。照向导的嘱咐，我们在门口脱下靴子；所幸——鉴于后来发生的事——我们多了个心眼，把靴子随身带进了寺里。我们从正殿走进一间偏殿，那里为我们展开了多面埃及军旗，是 ’82 年战争的遗物。我们正端详间，片刻前离开我们的向导脸色发白地跑回来，说寺里来了一伙英国游客，不肯脱靴，看那样子是存心生事。他说话的当口，一阵不祥的怒嚷声隐隐传进我们耳朵，越听越响。向导竖起耳朵，惶惶不安地望着门口。
“马上就要出乱子了，”他郑重其事地说，“要是那几位少爷还不检点些的话。诸位先生（messieurs，法语）若肯听我一言，这就走吧，我能带各位从后头一条道出去。”

===Original===
For a moment I felt inclined to follow his advice, but Beckenham’s next speech decided me to stay.
“You will not go away and leave those stupid fellows to be killed?” he said, moving towards the door into the mosque proper. “However foolish they may have been, they are still our countrymen, and whatever happens we ought to stand by them.”
“If you think so, of course we will,” I answered, “but remember it may cost us our lives. You still want to stay? Very good, then, come along, but stick close to me.”
We left the small anteroom, in which we had been examining the flags, and passed back into the main hall. Here an extraordinary scene presented itself.
===Chinese===
一时间我颇想依从他的劝告，可贝肯汉姆接下来的一句话，使我决意留下。
“难道你要走开，任凭那几个蠢家伙让人家活活打死吗？”他说着，朝通向正殿的门走去。“不管他们多么愚蠢，到底是我们的同胞；无论出什么事，我们都该站在他们一边。”
“你既然这么想，我们当然留下，”我答道，“可记住，这也许要赔上性命。你还是想要留下？很好，那就来吧，紧紧跟着我。”
我们离开那间看旗帜的小偏殿，回到正殿。眼前呈现出一幅异乎寻常的景象。

===Original===
In the furthest corner, completely hemmed in by a crowd of furious Arabs, were three young Englishmen, whose faces plainly showed how well they understood the dangerous position into which their own impudence and folly had enticed them.
Elbowing our way through the crowd, we reached their side, and immediately called upon them to push their way towards the big doors; but before this manoeuvre could be executed, someone had given an order in Arabic, and we were all borne back against the wall.
“There is no help for it!” I cried to the biggest of the strangers. “We must fight our way out. Choose your men and come along.”
So saying, I gave the man nearest me one under the jaw to remember me by, which laid him on his back, and then, having room to use my arms, sent down another to keep him company. All this time my companions were not idle, and to my surprise I saw the young Marquis laying about him with a science that I had to own afterwards did credit to his education. Our assailants evidently did not expect to meet with this resistance, for they gave way and began to back towards the door. One or two of them drew knives, but the space was too cramped for them to do much harm with them.
===Chinese===
最里面的角落里，三个英国青年被一群狂怒的阿拉伯人围得水泄不通；他们的脸色明白地显示，自己何等清楚这危险的处境——那正是他们自己的无礼与愚蠢把他们诱进去的。
我们从人丛里挤过去，到了他们身边，立刻招呼他们朝大门的方向推开去；可这一手还没来得及施展，有人用阿拉伯语喝令了一声，我们便一股脑儿全被挤压回墙根。
“没法子了！”我朝那几个生人里最壮实的一个喊道，“我们只有打出一条路去。挑好你们的人，上吧。”
说着，我朝离我最近的那人下巴上击了一记，叫他牢牢记住我，一拳把他放翻在地；随后腾出了挥臂的余地，又撂倒一个去陪他。这工夫我的同伴们也没闲着；叫我吃惊的是，我看见年轻的侯爵抡开手脚四面出击，那套路分明有章有法——这一点我事后不能不承认，足为他所受的教育增光。袭击我们的人显然没料到会碰上这般抵抗，纷纷退让，开始朝门口退去。其中一两个拔出了刀，可地方太窄，施展不开，伤不着什么人。

===Original===
“One more rush,” I cried, “and we’ll turn them out.”
We made the rush, and next moment the doors were closed and barred on the last of them. This done, we paused to consider our position. True we had driven the enemy from the citadel, but then unless we could find a means of escape, we ourselves were equally prisoners in it. What was to be done?
Leaving three of our party to guard the doors, the remainder searched the adjoining rooms for a means of escape; but though we were unsuccessful in our attempt to find an exit, we did what was the next best thing to it, discovered our cowardly guide in a corner skulking in a curious sort of cupboard.
By the time we had proved to him that the enemy were really driven out, and that we had possession of the mosque, he recovered his wits a little, and managed, after hearing our promise to throw him to the mob outside unless he discovered a means of escape for us, to cudgel his brains and announce that he knew of one.
===Chinese===
“再冲一回，”我喊道，“就能把他们统统赶出去。”
我们冲了上去，转眼间大门便在那伙人最后一个身后砰然关上，落了闩。事毕，我们停下来掂量自己的处境。不错，敌人是被我们从城堡里赶出去了；可除非找得到脱身的法子，我们自己同样成了城堡里的囚徒。怎么办呢？
我们留下三个人把守大门，其余的人搜遍相邻的各间屋子，寻找出路；出路虽没找到，却办成了仅次于此的好事——我们发现那位胆小如鼠的向导，正缩在一个样子古怪的壁橱里。
等我们向他证实敌人确已被赶出去、清真寺已在我们手里，他才稍稍回过神来；又听我们撂下话——他若不给我们寻出一条生路，就把他扔给外面的人群——这才绞尽脑汁，宣布说他知道一条。

===Original===
No sooner did we hear this, than we resolved to profit by it. The mob outside was growing every moment more impatient, and from the clang of steel-shod rifle butts on the stone steps we came to the conclusion that the services of a force of soldiery had been called in. The situation was critical, and twice imperious demands were made upon us to open the door. But, as may be supposed, this we did not feel inclined to do.
“Now, for your way out,” I said, taking our trembling guide, whose face seemed to blanch whiter and whiter with every knock upon the door, by the shoulders, and giving him a preliminary shake. “Mind what you’re about, and remember, if you lead us into any trap, I’ll wring your miserable neck, as sure as you’re alive. Go ahead.”
Collecting our boots and shoes, which, throughout the tumult, had been lying scattered about upon the floor, we passed into the anteroom, and put them on. Then creeping softly out by another door, we reached a small courtyard in the rear, surrounded on all sides by high walls. Our way, so our guide informed us, lay over one of these. But how we were to surmount them was a puzzle, for the lowest scaling place was at least twelve feet high. However, the business had to be done, and, what was more to the point, done quickly.
Calling the strongest of the tourists, who were by this time all quite sober, to my side, I bade him stoop down as if he were playing leapfrog; then, mounting his back myself, I stood upright, and stretched my arms above my head. To my delight my fingers reached to within a few inches of the top of the wall.
===Chinese===
一听这话，我们当即决定照此办理。外面的人群一刻比一刻不耐烦，从钢皮枪托磕在石阶上的铿锵声里，我们断定已经调来了一队兵弹压。局势千钧一发，已两度传来声色俱厉的喝令，叫我们开门。可是，不难想见，这扇门我们无意去开。
“好了，你带的道儿在哪儿？”我说着，一把攥住我们那位哆哆嗦嗦的向导的肩膀——门上每添一声敲击，他的脸似乎就更白一分——先摇了他一下，煞煞他的威风。“放明白些，记住：你要是把我们引进什么圈套，我担保拧断你那根可怜的脖子。头前带路。”
我们收拢起混乱之中散落一地的靴子鞋袜，进了偏殿，穿戴齐全。然后从另一扇门里蹑手蹑脚地溜出去，到了后头一座小院，四面都是高墙。向导告诉我们，出路就在其中一堵墙上。可怎么翻过去却是个难题，因为最低的攀爬处也有十二英尺高。然而这事非办成不可，而且更要紧的是，得快。
我把那几位游客中最壮实的一个——这会儿他们全都酒醒了——唤到跟前，叫他弯下腰去，就像玩跳背游戏那样；然后我蹬上他的背，直起身子，双臂尽力向上伸。令我欢喜的是，指尖离墙顶只差几英寸了。

===Original===
“Stand as steady as you can,” I whispered, “for I’m going to jump.”
I did so, and clutched the edge. Now, if anybody thinks it is an easy thing to pull oneself to the top of the wall in that fashion, let him try it, and I fancy he’ll discover his mistake. I only know I found it a harder business than I had anticipated, so much harder that when I reached the top I was so completely exhausted as to be unable to do anything for more than a minute. Then I whispered to another man to climb upon the first man’s back, and stretch his hands up to mine. He did so, and I pulled him up beside me. The guide came next, then the other tourist, then Lord Beckenham. After which I took off and lowered my coat to the man who had stood for us all, and having done so, took a firm grip of the wall with my legs, and dragged him up as I had done the others.
It had been a longer business than I liked, and every moment, while we were about it, I expected to hear the cries of the mob inside the mosque, and to find them pouring into the yard to prevent our escape. The bolts on the door, however, must have possessed greater strength than we gave them credit for. At any rate, they did not give way.
When we were all safely on the wall, I asked the guide in which direction we should now proceed; he pointed to the adjoining roofs, and in Indian file, and with the stealthiness of cats, we accordingly crept across them.
===Chinese===
“尽量站稳，”我悄声说，“我要跳了。”
我纵身一跳，扣住了墙沿。倘若有人以为照这么个法子把自己拽上墙顶是件容易事，那让他自己去试一试，我料他准会发现自己的错处。我只知道这比我预想的艰难得多，艰难到等我爬上墙顶，已经筋疲力尽，足足一分多钟什么也做不成。随后我悄声叫另一人踩上头一个人的背，把手举上来够我的手。他照办了，我把他也拉上来，坐在我身旁。接着上来的是向导，再是另一位游客，然后是贝肯汉姆侯爵。最后，我脱下外衣放下去，给那个给我们大家当了人梯的人；放妥之后，我用双腿牢牢夹住墙头，像拉旁人一样把他也拽了上来。
这桩事耗时太久，出乎我的意愿；干着的每一刻，我都在提心吊胆，只怕听见寺里人群的呐喊，看见他们涌进院子来断我们的生路。然而门上的闩，想必比我们估量的结实得多。无论如何，它们没有断。
等大家全都平安上了墙，我问向导接下来该往哪个方向走；他指了指毗邻的屋顶，于是我们排成一列纵队，蹑手蹑脚地像猫一般爬了过去。

===Original===
The third house surmounted, we found ourselves overlooking a narrow alley, into which we first peered carefully, and, having discovered that no one was about, eventually dropped.
“Now,” said the guide, as soon as we were down, “we must run along here, and turn to the left.”
We did so, to find ourselves in a broader street, which eventually brought us out into the thoroughfare through which we had passed to reach the mosque.
Having got our bearings now, we headed for the harbour, or at least for that part of the town with which I was best acquainted, as fast as our legs would carry us. But, startling as they had been, we had not yet done with adventures for the night.
===Chinese===
翻过第三座房子，我们俯临一条窄巷；先仔细窥探一番，见四下无人，这才挨个儿跳了下去。
“好了，”我们一落地，向导便说，“先顺着这儿跑，再往左拐。”
我们照办了，来到一条宽些的街上，它最终把我们引上大街——正是我们来清真寺时穿过的那条。
辨明了方位，我们便撒开两腿，能跑多快跑多快，直奔港口——至少是奔城里我最熟悉的那片地界。然而，尽管今晚的奇遇已够惊心，这一夜的祸事却还没完。

===Original===
Once in the security of the gaslit streets, we said goodbye to the men who had got us into all the trouble, and having come to terms with our guide, packed him off and proceeded upon our way alone.
Five minutes later the streaming lights of an open doorway brought us to a standstill, and one glance told us we were looking into the Casino. The noise of the roulette tables greeted our ears, and as we had still plenty of time, and my companion was not tired, I thought it a good opportunity to show him another phase of the seamy side of life.
But before I say anything about that I must chronicle a curious circumstance. As we were entering the building, something made me look round. To my intense astonishment I saw, or believed I saw, Dr. Nikola standing in the street, regarding me. Bidding my companion remain where he was for a moment, I dashed out again and ran towards the place where I had seen the figure. But I was too late. If it were Dr. Nikola, he had vanished as suddenly as he had come. I hunted here, there, and everywhere, in doorways, under verandahs, and down lanes, but it was no use, not a trace of him could I discover. So abandoning my search, I returned to the Casino. Beckenham was waiting for me, and together we entered the building.
The room was packed, and consequently all the tables were crowded, but as we did not intend playing, this was a matter of small concern to us. We were more interested in the players than the game. And, indeed, the expressions on the faces around us were extraordinary. On some hope still was in the ascendant, on others a haggard despair seemed to have laid its grisly hand; on everyone was imprinted the lust of gain. The effect on the young man by my side was peculiar. He looked from face to face, as if he were observing the peculiarities of some strange animals. I watched him, and then I saw his expression suddenly change.
===Chinese===
一走进煤气灯照明的安稳街市，我们便向那几个惹出全部乱子的家伙道了别；又同向导讲定价钱，打发他走人，独自上路。
五分钟后，一扇敞开的门里流泻出的灯火叫我们收住了脚；一眼便认出来，我们正望进一家赌场（Casino）。轮盘赌台的喧闹声迎面而来；我们时间还宽裕，同伴也不觉疲倦，我想这倒是个好机会，让他见识见识人生阴暗面的又一副面孔。
但在讲这些之前，我得先记下一桩离奇的事。我们正要走进那座建筑时，不知什么东西叫我回头一望。我大为骇异地看见——或者自以为看见——尼古拉博士站在街上，正注视着我。我嘱同伴在原地稍候，转身冲了出去，朝那身影立处奔去。可我晚了一步。那若是尼古拉博士，他已像来时一样倏忽不见了。我四处搜寻，门洞里、游廊下、小巷深处，全都白费，连他一丝踪迹也没找见。于是只得罢手，回到赌场。贝肯汉姆正等着我，我们便一同走了进去。
屋里挤得水泄不通，各张赌台自然也都围满了人；好在我们不打算下注，这倒无关紧要。比起赌局来，我们对赌客更感兴趣。的确，四周一张张脸上的神情可谓千奇百怪：有的脸上希望仍占着上风，有的脸上憔悴的绝望似乎已扼下魔爪；而每一张脸上，都烙着贪财的欲念。这景象对我身旁这位年轻人的影响很是奇特。他从一张脸看到另一张脸，仿佛在观察某种奇怪动物的特性。我望着他，忽见他的神色陡然一变。

===Original===
Following the direction of his eyes, I observed a young man putting down his stake upon the board. His face was hidden from me, but by taking a step to the right I could command it. It was none other than the young cripple who had represented his parents to be in such poverty-stricken circumstances; the same young man whom Beckenham had assisted so generously only two hours before. As we looked, he staked his last coin, and that being lost, turned to leave the building. To do this, it was necessary that he should pass close by where we stood. Then his eyes met those of his benefactor, and with a look of what might almost have been shame upon his face, he slunk down the steps and from the building.
“Come, let us get out of this place,” cried my companion impatiently, “I believe I should go mad if I stayed here long.”
Thereupon we passed out into the street, and without further ado proceeded in the direction in which I imagined the *Saratoga* to lie. A youth of about eighteen chequered summers requested, in broken English, to be permitted the honour of piloting us, but feeling confident of being able to find my way I declined his services.
For fully a quarter of an hour we plodded on, until I began to wonder why the harbour did not heave in sight. It was a queer part of the town we found ourselves in; the houses were perceptibly meaner and the streets narrower. At last I felt bound to confess that I was out of my reckoning, and did not know where we were.
===Chinese===
顺着他的目光望去，我看见一个年轻人正把赌注押上台面。他的脸背着我们，可我朝右挪一步便能看清。那不是别人，正是那个把自己的父母说成一贫如洗的跛脚青年——两个钟头之前，贝肯汉姆才那样慷慨地接济过他。我们望着时，他押下了最后一枚钱币，输掉之后，便转身离场。这必须打我们站的地方近旁经过。他的眼睛撞上了恩人的眼睛，脸上带着一种几乎可算羞惭的神情，溜下台阶，出了那座建筑。
“走吧，我们离开这地方，”同伴不耐烦地喊道，“在这儿待久了，我看我要发疯的。”
于是我们出门上街，不再耽搁，径直朝我估摸*「萨拉托加」号*停泊的方向走去。一个年约十八、身着花格衣的少年，用结结巴巴的英语请求赏光，由他来给我们引路；但我自信认得路，便谢绝了他的好意。
我们闷头闷脑走了足足一刻钟，我开始纳闷，怎么港口老不见露面。我们发现自己身处城中一片古怪地界；房屋明显寒酸了，街道也窄了。末了我只得承认，我算错了方向，不知道自己身在何处。

===Original===
“What are we to do?” asked my lord, looking at his watch. “It’s twenty minutes to eleven, and I promised Mr. Baxter I would not be later than the hour.”
“What an idiot I was not to take that guide!”
The words were hardly out of my mouth before that personage appeared round the corner and came towards us. I hailed his coming with too much delight to notice the expression of malignant satisfaction on his face, and gave him the name of the vessel we desired to find. He appeared to understand, and the next moment we were marching off under his guidance in an exactly contrary direction.
We must have walked for at least ten minutes without speaking a word. The streets were still small and ill-favoured, but, as this was probably a shortcut to the harbour, such minor drawbacks were not worth considering.
From one small and dirty street we turned into another and broader one. By this time not a soul was to be seen, only a vagrant dog or two lying asleep in the road. In this portion of the town gas lamps were at a discount, consequently more than half the streets lay in deep shadow. Our guide walked ahead, we followed half-a-dozen paces or so behind him. I remember noticing a Greek cognomen upon a signboard, and recalling a similar name in Thursday Island, when something very much resembling a thin cord touched my nose and fell over my chin. Before I could put my hand up to it, it had begun to tighten round my throat. Just at the same moment I heard my companion utter a sharp cry, and after that I remember no more.
===Chinese===
“我们怎么办？”大人看了看表，问道，“差二十分十一点了，我答应过巴克斯特先生，不迟于十一点回船。”
“我真是个傻瓜，怎么没把那向导留下！”
话音未落，那位人物就从拐角转了出来，朝我们走来。我见他来了喜出望外，竟没留意他脸上那副恶毒得意的神情，便把我们想寻的那条船的名字告诉了他。他像是听明白了；转眼间，我们便已在他的引领下，朝一个截然相反的方向开步走去。
我们一言不发地走了至少十分钟。街道依旧又窄又破，可这多半是通往港口的近路，这点小小的不便也就不值一顾了。
从一条又窄又脏的街，我们拐进另一条宽些的街。这时已不见一个人影，只有一两只野狗躺在路当中睡觉。城的这一带煤气灯稀罕，多半条街都沉在浓影里。向导走在前头，我们跟在他身后约六步远。我记得瞥见一块招牌上写着个希腊文的店名，还想起点星期四岛上的一个类似名字——就在这时，一件极像细绳的东西碰上我的鼻子，垂过我的下巴。不等我抬手去碰，它已经收紧，勒住了我的咽喉。就在同一瞬间，我听见同伴发出一声尖叫，此后的事，我什么也不记得了。

## V The Islands and What We Found There / 第五章 群岛与我们的发现

===Original===
To those who have had no experience of the South Pacific the constantly recurring beauties of our voyage would have seemed like a foretaste of Heaven itself. From Sydney, until the Loyalty Group lay behind us, we had one long spell of exquisite weather. By night under the winking stars, and by day in the warm sunlight, our trim little craft ploughed her way across smooth seas, and our only occupation was to promenade or loaf about the decks and to speculate as to the result of the expedition upon which we had embarked.
Having sighted the Isle of Pines we turned our bows almost due north and headed for the New Hebrides. Every hour our impatience was growing greater. In less than two days, all being well, we should be at our destination, and twenty-four hours after that, if our fortune proved in the ascendant, we ought to be on our way back with Phyllis in our possession once more. And what this would mean to me I can only leave you to guess.
One morning, just as the faint outline of the coast of Aneityom was peering up over the horizon ahead, Wetherell and I chanced to be sitting in the bows. The sea was as smooth as glass, and the tinkling of the water round the little vessel’s nose as she turned it off in snowy lines from either bow, was the only sound to be heard. As usual the conversation, after wandering into other topics, came back to the subject nearest our hearts. This led us to make a few remarks about Nikola and his character. There was one thing I had always noticed when the man came under discussion, and that was the dread Wetherell had of him. My curiosity had been long excited as to its meaning, and, having an opportunity now, I could not help asking him for an explanation.

===Chinese===
对于那些从未见识过南太平洋的人，我们航程中层出不穷的美景，简直就像是天堂滋味的预尝。从悉尼起，直到把洛亚尔蒂群岛（Loyalty Group）甩在身后，我们一直享有整整一段绝美的天气。夜里在眨眼的繁星之下，白天在暖洋洋的阳光之中，我们那艘整洁的小船犁过平滑的海面，而我们唯一的营生，便是在甲板上溜达或闲荡，揣度我们此番远征会有个什么结果。
望见松树岛（Isle of Pines）之后，我们把船头几乎转往正北，朝新赫布里底群岛（New Hebrides）进发。我们的焦躁一个钟头比一个钟头更甚。倘若一切顺利，不出两天我们就该抵达目的地；再过二十四小时，如果时运高照，我们就该带着重新到手的菲莉斯踏上归程了。这对我意味着什么，我只有留给你去猜想。
一天早晨，阿内蒂乌姆（Aneityom）海岸模糊的轮廓恰好从前方的地平线上探出头来，我和韦瑟雷尔碰巧坐在船头。海面平滑如镜，小船从两舷撇出一道道雪白的水线，海水绕着船头叮咚作响，那是唯一听得见的声音。和往常一样，谈话兜过几个别的题目，又回到离我们心最近的那件事上，于是我们议论起尼古拉和他的为人。每当这个人被谈起，有一样东西是我一直注意到的，那就是韦瑟雷尔对他的恐惧。它的含义早已勾起我的好奇，如今有了机会，我不禁请他解释一番。

===Original===
“You want to know how it is that I am so frightened of Nikola?” he asked, knocking the ash off his cigar on the upturned fluke of the anchor alongside him. “Well, to give you my reason will necessitate my telling you a story. I don’t mind doing that at all, but what I am afraid of is that you may be inclined to doubt its probability. I must confess it is certainly more like the plot of a Wilkie Collins novel than a bit of sober reality. However, if you want to hear it you shall.”
“I should like to above all things,” I replied, making myself comfortable and taking another cigar from my pocket. “I have been longing to ask you about it for some time past, but could not quite screw up my courage.”
“Well, in the first place,” Mr. Wetherell said, “you must understand that before I became a Minister of the Crown, or indeed a Member of Parliament at all, I was a barrister with a fairly remunerative practice. That was before my wife’s death and when Phyllis was at school. Up to the time I am going to tell you about I had taken part in no very sensational case. But my opportunity for earning notoriety was, though I did not know it, near at hand. One day I was briefed to defend a man accused of the murder of a Chinaman aboard a Sydney vessel on a voyage from Shanghai. At first there seemed to be no doubt at all as to his guilt, but by a singular chance, with the details of which I will not bore you, I hit upon a scheme which got him off. I remember the man perfectly, and a queer fellow he was, half-witted, I thought, and at the time of the trial within an ace of dying of consumption. His gratitude was the more pathetic because he had not the wherewithal to pay me. However, he made it up to me in another way, and that’s where my real story commences.
“One wet night, a couple of months or so after the trial, I was sitting in my drawing-room listening to my wife’s music, when a servant entered to tell me that a woman wanted to see me. I went out into the passage to find waiting there a tall buxom lass of about five-and-twenty years of age. She was poorly dressed, but in a great state of excitement.

===Chinese===
「你想知道我怎么就这么怕尼古拉？」他问，一面把雪茄灰磕在身边翻起的锚爪上。「好吧，要说出缘由，就免不了给你讲一个故事。讲我倒完全无所谓，我只怕你会倾向于怀疑它是否可信。我必须承认，这实在更像威尔基·柯林斯（Wilkie Collins）一部小说的情节，而不像一段清醒的现实。不过，你既然想听，那就听吧。」
「那真是求之不得。」我答道，一边把身子坐舒服了，从口袋里又摸出一支雪茄。「这些时候我一直想问你这件事，只是鼓不起足够的勇气。」
「那么，头一件要说的，」韦瑟雷尔先生道，「你得明白，在我成为王室大臣之前——甚至说实在的，在我进议会之前——我是个出庭律师，执业收入相当可观。那是我妻子去世前、菲莉斯还在念书的时候。在我要讲的这件事之前，我从没接手过什么轰动的大案。可我博取声名的机会已经近在眼前，只是我自己并不知道。有一天，我受聘为一个人辩护，他被控在一艘从上海返航途中的悉尼船上谋杀了一名中国人。起初他的罪名似乎毫无疑义，但出于一个难得的机缘——细节我就不拿来烦你了——我想出了一条让他脱罪的计算。那人我记得清清楚楚，是个古怪的家伙，依我看脑子半痴半呆，受审的时候痨病沉重，离死只差一口气。他的感激因此更叫人心酸，因为他拿不出付我酬金的钱。不过，他换了另一种方式报答我——我的正戏，也就从这里开场。」
「开完庭大约两个月后的一个雨夜，我正坐在客厅里听妻子弹琴，仆人进来禀报说有个女人要见我。我出去到过道里，只见那儿等着一个高挑健壮的姑娘，年纪约莫二十五岁。她穿得寒酸，却激动得不得了。」

===Original===
“ ‘Are you Mr. Wetherell?’ she said; ‘the gentleman as defended China Pete in the trial the other day?’
“ ‘I am,’ I answered. ‘What can I do for you? I hope China Pete is not in trouble again?’
“ ‘He’s in a worse trouble this time, sir,’ said the woman. ‘He’s dyin’, and he sent me to fetch you to ’im before he goes.’
“ ‘But what does he want me for?’ I asked rather suspiciously.
“ ‘I’m sure I dunno,’ was the girl’s reply. ‘But he’s been callin’ for you all this blessed day: “Send for Mr. Wetherell! send for Mr. Wetherell!” So off I came, when I got back from work, to fetch you. If you’re comin’, sir, you’d best be quick, for he won’t last till mornin’.’

===Chinese===
「『你是韦瑟雷尔先生吧？』她说，『就是前几天开庭替中国皮特（China Pete）辩护的那位先生？』」
「『正是，』我答道，『有什么能为你效劳？但愿中国皮特没又惹上麻烦？』」
「『他这回的麻烦更大了，先生，』那女人说，『他快不行了，打发我在他咽气之前把你请去。』」
「『可他要我去做什么？』我颇为狐疑地问。」
「『那我可说不上，』姑娘答道，『可他这一整天都在念叨你：「快去请韦瑟雷尔先生！快去请韦瑟雷尔先生！」所以我一下工就赶来接你了。先生你要是去，最好快点，他撑不到天亮了。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Very well, I’ll come with you at once,’ I said, taking a mackintosh down from a peg as I spoke. Then, having told my wife not to sit up for me, I followed my strange messenger out of the house and down into the city.
“For nearly an hour we walked on and on, plunging deeper into the lower quarter of the town. All through the march my guide maintained a rigid silence, walking a few paces ahead, and only recognising the fact that I was following her by nodding in a certain direction whenever we arrived at cross thoroughfares or interlacing lanes.
“At last we arrived at the street she wanted. At the corner she came suddenly to a standstill, and putting her two first fingers into her mouth blew a shrill whistle, after the fashion of street boys. A moment later a shock-headed urchin about ten years old made his appearance from a dark alley and came towards us. The woman said something to him, which I did not catch, and then turning sharply to her left hand beckoned to me to follow her. This I did, but not without a feeling of wonderment as to what the upshot of it all would be.
“From the street itself we passed, by way of a villainous alley, into a large courtyard, where brooded a silence like that of death. Indeed, a more weird and desolate place I don’t remember ever to have met with. Not a soul was to be seen, and though it was surrounded by houses, only two feeble lights showed themselves. Towards one of these my guide made her way, stopping on the threshold. Upon a panel she rapped with her fingers, and as she did so a window on the first floor opened, and the same boy we had met in the street looked out.

===Chinese===
「『好吧，我这就跟你去，』我说着，从挂钩上取下一件雨衣。随后嘱咐妻子不必等我，便跟着这位奇怪的送信人出了门，一路走进城里。」
「我们走了将近一个钟头，越走越深，一头扎进了城里的下等街区。一路上向导始终缄口不言，只顾走在前面几步，每逢走到十字路口或纵横交错的小巷，才朝某个方向点点头，算是承认身后还跟着我。」
「终于到了她要找的那条街。在街角她突然站住，把两根食指伸进嘴里，学着街上顽童的样子吹了一声尖利的唿哨。片刻之后，一个乱蓬蓬脑袋的顽童从黑黢黢的小巷里钻出来，朝我们走来。女人对他说了句什么，我没听清，随即猛地朝左手一拐，招手让我跟上。我照办了，心里却不免纳闷，这一切究竟会有个什么下场。」
「我们从大街拐进一条肮脏不堪的小巷，来到一个大院子，那里笼罩着死一般的寂静。说实在的，比这更阴森荒凉的地方，我不记得还见过。一个人影也没有，院子四周虽被房屋围着，却只有两点微弱的灯火。向导朝其中一点走去，在门口停下。她用手指敲了敲门上的镶板，就在这时，二楼的一扇窗子打开了，探出头来的是我们方才在街上见过的那个男孩。」

===Original===
“ ‘How many?’ enquired the woman, who had brought me, in a loud whisper.
“ ‘None now,’ replied the boy; ‘but there’s been a power of Chinkies hereabouts all the evenin’, an’ ’arf an hour ago there was a gent in a cloak.’
“Without waiting to hear any more the woman entered the house and I followed close on her heels. The adventure was clearly coming to a head now.
“When the door had been closed behind us the boy appeared at the top of a flight of stairs with a lighted candle. We accordingly ascended to him, and having done so made our way towards a door at the end of the abominably dirty landing. At intervals I could hear the sound of coughing coming from a room at the end. My companion, however bade me stop, while she went herself into the room, shutting the door after her. I was left alone with the boy, who immediately took me under his protection, and for my undivided benefit performed a series of highly meritorious acrobatic performances upon the feeble banisters, to his own danger, but apparent satisfaction. Suddenly, just as he was about to commence what promised to be the most successful item in his repertoire, he paused, lay flat on his stomach upon the floor, and craned his head over the side, where once banisters had been, and gazed into the half dark well below. All was quiet as the grave. Then, without warning, an almond-eyed, pigtailed head appeared on the stairs and looked upwards. Before I could say anything to stop him, the youth had divested himself of his one slipper, taken it in his right hand, leaned over a bit further, and struck the ascending Celestial a severe blow on the mouth with the heel of it. There was the noise of a hasty descent and the banging of the street door a moment later, then all was still again, and the youngster turned to me.

===Chinese===
「『有几个？』领我来的女人扯着嗓子悄声问。」
「『这会儿一个没了，』男孩答道，『可一晚上这左近来了好一大帮中国佬，半个钟头前还有位披斗篷的老爷来着。』」
「那女人不等再听下去便进了屋，我紧随其后。这场奇遇显然就要见分晓了。」
「身后的门一关上，那男孩便举着一支点亮的蜡烛出现在楼梯顶上。我们便朝他走上去，随后又走向那脏得吓人的楼梯平台尽头的一扇房门。我不时听见尽头屋里传出一阵阵咳嗽声。同伴却叫我止步，自己进了房间，随手带上了门。我被留下同那男孩做伴，他立刻把我置于他的保护之下，专门表演给我一人看，在那摇摇欲坠的楼梯扶手上献演一连串堪称卓绝的杂技，全然不顾性命，自己倒似乎颇为得意。忽然，就在他正要开始他那套戏码里预兆最叫座的一手时，他忽然停住，肚子贴地趴在地板上，把脑袋从原先装过扶手的一侧探出去，朝下面半明半暗的楼梯井里张望。四下静得如同坟墓。接着，毫无征兆地，一颗杏眼、拖辫子的脑袋出现在楼梯上，朝上张望。我还没来得及出声阻拦，那小家伙已经脱下他仅有的一只拖鞋，抓在右手里，把身子又往外探了探，用鞋跟朝那位往上走的天朝人狠狠砸在嘴上。紧接着是一阵仓皇下楼的脚步声，片刻后街门砰的一声撞上，随后一切复归寂静，小家伙转向我。」

===Original===
“ ‘That was Ah Chong,’ he said confidentially. ‘He’s the sixth Chinkie I’ve landed that way since dark.’
“This important piece of information he closed with a double-jointed oath of remarkable atrocity, and, having done so, would have recommenced the performance of acrobatic feats had I not stopped him by asking the reason of his action. He looked at me with a grin, and said﻿—
“ ‘I dunno, but all I cares is that China Pete in there gives me a sprat (sixpence) for every Chinkie what I keeps out of the ’ouse. He’s a rum one is China Pete; an’ can’t he cough﻿—my word!’
“I was about to put another question when the door opened and the girl who had brought me to the house beckoned me into the room. I entered and she left me alone with the occupant.

===Chinese===
「『那是阿昌（Ah Chong），』他一副交底的口气说，『天黑以来，用这法子撂倒的，他是第六个中国佬了。』」
「通报完这桩重要情报，他还缀上一句花式百出、毒得吓人的咒骂，骂完又要重演他的杂技，被我拦住，问他何以如此。他咧嘴朝我一笑，说道——」
「『俺不知道，俺只知道里头那位中国皮特，俺每挡住一个中国佬不叫进屋，他就给俺一枚「小鱼儿」（六便士）。中国皮特是个怪人；他咳嗽那劲儿——老天爷！』」
「我正要再问一句，门开了，领我来的姑娘招手让我进屋。我走进去，她便丢下我，让我和屋里的人单独相对。」

===Original===
“Of all the filthy places I have ever seen﻿—and I have had the ill-luck to discover a good many in my time﻿—that one eclipsed them all. The room was at most ten feet long by seven wide, had a window at the far end, and the door, through which I had entered, opposite it. The bed-place was stretched between the door and the window, and was a horrible exhibition. On it, propped up by pillows and evidently in the last stage of collapse, was the man called China Pete, whom I had last seen walking out of the dock at the Supreme Court a couple of months before. When we were alone together he pointed to a box near the bed and signified that I should seat myself. I did so, at the same time taking occasion to express my sorrow at finding him in this lamentable condition. He made no reply to my civilities, but after a little pause found strength enough to whisper, ‘See if there’s anybody at the door.’ I went across, opened the door and looked into the passage, but save the boy, who was now sitting on the top step of the stairs at the other end, there was not a soul in sight. I told him this and having again closed the door, sat down on the box and waited for him to speak.
“ ‘You did me a good turn, Mr. Wetherell, over that trial,’ the invalid said at last, ‘and I couldn’t make it worth your while.’
“ ‘Oh, you mustn’t let that worry you,’ I answered soothingly. ‘You would have paid me if you had been able.’
“ ‘Perhaps I should, perhaps I shouldn’t, anyhow I didn’t, and I want to make it up to you now. Feel under my pillow and bring out what you find there.’

===Chinese===
「我平生见过的肮脏地方——我这辈子倒霉见过的着实不少——没有一处比得上那一间。屋子至多十英尺长、七英尺宽，远端开着一扇窗，我进来的那扇门正对着它。床架在门和窗之间，是一幅骇人的景象。床上，靠枕头半支着身子的，正是那个叫中国皮特的人，显然已到油尽灯枯的地步；我上一回见他，还是两个月前他从最高法院被告席上走出来的时候。屋里只剩我们两人时，他指了指床边的一只箱子，示意我坐。我坐下了，顺便说了一句，见他落到这般凄惨田地，我很是难过。对我的客套他没有应声，歇了片刻，才攒足力气低声说：『看看门口有没有人。』我走过去开门往过道里张望，除了那男孩——他此刻坐在另一头楼梯的顶上一级——一个人影也没有。我告诉了他，重新关上门，坐回箱子上，等他开口。」
「『那场官司，你帮了我大忙，韦瑟雷尔先生，』病人终于开口道，『我却没能报答你。』」
「『唉，这个你千万别放在心上，』我宽慰他道，『你若有能力，自然会付我钱的。』」
「『也许会，也许不会，反正我没有，如今我要补报你。伸手到我枕头底下，把摸到的东西拿出来。』」

===Original===
